# Full review of the Kotlin app (r051-int @ 20eeb178, version 0.5.5) - 2026-11-14

> Read-only review by 16 specialist lanes (Claude Fable 5.2). Nothing in the code was changed.
> Verdict column: confirmed / weakened = a second reviewer re-read the code and tried to refute it. unverified = the second reviewer did not run (usage limit) - re-check before acting.
> Lanes perf, release, tests, build are unverified. Lane "org" (code organisation, repo hygiene, docs) did not run.
> Build check the same day: jvmTest + assembleDebug succeeded but tests came from the Gradle cache (not re-executed); :androidApp:compileReleaseKotlin succeeded; :androidApp:lintDebug failed with 4 errors / 25 warnings (first: AppContainer.kt:191 java.time.ZoneId.of needs API 26, minSdk is 24).

## Owner to-do items (answers)

### perf-5 [Add a date-sorted view of transactions to the mirror] - Every in-memory read is a full collection scan plus sort; transactions have no date index and are sorted twice
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: later | effort: M | status: partially_built
- where: `native-app/firestore/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/firestore/DocQuery.kt:44`
- evidence: DocQuery.runOn (lines 44-52) filters docs.entries over the whole group map for every query and then sorts the hits with a comparator that looks the field up in the raw map and casts it on each comparison. FirestoreTransactionRepository.kt:21 then sorts the decoded list again by (occurredAt, sourceOrder). A one-month read therefore still touches all 3,000 documents; a full-history read sorts 3,000 rows twice. The LocalMirror header comment (LocalMirror.kt:17-20) records that the Home screen issues about 15 queries per load.
- impact: Cost per screen is proportional to total history, not to the visible month: at 3,000 transactions about 15 x 3,000 = 45,000 document checks per Home load plus the sorts; at 10,000 transactions (two more years, or a second bank) everything in perf-1..4 roughly triples. This is the reason the app gets slower every month.
- fix: Keep the transactions group additionally as a list pre-sorted by (occurredAt, sourceOrder) rebuilt once per mirror change (not per query), and answer date ranges with binary search; drop the second sort in the repository. Small change confined to LocalMirror/DocQuery, covered by the existing two-way parity test mentioned in DocQuery.kt:11.

### release-11 [Add a guidance-only disclaimer to inheritance results] - Inheritance calculator shows legal shares with no general 'guidance only' disclaimer
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/TextsAreaCalcInheritResult.kt:58`
- evidence: A grep of TextsAreaCalcInherit*.kt for guidance / lawyer / judge / official wording found only hints that item values are approximate (TextsAreaCalcInherit.kt:30, 43). The only court reference is INHRES_COURT_TITLE 'No text — ask the court' at line 58 and 114, shown for uncovered cases only. By contrast zakat carries 'For guidance, not a fatwa' (TextsZakat.kt:18, TextsAreaInvestmentZakat.kt:94). The calculator applies Saudi and Egyptian inheritance statutes (CODEMAP: InheritanceDistantSaudi.kt, InheritanceDistantEgypt.kt). A disclaimer worded differently or held in another text file would not have matched my grep.
- impact: A family may divide an estate on the app's numbers. Without a visible line saying this is an estimate and that the official division comes from the court, an error or an unsupported case becomes the owner's liability, and a store reviewer can read it as legal advice.
- fix: Add one fixed line on the result panel and on saved scenarios in all three text sets: guidance only, based on the named law, the binding division is the court's inheritance certificate. Mirror the zakat wording pattern.

### perf-7 [Add decidedKeys() and stop double-parsing parties in zoneNow] - Operation detail and Operations reload each re-scan the entire history for transfer parties
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: later | effort: S | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/usecase/ManageTransfers.kt:85`
- evidence: zoneNow() (lines 84-104) loads everything() = listByDateRange(EARLIEST, LATEST), calls transferPartyOf on every transaction, groups, then calls suspiciousTransferParties(all, ...) which (core/TransferParties.kt:226-241) calls transferPartyOf on every transaction a second time. It is invoked on every open of an operation (OperationDetailScreen.kt:109, only to learn which parties are decided), on every Operations load (OperationsScreen.kt:99, only for a count) and in TransfersScreen.kt:79. It does run on the work dispatcher (line 82). In OperationDetailScreen.kt:105-112 the other reads (edit.load, categories.list, merchants.listMerchants, legsIn) are on the UI thread.
- impact: At 3,000 transactions: one full scan, two sorts and 6,000 party parses per operation opened and per list reload. Off the UI thread, so it shows as the detail screen staying on its skeleton longer (estimate 30-100 ms) and as battery/CPU churn, not as a freeze. The decided-parties set needs only the transferParties collection (deps.parties.listAll()), not the transactions.
- fix: Add a light ManageTransfers.decidedKeys() that reads only the parties collection for the detail screen; compute the party once per transaction in zoneNow and pass the result to the suspicious-party step.

### countries-9 [Add max-age rule for FxRate] - Exchange rate has no staleness limit
- severity: low | verdict: confirmed | urgency: later | effort: S | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/usecase/LoadFutureInputs.kt:97`
- evidence: fx() builds FxRate(egpPerSar4(...), egp.source, minOf(sar.asOf, egp.asOf)) and returns it whatever its age. convertCountry (core/InvestFutureMath.kt:186-192) uses it with no date check. The date is only shown as text in ui/screens/investment/future/FutureCards.kt:137. This is latent today because USD_EGP is missing (countries-2).
- impact: Once the EGP rate is added: if the daily price job stops (the repo history shows it is a daily commit) or the phone is offline with a cached feed, the two-country total keeps using an old rate. Example: 500,000 EGP at a cached 13.00 instead of a real 14.30 shows 38,461 SAR instead of 34,965, an overstatement of about 3,500 SAR, presented as a normal number.
- fix: Treat a rate older than N days as unavailable or mark the total as approximate (the ApproxBadge component exists). Decide N with the owner.
- second reviewer: LoadFutureInputs.kt:97 returns the FxRate with asOf = min of the two rows and no age check, and InvestFutureMath.kt:175-192 (countriesTotal/convertCountry) only checks for null or non-positive rate. Latent today because fx() is always null without a USD_EGP row (countries-2), so it should be decided together with that fix; the max age is an owner decision.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: After how many days should an exchange rate count as too old?

### sync-2 [Add per-document tolerant decoding and a minimum-app-version gate before the first Play release.] - One document with an unknown or missing field makes every read of its collection throw (no schema version, no tolerant decode)
- severity: high | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/data/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/data/DocFields.kt:94`
- evidence: DocReader.str/long/bool throw DocumentError on a missing field (lines 22, 31, 39, 55) and wire() throws on an unknown stored enum value (lines 94-97). The single repository read path decodes the whole result with no per-document guard: DocQuery.kt:99 'query.runOn(local).map { it.entity(codec::decode) }' and FirestoreSpace.kt:98 'snapshot.documents.mapNotNull { it.rawData() }.map(::decode)'. A grep for schemaVersion/version in data and firestore commonMain found only the references-seed marker (FirestoreAccountAdapters.kt:101); stored documents carry no version field.
- impact: After the first store update that adds an enum value or a required field (a new economicKind, review state, asset kind), a user with two phones where one is not yet updated gets an exception on every transactions read on the old phone: home, lists and totals all fail, not just the one new row. The same happens with any document the frozen React app or a manual console edit writes in an unexpected shape.
- fix: Decide a forward-compatibility rule before the first public release: decode per document, quarantine the ones that fail (count them and show 'N rows need an app update' instead of throwing), and add a minimum-supported-app-version document under the account that an old build checks at open to ask for an update.
- second reviewer: DocFields.kt:22,31,39,55,94-97 throw DocumentError, and DocQuery.kt:99 / FirestoreSpace.kt:98 decode the whole result with no per-document guard; a grep of all main sources shows DocumentError is thrown but never caught, and there is no minimum-app-version or per-document schema version check. One correction to the title: extra unknown fields are tolerated (merge writes keep them, FirestoreSpace.kt:88-96); only a missing required field or an unknown enum value breaks the collection read.

### sec-firebase-4 [Add plausibility bounds and max daily change to price feed] - Price feed from GitHub is applied with no plausibility bounds
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/Assets.kt:235`
- evidence: Assets.kt:235-240 feedAmount accepts any positive integer; parsePriceFeed (246-270) checks only shape, date validity, source text and currency code. native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/usecase/SyncAssetPrices.kt:59 saves that price on every linked asset. The file comes from raw.githubusercontent.com/.../main/public/ (LoadOnlineFeeds.kt:31) and is produced by a bot that pushes straight to main (.github/workflows/prices.yml:70) from third-party APIs (scripts/fetch-prices.mjs:81-210). The averages parser, by contrast, does bound rates (Averages.kt MIN/MAX_ANNUAL_RATE_BP).
- impact: A wrong upstream value, a bug in the fetch script, or anyone who can push to main can set gold/stock prices to any number; every user's asset value, net worth and zakat base would silently be wrong (bank balances and transactions are not affected). asOf is not checked against today either, so a stale or future-dated price is accepted.
- fix: In parsePriceFeed (or SyncAssetPrices) reject a line outside a per-symbol plausible range and one that moves more than a set percentage from the last stored price; keep the old price and show the reason. Reject asOf in the future. Add the same check in the fetch script so a bad value never gets committed.
- second reviewer: core/Assets.kt:235-240 accepts any positive integer and parsePriceFeed (246-270) checks only shape, date validity, source and currency. SyncAssetPrices.kt:59 saves the price with no comparison to the previous one and no asOf-versus-today check. The only guards are a currency mismatch skip (SyncAssetPrices.kt:55) and FEED_MAX_AGE_MILLIS (LoadOnlineFeeds.kt:33), which is cache age, not price age; the averages parser does bound its rates (Averages.kt:72).

### perf-9 [Add read-count budget tests for Home, Operations, People, Detail loaders] - No speed budget is enforced for any main screen; the trace is emulator-only
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: partially_built
- where: `native-app/androidApp/src/main/kotlin/app/masroufy/android/MasroufyApplication.kt:27`
- evidence: PerfTrace.sink is only set when BuildConfig.FIREBASE_EMULATOR_PROJECT is non-empty (lines 27-31), so the owner's build records no timings. Tests that assert time or work bounds, found by name: app/src/jvmTest/.../SmsConfirmScaleTest.kt (line 127: confirm under BUDGET_MS = 30,000 ms, plus per-page count bounds at 107-111), BnplSpeedTest.kt:66 (under 700 ms), SmsHistoryBenchTest.kt:79 (range reads per page). No test by name covers Home, Operations, People or operation detail at scale, and the spans exist (screen:home, screen:operations, ops:zone, screen:people, screen:detail) but nothing asserts on them.
- impact: The UI-thread regressions in perf-1..4 cannot be caught automatically: a new card or a new read on Home silently adds to the freeze, and the owner is the first to notice. The 30-second SMS budget is also loose enough to hide a 10x regression.
- fix: Add one JVM test per main screen loader with a 3,000-transaction / 5,000-source-record fixture that counts repository calls through a counting wrapper (e.g. Home: at most 1 full-history read and 1 period read) rather than wall time; counts are stable on CI. Optionally keep a ring buffer of the last spans in the owner build's problem log so slow screens show up in his report.

### sync-7 [Add retry with backoff to FirestoreSync listeners.] - A failed listener is never restarted; reads for that group fall back to full server queries until the process restarts
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/firestore/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/firestore/FirestoreSync.kt:94`
- evidence: On any listener exception the catch block calls mirror.markStale(group), emits to failures and reports a problem (lines 94-99); the launched job then ends and there is no retry. LocalMirror.markStale adds the group to 'failed' (LocalMirror.kt:71-74), after which docsOf/awaitDocs return null (lines 82, 93) and every select() runs query.get() against Firestore (DocQuery.kt:100-103).
- impact: One transient listener error (permission blip during token refresh, quota error) leaves that collection permanently on the slow path for the life of the process: every screen read is a billed server query (a full-history read of transactions is 3,000 reads each time), remote changes for that group stop arriving, and offline reads go to the SDK cache path that was measured as too slow. With the quota problem in sync-1 this amplifies itself: quota errors kill listeners, which causes more billed queries.
- fix: Restart the listener with backoff after a failure (and clear the 'failed' flag once a server snapshot arrives); show a small 'sync paused' status while it is down.
- second reviewer: FirestoreSync.kt:94-99 marks the group stale, emits and reports, then the job ends with no retry; nothing in main sources collects FirestoreSync.errors or restarts a listener, and LocalMirror.kt:71-74,82,93 keeps the group in 'failed' until clear(), so DocQuery.kt:100-103 runs server queries for it. Two corrections: the SDK retries ordinary network drops itself, so the trigger is limited to terminal errors (permission denied, resource exhausted), and the fallback queries carry their filters, so not every read is a full 3,000-document read; the state also resets on sign-out, not only on process restart.

### tests-2 [Add ubuntu CI job for ui/wiring/firestore jvmTest and an Android emulator-variant build] - CI never runs ui, wiring or firestore tests and never builds the Android app
- severity: high | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: not_built
- where: `.github/workflows/native-core.yml:30`
- evidence: Line 30 runs only `:core:jvmTest :app:jvmTest :data:jvmTest :device:jvmTest`; line 37 the iOS simulator tests of core/data/device; line 64 iOS compiles of core/app/data/firestore/device. No `:ui:jvmTest` (563 tests), `:wiring:jvmTest` (79), `:firestore:jvmTest` (21), no `:androidApp:assemble*`, no ui/wiring compile at all. Trigger is `push` on any branch touching native-app/** (lines 6-11), no pull_request trigger.
- impact: About 660 tests and the whole presentation + composition layer (64k lines) plus the shipped Android binary are verified only when a session remembers to run the long local command on the owner's PC. A broken screen or wiring graph can be pushed with a green (once fixed) CI.
- fix: Add a cheap ubuntu job: `./gradlew :ui:jvmTest :wiring:jvmTest :firestore:jvmTest :ui:compileKotlinJvm` (no secrets needed). If google-services.json blocks assembling androidApp, build the `emulator` variant that targets the demo project.

### release-4 [Add usage-data notice and switch before outside testers] - Usage analytics run with no notice and no switch; crash reports default on and carry the account id
- severity: high | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: by_design
- where: `native-app/androidApp/src/main/kotlin/app/masroufy/android/FirebaseUsage.kt:55`
- evidence: FirebaseUsage.kt:14 documents 'running with no switch and no explanatory line (owner decision)' and line 55 calls setAnalyticsCollectionEnabled(true) for every owner build; line 41 sets ANALYTICS_STORAGE to GRANTED in code. CrashReports.kt:30 defaults the crash switch to true and line 64 sets the Firebase uid as the Crashlytics user id. build.gradle.kts:35-37 turns both on for debug and fast, the builds testers receive. This is OVERRIDES section 114 item 4 and section 90. Ad id and ad personalisation are correctly off (AndroidManifest.xml:7-9, 33-37).
- impact: The owner decision was taken for a two-person family test. Once other people install it, the app collects usage data and account-linked crash data with no notice and no opt-out. Saudi PDPL and Egypt's data protection law both expect at least notice and a lawful basis; Play's Data safety form must declare both streams (OVERRIDES:2520 already notes this). I did not verify the exact legal wording of either law.
- fix: Keep the minimal event design, but add one line in onboarding and Settings ('we send anonymous usage counts and crash reports, no amounts or names') with a switch for usage like the existing crash switch, and cover both in the privacy policy. Consider not attaching the uid to crash reports for non-owner accounts.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: Section 114 item 4 was decided for the family test: does it still stand for outside testers and the store, or do we add a notice line and a switch?

### perf-6 [Batch the mirror update in saveAll] - Mirror copies the whole collection map once per written document (batch of N on M documents costs N x M)
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/firestore/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/firestore/LocalMirror.kt:113`
- evidence: LocalMirror.applySet (line 112-114) does `current + (id to Entry(...))`, which builds a new map of the whole group. FirestoreSpace.saveAll (FirestoreSpace.kt:101-108) calls it once per item: `forms.forEach { (id, form) -> m.applySet(codec.group, id, form) }` for chunks of up to 500. applyUpdate (line 117-119) and applyReplace do the same.
- impact: Recording a 500-item batch into a 3,000-document collection copies about 1.5 million map entries (and the same again for each other collection written with it, e.g. source records). Reading 5,000 old SMS into an account that ends at several thousand transactions is on the order of 10-20 million entry copies per collection plus heavy garbage collection: estimate several extra seconds over a full history read, and GC pauses that also stutter the UI thread while it runs.
- fix: Add applySetAll(group, List<Pair<id, fields>>) that copies the map once per batch (as applyChanges at line 101-109 already does) and use it from saveAll.

### build-3 [Bring the build tools up to a combination that officially supports the Kotlin version, in one planned round.] - Kotlin 2.4.20 is ahead of the R8 in AGP 8.13.0 and of Gradle 8.14.3: thousands of warnings on every minified build
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/build.gradle.kts:2`
- evidence: Root build.gradle.kts lines 2-11 pin Kotlin 2.4.20, AGP 8.13.0, Compose Multiplatform 1.11.1; gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.properties line 3 pins Gradle 8.14.3. Across the existing native-app/build-*.log files: 5230 lines 'WARNING: R8: An error occurred when parsing kotlin metadata. This normally happens when using a newer version of kotlin than the kotlin version released when this version of R8 was created' (the last 40 lines of build-g.log and build-r051.log are almost entirely this), and 17 times 'The used Gradle version (Gradle 8.14.3) is deprecated and will not be supported in future Kotlin Gradle Plugin releases'. ui/build.gradle.kts line 23 notes Compose 1.12 needs compileSdk 37 and AGP 9.1.
- impact: R8 shrinks and optimizes the only variant people receive without being able to read Kotlin metadata. With -dontobfuscate and the broad keeps it works today, but anything relying on Kotlin metadata after shrinking (kotlinx.serialization lookups inside GitLive, reflection) is running outside the tested combination, and real R8 warnings are buried under thousands of identical lines. The next Kotlin plugin bump will refuse Gradle 8.14.3, and moving to AGP 9 changes how Kotlin Multiplatform modules apply the Android plugin (verify: com.android.library together with kotlin multiplatform is replaced by the Android KMP library plugin), which touches ui, wiring, device and firestore at once.
- fix: Short term: pin a newer R8 that understands Kotlin 2.4 metadata via the buildscript classpath (com.android.tools:r8) or move to the newest AGP 8.x/9.x that lists Kotlin 2.4 as supported, then confirm the warning count is zero. Plan one dedicated upgrade round (Gradle 9.x + AGP 9.x + Android KMP library plugin) instead of letting it be forced by a dependency bump. Verify exact compatible versions against current documentation.

### build-1 [Build a signed store variant (AAB) with keystore values read from outside the repo, and register its fingerprints in Firebase.] - No store-ready variant: release is unminified and unsigned, the shipped 'fast' variant is debug-signed
- severity: high | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: not_built
- where: `native-app/androidApp/build.gradle.kts:50`
- evidence: Lines 50-52: release has only isMinifyEnabled = false and no signingConfig. Lines 58-67: 'fast' does initWith(release), minify + shrinkResources, signingConfig = signingConfigs.getByName("debug"), versionNameSuffix = "-fast". There is no signingConfigs block anywhere in the file, no keystore path/password plumbing (env or properties), and no bundle (AAB) configuration. versionName is "kotlin-0.5.5" (line 31). OVERRIDES.md §118.2 (line 2681) records the owner decision to stay on the trial key until just before the store, then switch once with uninstall/reinstall.
- impact: Google Play rejects debug-signed uploads, so nothing the build produces today can be uploaded. The switch to a real upload key also changes the SHA-1 used for Google sign-in (and Play App Signing adds a third fingerprint), so the first store build will fail sign-in unless the new fingerprints are registered in Firebase first. The step is decided but not prepared, so it will be done under time pressure.
- fix: Add a real 'release' (or 'store') variant: minify + shrinkResources with the same proguard file, signingConfig reading keystore path/alias/passwords from environment variables or a gitignored properties file under secrets/ (fail the build with a clear message when missing), produce an AAB via bundleRelease, drop the '-fast' suffix and 'kotlin-' prefix for the store versionName. Register upload-key and Play-signing SHA-1/SHA-256 in Firebase before the first store build. Keep 'fast' as is for sideload until the §118.2 switch.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: OVERRIDES §118.2 already decided the timing; open point is where the real keystore and its passwords will live and who keeps the backup.

### sec-firebase-1 [Cap and harden shared merchant sync (limit query, no new merchants from unconfirmed entries, tighten update rule)] - Shared merchant catalog: any signed-in account can flood or overwrite it, and every client ingests it without limits
- severity: high | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `firestore.rules:14`
- evidence: firestore.rules:14-17 allows create by any signed-in user and update of any entry whose confirmed == false (no owner field, no rate limit, no per-user cap). Client side: native-app/firestore/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/firestore/FirestoreAccountAdapters.kt:149-155 listChangedSince() runs base.orderBy("updatedAt").get() with no limit (first sync reads the whole collection). native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/usecase/SharedMerchants.kt:104-106 turns every unconfirmed entry into a write in the user's own merchants collection (planSharedSuggestions adds a new Merchant per entry, core/SharedMerchants.kt:147-153). native-app/androidApp/src/main/kotlin/app/masroufy/android/AppContainer.kt:155 runs this sync automatically at every session start. native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/Categorize.kt:138-141 applies the unconfirmed sharedCategoryId as a SUGGESTED category on transactions.
- impact: One self-registered account (sign-up is open, config is public) can write tens of thousands of junk entries: every phone then reads them all (Spark limit 50,000 reads/day is shared by all users, so Firestore stops for everyone that day), and each account gets the junk copied in as merchants with attacker-chosen display names (up to 80 chars of any text) and wrong category suggestions. The attacker can also flip the category of any real unconfirmed merchant, since update needs no ownership.
- fix: Client: add a hard limit to listChangedSince (page of a few hundred, advance cursor), cap adds per sync, and do not create new account merchants from unconfirmed entries (only fill a suggestion on merchants the account already has). Rules: forbid changing displayName/aliases on update of an existing entry, or move contributions to sharedMerchants/{key}/votes/{uid} so one account = one vote. Enforce App Check on Firestore before wider release.
- second reviewer: firestore.rules:14-17 has no owner field or cap, FirestoreAccountAdapters.kt:149-152 runs orderBy("updatedAt").get() with no limit, usecase/SharedMerchants.kt:104-106 saves every hint, core/SharedMerchants.kt:147-153 creates a new Merchant per unconfirmed entry, and AppContainer.kt:155 runs it at session start. Mitigations that exist but do not stop the flood: entries whose category is not in the tree are skipped for writing (core/SharedMerchants.kt:140) though still read, and a suggestion never overrides the account's own category (Categorize.kt:137-143). Sending unconfirmed entries as suggestions is an owner decision of 2026-10-22 (SharedMerchants.kt:53), so the 'no new merchants from unconfirmed' part of the fix needs the owner; the query limit and per-sync cap do not.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: The 2026-10-22 owner reply ("علطول كاقتراح") made unconfirmed contributions reach every account immediately; that decision is what turns catalog poisoning into something all users see. Needs an owner choice: keep instant suggestions (with caps) or only confirmed entries.

### build-10 [Check the old libraries for known problems and newer versions.] - Old or security-relevant Android dependencies to verify
- severity: low | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/device/build.gradle.kts:56`
- evidence: Line 56: com.tom-roush:pdfbox-android:2.0.27.0 parses PDF bank statements the user picks from storage. Line 60: androidx.biometric:biometric:1.1.0 (stable from 2021) is the app lock. Line 64: androidx.core:core:1.12.0 declared explicitly (2023; a newer one is probably resolved transitively through activity-compose 1.11.0). proguard-rules.pro lines 31-37 silence missing optional classes for pdfbox (bouncycastle, jp2, awt).
- impact: VERIFY (my knowledge has a cutoff): the PDF parser handles untrusted files and is based on an old PDFBox 2.0.x line; check current advisories and whether a newer pdfbox-android exists. Encrypted PDFs will fail at runtime because bouncycastle is deliberately absent. Biometric 1.1.0 predates several Android releases; check behaviour of device-credential fallback on Android 14-16.
- fix: Run a dependency update/advisory check (for example the Gradle versions plugin or GitHub Dependabot, which is free on a public repo) and decide per library; add a test PDF that is password-protected to confirm the error is shown cleanly.

### arch-5 [Commit deferred SMS picks through a container-scoped use case that reports failures] - Deferred bank-message picks are committed from a detached scope that drops failures
- severity: low (finder said medium) | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/screens/imports/BankSmsR05.kt:84`
- evidence: flushDetached (BankSmsR05.kt:79-87) clears the pending items, then starts CoroutineScope(SupervisorJob() + Dispatchers.Default).launch { runCatching { commit(...) } } and ignores the result: no log, no Problems.report, no retry. The picks only exist in the screen's in-memory map during the undo window (items.remove at line 76).
- impact: If the user picks categories and leaves the screen (or the app is killed) during the undo window and the write fails, the choices vanish silently: the message goes back to waiting and nobody is told. It also bypasses rule 4 and 5 (a ui class owning a write lifecycle).
- fix: Give the commit to an application-scoped use case (the container scope already exists in AppContainer.kt:67) that reports failures through Problems and keeps the picks until the write succeeds.
- second reviewer: BankSmsR05.kt:79-87 clears the picks and launches on a throwaway CoroutineScope with runCatching whose result is dropped; it is called from onDispose at BankSmsScreen.kt:83, and SmsScreenExtras.kt:25-31 (the confirm path) reports nothing either. Severity lowered: on failure the message simply returns to the waiting list with no ledger write lost, and the app-killed case is not fixed by the proposed change unless picks are persisted.

### sec-firebase-2 [Complete and enforce App Check; restrict the API key in the console] - Open sign-up + public Firebase config + App Check off + no write size limits = one script can exhaust the free quota for all users
- severity: medium (finder said high) | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: partially_built
- where: `native-app/androidApp/build.gradle.kts:20`
- evidence: build.gradle.kts:19-20 App Check is off unless -Pmasroufy.appCheck=true; src/appCheckOn/.../AppCheckSetup.kt:15 says the on-path was never tried on a device. FIREBASE.md:46 (tracked, public repo) holds the full web config including the API key. FirebaseAuthAdapter.kt:50-53 registerWithEmail creates accounts with no email verification. firestore.rules:4-7 lets an account write anything of any size under users/{uid}/** (no type/size validation), and firestore.rules:57-60 lets any signed-in account create unlimited 2000-char inbox documents. HANDOVER.md:286 records AUTH-1 as partial.
- impact: Spark has shared daily caps (20k writes, 50k reads, 1 GiB). A stranger can create accounts with the public key and burn the write quota or fill storage; when the cap is hit, saving and syncing stop for every real user until the next day (or permanently for storage). No user data is exposed by this, it is an availability risk.
- fix: Finish AUTH-1: register the app for Play Integrity, build with App Check on, test, then enforce App Check for Firestore and Auth in the console. In Google Cloud restrict the Android API key to the package name + signing SHA. Add console usage alerts. Optionally require email verification before first write.
- second reviewer: androidApp/build.gradle.kts:19-20 and :78-80 keep App Check off by default, FirebaseAuthAdapter.kt:52 creates accounts with no verification, firestore.rules:4-7 has no size or type checks, and FIREBASE.md:46 holds the web API key in a tracked file. HANDOVER.md:286 already records AUTH-1 as partial and waiting on the owner. I rate it medium rather than high: it is a known, tracked availability risk with no data exposure, and I did not verify that the repo is public or what the console enforces.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: Owner approval is still pending for downloading the two App Check libraries and doing the console steps (HANDOVER question, AUTH-1).

### errors-8 [Connect the alert engine to the background cycle or document that it is off] - Background alert engine is not wired: the 4-hour cycle never sends due/budget notifications
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: later | effort: M | status: not_built

- evidence: backgroundCycle() builds BackgroundCycleDeps with candidates = null, engine = null (line 78; the comment at lines 57-60 says the engine is not connected yet). RunBackgroundCycle returns before the alert step when either is null (app/.../usecase/RunBackgroundCycle.kt:111). The header of BackgroundWork.kt (lines 30-31) still describes the periodic cycle as running the engine for today's due date and late instalments.
- impact: With the app closed, the only phone notifications are the three SMS-reading states; reminders for dues, instalments and budgets do not fire from the background. The periodic worker wakes the app every 4 hours mainly for repairs and SMS catch-up.
- fix: Wire GatherAllSpaceAlerts + RunAlertEngine into backgroundCycle (the cycle already supports it), or correct the comment and the release notes so nobody assumes reminders work while the app is closed.
- second reviewer: BackgroundWiring.kt:78 passes candidates = null, engine = null and RunBackgroundCycle.kt:111 returns before the alert step; the BackgroundWork.kt:30-31 header is stale. This is a known unbuilt part, already recorded in ARCHITECTURE.md:1485, HANDOVER.md:650 and PLAN-0.6.md:290, so it is not a new discovery. Not checked: whether the foreground engine (ShellGraph.kt:44) schedules future phone notices while the app is open, which would mean some reminders still fire after it closes.

### sms-7 [Count and surface SMS messages dropped before storage, per sender and reason] - Messages dropped by the pre-storage filter leave no trace and are never revisited
- severity: low | verdict: confirmed | urgency: later | effort: S | status: by_design
- where: `native-app/device/src/androidMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/device/AndroidSmsInbox.kt:158`
- evidence: SmsSafety.sanitize returns null for bodies over 8000 characters, for texts the guard classifies as code/offer/declined at the head, and for texts with no amount (SmsSafety.kt:64-68). SmsInboxStore.enqueue then returns false (line 174). In the scan the result is ignored and the cursor still advances past the message (AndroidSmsInbox.kt:158-159, 167); the live receiver simply returns false (BankSmsReceiver.kt:44). No counter or problem code records how many were dropped. The file comments describe several past rounds where real transactions were lost this way.
- impact: If a bank changes wording so that a real transaction trips the filter, it disappears silently; the owner sees nothing waiting and cannot know a transaction is missing until the balance drifts.
- fix: Keep a per-sender counter of dropped messages by reason (no text), show it in SMS settings, and include it in the usage data so template drift is noticed. Optionally let 'read from past' re-evaluate after an app update.
- second reviewer: SmsSafety.kt:64-68 returns null for over-length, guard-ignored and no-amount texts; AndroidSmsInbox.kt:158-159 ignores enqueue's result and line 167 still advances the cursor (the comment calls this intentional exclusion), and BankSmsReceiver.kt:44 just returns false. No counter or problem code records the drop. Intended behaviour, with a real observability gap.

### release-9 [Cover third-party data in the policy and verify phone-number redaction] - The app stores other people's names, account endings and debts with no stated basis or notice
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: by_design
- where: `native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/SmsTransferParties.kt:6`
- evidence: Lines 6-7: the counterparty of a transfer is extracted from bank messages as 'the name + last 4 digits' and the stored description is the redacted message text; lines 14-15 show Egyptian wallet messages carry the sender's mobile number and registered name. The app also has people, debts, shares and inheritance-of-another-person features (CODEMAP: Person in StorageEntities.kt, CalculateInheritance.kt 'estate of another person'). All of this syncs to the user's Firestore space.
- impact: This is personal data about people who never agreed to anything. For personal household use the legal exposure is low, but the privacy policy and the Play Data safety form must say it is collected and synced. The Egyptian case where a full mobile number may sit inside stored text should be checked against the 'last four only' rule (CLAUDE.md rule 11). I did not verify whether phone numbers are redacted like account numbers.
- fix: State it in the privacy policy (stored only in the user's own space, never shared, deleted with the account). Confirm redactSms also cuts mobile numbers in Egyptian wallet messages. Keep third-party names out of analytics and crash reports (already enforced by design per OVERRIDES section 114).

### release-5 [Create a signed release bundle variant and smoke-test it] - No store-ready build: 'release' is unminified and unsigned, and the only R8 variant is signed with the debug key
- severity: high | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: not_built
- where: `native-app/androidApp/build.gradle.kts:64`
- evidence: Lines 50-52: release has isMinifyEnabled = false and no signingConfig. Lines 58-67: the 'fast' variant enables R8 and resource shrinking but uses signingConfigs.getByName("debug") and adds the '-fast' suffix. Line 98: lint { checkReleaseBuilds = false }. proguard-rules.pro:6 uses -dontobfuscate (deliberate, fine). A git grep of build scripts and docs found no bundle task or bundle instructions for this app.
- impact: Nothing in the project can be uploaded to Play today: Play needs an app bundle signed with a non-debug upload key (from memory; not re-confirmed today). The first store build will be a new, never-tested variant, and R8 plus a new key is exactly where sign-in and Firestore mapping break.
- fix: Define a real release variant now: same R8 settings as 'fast', signing config read from files outside the repo (the repo is public), produced as an app bundle, enrolled in Play App Signing. Smoke-test it on the emulator and a phone, and re-enable lint for it or record why not.

### sec-firebase-6 [Create and back up a release/upload keystore; add release signing config] - All distributed builds are signed with the machine's debug key; no release signing exists
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: not_built
- where: `native-app/androidApp/build.gradle.kts:64`
- evidence: build.gradle.kts:41-42 debug uses ~/.android/debug.keystore whose SHA-1 is what Google sign-in is registered with; line 64 the owner/tester build type 'fast' sets signingConfig = signingConfigs.getByName("debug"); lines 50-52 the release type has no signing config at all.
- impact: The key that every tester's installed copy depends on lives only in one PC's user folder and is not a managed secret. If that PC or folder is lost, no update can be installed over existing copies and Google sign-in stops until a new fingerprint is registered. Google Play will not accept this key, so the store build needs a new key anyway and current testers will have to reinstall.
- fix: Create a real upload keystore kept outside the repo (already ignored by .gitignore:39-40,58-59), read its passwords from an untracked properties file or environment, back it up in two places, register its SHA-1/SHA-256 in Firebase, and plan the tester migration once.
- second reviewer: androidApp/build.gradle.kts:41-42 uses the default debug keystore, :64 signs the owner/tester 'fast' build with signingConfigs "debug", and :50-52 gives release no signing config. The comment at :56 states the choice is deliberate, to keep Google sign-in working and install over 0.1, so this is a known pre-store gap rather than an accident. The backup risk of the single debug key is real.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: When to create the store signing key and move testers to it.

### release-6 [Create and back up the release key and migrate the two current users now] - Everyone is on the machine-local debug key; the planned key change forces uninstall for every tester added before it
- severity: high | verdict: unverified | urgency: now | effort: S | status: by_design
- where: `OVERRIDES.md:2681`
- evidence: OVERRIDES.md:2681 (section 118 item 2): stay on the test key until just before the store, then change once; 'the owner and his brother delete the app and install it again, data comes back from Firebase'. build.gradle.kts:41 and 64 confirm debug and fast are signed with ~/.android/debug.keystore, whose SHA-1 is what Google sign-in is registered against. PLAN-0.6.md:32 shows keystores are git-ignored, so the key lives only on the owner's PC.
- impact: Three risks. (a) If that PC or user profile is lost, the key is gone and nobody can update. (b) Each new tester added before the switch is one more person who must uninstall later. (c) Uninstall wipes anything that only lives on the phone: the pending bank-SMS inbox, device backups and settings. I did not inventory which on-device stores are not synced. The new key also needs its fingerprints registered for Google sign-in and later App Check, or sign-in breaks.
- fix: Do the key switch now while there are two users, not right before the store. Generate the release key, back it up in two places outside the repo, register SHA-1 and SHA-256 in Firebase, and tell both users to sync and export a backup before reinstalling. Back up the debug keystore today regardless.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: Section 118 item 2 says switch the key just before the store. Move it earlier, before any new tester is added?

### countries-7 [Decide and fix payday inheritance for non-default spaces] - Egypt payday chosen equal to Saudi's is not stored, so it silently follows later Saudi changes
- severity: low | verdict: weakened | urgency: later | effort: S | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/CountryPayday.kt:22`
- evidence: savedFromSpace (lines 19-24) writes the country's day into paydays only if payday != base.paydayIn(spaceId). paydayIn (line 10) falls back to the Saudi payday when the country has no entry. So an explicit choice equal to the inherited value is never stored.
- impact: Saudi payday is 28. In Egypt the owner opens the setting and keeps 28 on purpose (Egyptian salary also on the 28th); nothing is stored. Later he changes the Saudi payday to 25. Egypt's fiscal month now also starts on the 25th. OVERRIDES §111-3 says Egypt's payday does not change Saudi's month, but the reverse dependency remains for a country with no stored day, and all Egypt periods and budgets shift without any action in Egypt.
- fix: Store the country's day whenever the user saves the payday screen from that country (an explicit save), or snapshot the inherited value into paydays when the country is created.
- second reviewer: CountryPayday.kt:19-24 behaves as described, but it is documented design: the comment at lines 16-17 explains the 'only if changed' rule (to stop unrelated Egypt profile saves from pinning Saudi's day), and OVERRIDES.md:2557 (§111-3) states a country without a day takes Saudi's. SpaceProfileRepository.kt:16 routes every profile save through it, so 'store on every save' would reintroduce the pinning problem; the edge case (explicit same value) is real but needs an owner decision and a dedicated payday-save path, not the proposed fix as written.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: Should a country with no payday of its own keep following Saudi forever, or freeze the value when the country is created?

### sms-3 [Decide and implement what part of the SMS text is persisted to Firestore] - Bank message text is stored in Firestore permanently (names, balance, merchant)
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: by_design
- where: `native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/BankSmsModel.kt:244`
- evidence: smsRow sets description = raw = redactSms(body) (BankSmsModel.kt:238-244) and the recorder saves it as the transaction's rawDescription (app/.../RecordLines.kt:40); the source record carries the same raw text. redactSms only masks phone numbers, IBANs and runs of 5 or more digits (BankSmsModel.kt:183-207) and explicitly keeps amounts after 'balance/الرصيد' (REDACT_KEEP, lines 193-196). So counterparty names in transfers, the account balance after each operation, merchant, and any 4-digit numbers stay in the stored text. I found no retention or trimming step. Unknown and rejected messages are NOT uploaded (only a reason string is kept: ReviewSmsInbox.kt:90) and OTP messages are dropped before device storage (SmsVocabulary.kt:106).
- impact: Every recorded transaction carries a near-complete copy of the bank message in the cloud forever. That raises the cost of any rules mistake or account compromise, and it must be declared accurately in the Play data-safety form and privacy policy.
- fix: Decide what is really needed after parsing (merchant, reference tail, fee, the template key) and either store only those fields or strip balance and party names from the saved description; alternatively keep the full text on the device only. Document the decision in OVERRIDES.md.
- second reviewer: BankSmsModel.kt:238-244 sets description and raw to redactSms(body), and RecordLines.kt:40 saves it as rawDescription; redactSms (lines 201-215) masks only phones, IBANs and runs of 5+ digits and deliberately keeps balance amounts (REDACT_KEEP, 193-196). OVERRIDES.md:1008 records the masking rule as intended, but I found no decision on retention or on stripping names and balance, so it is by design with an open privacy decision.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: Keep the full (masked) bank message text in the cloud with each transaction, or keep only the fields the app needs?

### release-7 [Decide and record store application id and version scheme] - Application id, version name and version code are not settled for the store
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: partially_built

- evidence: Lines 27-31: applicationId 'app.masroufy.mobile', versionCode 12, versionName 'kotlin-0.5.5' (plus '-fast' at line 65), namespace 'app.masroufy.android' at line 23. The frozen old app uses 'app.masroufy.personal' at versionCode 47, versionName '1.46' (android/app/build.gradle:7-11). firebase-trial uses 'app.masroufy.trial' with .direct and .gitlive suffixes. HANDOVER.md:659 itself hedges 'app.masroufy.android (or the actual package name)'. ARCHITECTURE.md:1030 still documents a backup path under app.masroufy.personal.
- impact: The id chosen at first upload is permanent on Play. If the store app is app.masroufy.mobile, anyone on the old app gets no update path and the old on-device backups sit under a different folder. A user-visible version like 'kotlin-0.5.5-fast' looks unfinished in the listing and in crash reports, and the version code has no written rule.
- fix: Record one decision in OVERRIDES: the store id, a clean version name (for example 0.6.0), and a version code rule that only ever increases. Confirm the trial ids are never registered in the production Firebase project for release.
