### sms-8 [Document or fix same-second identical SMS collapse] - Two genuine identical messages in the same second collapse into one
- severity: low | verdict: confirmed | urgency: later | effort: S | status: by_design
- where: `native-app/device/src/androidMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/device/SmsInboxStore.kt:182`
- evidence: The queue's primary key is (owner, id) where id = sha(sender|timestamp|sanitised body) (SmsInboxStore.kt:22, 177; SmsSafety.kt:92) and inserts use CONFLICT_IGNORE (line 182). The ledger reference has the same composition (BankSmsModel.kt:243) and an equal reference is classified DUPLICATE (Dedupe.kt:106-111). Two real charges of the same amount at the same merchant whose texts carry no running balance and share the same network second are indistinguishable.
- impact: A rare real double charge could be recorded once, which is precisely the case the 'duplicate?' feature is meant to catch.
- fix: Include the provider row _id (scan path) or a per-second sequence in the queue id while keeping the ledger reference stable, or accept and document the limitation.
- second reviewer: SmsInboxStore.kt:22 keys on (owner,id), id = sha(sender|timestamp|body) (SmsSafety.kt:92), inserted with CONFLICT_IGNORE (line 182); the ledger reference has the same composition (BankSmsModel.kt:243). Two identical-text messages with the same timestamp collapse into one; this is rare and the SmsSafety.kt:91 comment shows it is intended.

### sec-firebase-8 [Document residual data after deletion; decide on uid in crash reports] - Account deletion leaves data outside users/{uid}: crash reports keyed by uid, inbox messages, shared contributions
- severity: low | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: by_design
- where: `native-app/androidApp/src/main/kotlin/app/masroufy/android/CrashReports.kt:64`
- evidence: CrashReports.kt:64 sets the Crashlytics user id to the Firebase uid, and line 30 defaults crash reporting to ON. FirestoreAccountEraser.kt:14 and UserCollections.kt:43-47 state that sharedMerchants and inbox are kept on purpose (no uid in them, OVERRIDES §80). Inside users/{uid} the diff is empty: the eraser iterates USER_COLLECTIONS (UserCollections.kt:29-35 = all codec groups + backup groups + profile + initialization + concurrency + spaceTransfers + alert groups + userSettings + legacy names) for the account root and every spaces/{id}, and every repository writes through FirestoreSpace.collection(group) (FirestoreSpace.kt:29).
- impact: After 'delete my account', crash reports already sent stay in Firebase linked to the old uid until their retention ends, and any free-text support message stays in inbox until the private job removes it. This must be declared correctly in the Play data-safety and deletion form; it is not a leak.
- fix: State it in the privacy text and Play form; on delete call setUserId("") and deleteUnsentReports (already done on sign-out path via session(null)); consider not attaching uid to crash reports at all.
- second reviewer: CrashReports.kt:64 sets the Crashlytics user id to the uid and :30 defaults reporting to on. Documented as intended at CrashReports.kt:21 ('identity = Firebase uid only', OVERRIDES §90/§80). I did not open FirestoreAccountEraser or UserCollections, so the claim that nothing is left inside users/{uid} is unverified. This is a disclosure item, not a leak.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: Crash reports on by default with uid attached is still an open owner question (CrashReports.kt:18-19).

### release-8 [Emulator-test account deletion and publish a web deletion page] - 'Delete my account' was never run against Firebase, and there is no web deletion link
- severity: high | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: partially_built
- where: `OVERRIDES.md:2721`
- evidence: OVERRIDES.md:2721 describes the flow (Account screen, type the word, SignIn.deleteAccount: server data, then phone, then the login account) and ends 'tested in memory only — not tried on Firebase or the emulator'. Line 2723 leaves an open owner question that some collections may be outside the list. FirestoreAccountEraser.kt:10-14 confirms deletion works from a hand-kept list of collection names (USER_COLLECTIONS, KNOWN_SPACE_IDS) because the mobile SDK cannot enumerate subcollections; its header says a blocker was fixed in r06-fix055b, but I found no record of an emulator run. Server data is erased (lines 20-25) before the login account is deleted, which needs a recent sign-in. No web deletion page exists in the repo (git ls-files).
- impact: Play requires both an in-app deletion path and a web link where a user can request deletion without the app. A collection missing from the list means private financial data survives a 'deleted' account, which is a privacy breach and a policy violation. The recent-login failure path can leave an empty login account behind.
- fix: Run the flow on the Firebase emulator with a seeded account in two countries and assert that nothing remains under users/{uid}. Add a test that fails when a repository writes to a collection not in the eraser list. Publish a simple web page (same host as the privacy policy) explaining how to request deletion and what is kept (anonymous shared merchant rules, feedback without uid, crash reports for a limited period).
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: Open question in section 118: approve testing account deletion on the Firebase emulator before any wider release?

### countries-3 [Enforce 25 senders per country and surface dropped senders] - Bank cap is 50 in total, not 25 per country, and auto-add stops silently at the cap
- severity: low (finder said medium) | verdict: weakened | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/SmsSenderLimit.kt:10`
- evidence: SmsSenderLimit.kt:10 sets MAX_SMS_SENDERS = 25 × the number of country packs that have a reader (2), so 50, regardless of how many countries the user has open. Its comment (line 5) says the device keeps one sender list for all countries. Every check I found compares only the total: ManageSmsInbox.kt:145, ReadBankSms.kt:49, AndroidBankSms.kt:36, AndroidSmsInbox.kt:49. KnownBankSenders.kt:32-35 (sendersWithKnown) breaks out of the loop once the list reaches max and drops the remaining known senders. ui/screens/imports/SmsHandoff.kt:93-96 (connectKnownBanks) then enables the truncated list and returns only a count.
- impact: A Saudi-only user can add 50 senders, although §112-7 says 25 per country. With two countries, one country can use all 50 slots; a bank wallet then added in the other country is not auto-registered, so its SMS never reach the app, and this function raises no error. Whether a caller warns from the returned count was not checked.
- fix: Count senders per country (by the wallet mapping per space, or by tagging senders with a space) and enforce 25 for each. When sendersWithKnown drops a sender, return the dropped names so the screen can say which bank was not connected.
- second reviewer: The 50-total cap is a documented state, not a bug: OVERRIDES.md:2568 (§112-7) says the device list is shared so the limit is 25 x countries with a reader (50 now), and leaves per-country splitting as an open owner question, which must not be decided unilaterally. The silent-drop part is real: KnownBankSenders.kt:32-35 breaks at max and SmsHandoff.kt:90-97,149-153 only shows a generic toast based on n > 0, so a partially dropped bank is not named; it needs 50 existing senders to trigger, so it is rare.

### arch-4 [Extract SMS screen orchestration from wiring into a use case with a lock] - SmsGraph in the composition module holds real orchestration and unsynchronised session state
- severity: low (finder said medium) | verdict: weakened | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/wiring/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/wiring/SmsGraph.kt:107`
- evidence: SmsGraph.kt is 298 lines (cap is 300) and is not just wiring: overview() at 107-129 maps senders to wallets, builds a review per bank, filters duplicates and dismisses messages; record() at 166-177 iterates the plain var 'last' (line 68) that overview() assigns at line 123 from a background dispatcher, with no lock; settingsCache (74) and memo (71) are also mutable instance state. The header comment at lines 49-50 admits targetOf is a copy of the private AutoRecordSms.walletFor. wiring holds 18 control-flow lines in total, 11 of them in this file.
- impact: The riskiest money path (recording bank messages into wallets) partly lives outside the use-case layer, with a duplicated wallet rule that can drift from the background one. A pull-to-refresh finishing while 'record all' runs can swap the list being recorded, so what gets saved may differ from what was shown.
- fix: Move this into an app/usecase class (for example SmsScreenSession) that owns 'last' behind a Mutex and exposes the bank target rule from AutoRecordSms instead of copying it; leave SmsGraph as pure construction.
- second reviewer: SmsGraph.kt:107-129 and 166-177 do hold orchestration and an unlocked var last (line 68), and the header at lines 49-50 admits the copied wallet rule, so the layering point stands. The claimed race is wrong in effect: the for loop at line 168 iterates the list it captured, so a concurrent overview() cannot change what is being recorded; the real side effect is line 175 (last = emptyList()) wiping the fresh overview so the next record-all returns 0 until a reload. That is a nuisance, not a wrong-wallet or wrong-items write.

### build-2 [Fail the fast build when the signing fingerprint is not the registered one, and confirm the key file is backed up.] - 'fast' depends on a per-machine debug keystore that is not in the repo and the build never verifies its fingerprint
- severity: high | verdict: unverified | urgency: now | effort: S | status: partially_built
- where: `native-app/androidApp/build.gradle.kts:64`
- evidence: Line 41 comment: the default dev key (~/.android/debug.keystore) has its SHA-1 registered in the owner's Firebase project for Google sign-in. Line 64: 'fast' signs with signingConfigs.getByName("debug"). AGP auto-generates a new debug.keystore when none exists, so a build on any other machine succeeds with a different certificate. Nothing in native-app checks the certificate. By contrast the old app's script scripts/build-android.ps1:12 throws when the trial key is missing ('never generate a replacement') and lines 41-45 compare the new APK certificate with the previous APK. PLAN-0.6.md line 30 (SECRETS-1) already lists the loss risk.
- impact: A next AI model or a reinstalled Windows builds 'fast' on a clean machine: the APK looks fine but cannot install over the testers' existing app (signature mismatch means uninstall, losing the local SMS inbox database) and Google sign-in fails because the new SHA-1 is not registered. The failure appears only on the tester's phone.
- fix: Add a small Gradle verification task finalizing assembleFast that reads the signing certificate SHA-256 of the output APK and fails if it differs from an expected fingerprint constant kept in the repo (fingerprints are public, not secret). Document in HANDOVER that ~/.android/debug.keystore must be restored from the owner's backup on a new machine, and confirm a backup actually exists outside the PC.

### countries-2 [Fetch USD_EGP in the prices job and prefill the settle rate] - No EGP exchange rate in the price feed: 'both countries' total and 'today's rate' never work
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: partially_built
- where: `native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/usecase/LoadFutureInputs.kt:96`
- evidence: LoadFutureInputs.fx() (lines 93-98) returns null unless the feed has both USD_SAR and USD_EGP. A grep for USD_EGP across scripts/, .github/, public/, prices/ and data/ finds nothing; only USD_SAR appears, at scripts/fetch-prices.mjs:88 and public/prices.json:5. SettleOtherCurrency.kt:20 prefillRate4() is hard-coded to return null, with a comment saying there is no EGP source (cty-11). core/InvestFutureMath.kt:175-183 countriesTotal returns null when fx is null.
- impact: OVERRIDES §111-4 says a debt paid in the other currency comes down 'at today's rate, and you can edit the rate'. In practice the rate field is always empty and the owner must look the rate up and type it every time. The combined two-country figure in the Future screen is always 'unavailable'. No wrong number is produced, but the feature looks finished and is not (CLAUDE.md rule 15).
- fix: Add USD_EGP to scripts/fetch-prices.mjs (GitHub Actions, no Cloud Functions needed), then make prefillRate4 read the same FxRate. Until then, list this as missing in release notes.
- second reviewer: LoadFutureInputs.kt:93-98 needs a USD_EGP row and SettleOtherCurrency.kt:20 returns null; no row with that symbol is written anywhere. Correction to the evidence: scripts/fetch-prices.mjs:155-160 already fetches the Yahoo EGP=X rate into usdToEgpScaled and only uses it for gold/silver, so the fix is a single results.USD_EGP entry (in piasters with currency 'EGP', matching LoadFutureInputsTest.kt:116). This is a documented open item, not a hidden one: OVERRIDES.md:2312 lists the USD_EGP row as pending (Q5) and OVERRIDES.md:2558 (§111-4) states the rate field is empty and mandatory until then.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: Which source to use for the USD/EGP rate (official bank rate vs market rate) is an owner decision.

### tests-6 [First 5 Compose interaction tests on the memory graph; move PNG generators out of the test count] - UI layer: no interaction tests, 12 render tests are no-ops by default, androidApp has zero tests
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: not_built
- where: `native-app/ui/src/jvmTest/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/screens/operations/TopSpendPngTest.kt:116`
- evidence: ui has 479 commonMain + 94 androidMain files and 129 test files / 563 @Test, all model/state/text tests (e.g. HomeModelTest). `git grep runComposeUiTest|createComposeRule|onNodeWithText` returns nothing and no ui-test dependency appears in any build.gradle.kts. TopSpendPngTest.kt:116 `System.getenv("TOPSPEND_PNG_DIR") ... ?: return`; the same early return is in CharacterComposePngTest:28, CharacterFramePngTest:58, CharacterPixelDiffTest:18, CharacterSheetTest:30/40, BnplPngTest:99, FuturePngTest:128, HoldingsPngTest:79, InsightsRenderTest:103: they pass without doing anything in a normal run. androidApp: 0 test files. The 0.5.5 emulator smoke found exactly this class of bug (HANDOVER.md:222 undo bar missing after deleting a wallet/asset/income source; :226 an ANR).
- impact: Navigation, sheets, undo bars, back handling and the Android glue are only checked by a manual emulator session. Regressions there are user-visible and the test count gives no protection. The passing count is also slightly inflated by render tests that return immediately.
- fix: Add a small set of Compose Desktop `runComposeUiTest` flows on the memory graph (add operation, delete with undo, import review, switch country). Mark the PNG generators as tools (separate Gradle task or @Ignore with reason) so they do not count as passing tests.

### tests-1 [Fix toSortedMap in AssistGuards.kt, push, verify native-core is green, note CI status in HANDOVER] - CI has been red for five weeks: JVM-only toSortedMap in core commonMain breaks the iOS step
- severity: high | verdict: unverified | urgency: now | effort: S | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/AssistGuards.kt:106`
- evidence: Line 106: `s.entities.ofType(AssistEntityType.WALLET).groupBy { it.start }.toSortedMap().values.map {...}`. toSortedMap is JVM-only. `gh run list --workflow native-core.yml`: last 5 runs all failure (2026-10-10 .. 2026-10-24), last success 2026-10-09. Annotations of run 40075627855 (head 7d851991, the commit right under r051-int): `AssistGuards.kt:106:83 Unresolved reference 'toSortedMap'` in step 'iOS simulator test'; the JVM step passed. grep of HANDOVER.md for native-core / CI finds no mention of the failure. The local gate in HANDOVER section 2 (`:core:compileKotlinMetadata`) evidently does not catch it.
- impact: The shared core no longer compiles for iPhone, and nobody is looking at the only automated gate; any further iOS-only break piles up unseen behind this one. A permanently red CI trains every session to ignore it.
- fix: Replace with a common-stdlib form, e.g. `.groupBy { it.start }.entries.sortedBy { it.key }.map { (_, at) -> at.firstOrNull { !it.generic } ?: at.first() }`, push, confirm green. Add a HANDOVER rule: after every push read `gh run list` and record the result; add `:core:compileKotlinIosArm64` is not possible on Windows, so CI status must be the check.

### tests-3 [Full instrumented run (firestore + device) as a recorded release gate] - The 78 instrumented tests that prove Firestore and device adapters are run by hand and only partially
- severity: high | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: partially_built
- where: `HANDOVER.md:216`
- evidence: firestore/src/androidInstrumentedTest has 52 @Test in 26 classes, device/src/androidInstrumentedTest 26; firestore/src/jvmTest has only 21 @Test in 5 files (mirror/guards, no repository). HANDOVER.md:216 (round fix055b): only `AccountEraserOnFirestoreTest` was run on the emulator. HANDOVER-ARCHIVE.md:3897: firestore connected tests run 'with a filter of the classes the slices touched' (15 of 15). HANDOVER-ARCHIVE.md:3894 item 7: 'emulator tests: the slices built them and nobody ran them'. No workflow runs connected tests. Two classes skip silently via assumeTrue when owner files are absent (RealStatementOnFirestoreTest.kt:108, RealPdfPagesTest.kt:59).
- impact: Every money write that reaches the real database goes through adapters whose regression suite is not part of any release gate. A codec or query change can break saving or reading on the phone while 2,900 memory-based tests stay green.
- fix: Make a full `:firestore:connectedDebugAndroidTest` + `:device:connectedDebugAndroidTest` run a mandatory, recorded line of the release checklist (count from XML, e.g. 52/52 and 26/26). Later: move repository tests that need no Android API to a JVM run against the Firestore emulator REST/Java SDK, or run them in CI with an emulator action.

### sms-5 [Give the background SMS run a deadline and new-only mode; time it with 5,000 queued messages] - Every background cycle re-reads and re-evaluates the whole waiting queue with no time limit
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/usecase/RunBackgroundCycle.kt:88`
- evidence: The worker calls sms.run() with no memo and no deadline (RunBackgroundCycle.kt:88; AutoRecordSms.kt:104-105 passes deadline = null). walk() then pages through the entire queue, 200 at a time, up to MAX_PAGE_PASSES = 1000 (AutoRecordSms.kt:169-198); for each page every country parses every message and each wallet route builds a full import preview (SmsRouteWalk.kt:55-70), then routes.waiting() parses the newest page again (AutoRecordSms.kt:106). Messages that wait for the owner never leave the queue and nothing purges them (SmsInboxStore only deletes in forget(), line 66-69). This cycle is requested after every incoming bank SMS as expedited work (BankSmsReceiver.kt:25, BackgroundWork.kt:66-70) and every 4 hours. After 'read all history' the queue can hold thousands of unconfirmed messages.
- impact: On a phone with a large waiting queue, each new bank SMS triggers minutes of work and many Firestore reads; the system may stop the worker before it reaches the new message, so automatic recording becomes late or unreliable and battery use rises. Not timed in this review.
- fix: Use the existing memo/newOnly path (as runNew does) and a deadline in the background cycle; only do a full walk when something changed (new learned shape, new wallet mapping). Cache per-wallet ledger/preview inputs across pages. Add the 5,000-message timing to the release smoke test.
- second reviewer: BackgroundWiring.kt:68-78 builds AutoRecordSms with no memo and RunBackgroundCycle.kt:88 calls run() with no deadline; walk() (AutoRecordSms.kt:161-200) then pages the whole queue with newOnly=false, building a preview per wallet route per page (SmsRouteWalk.kt:55-70), and waiting messages are never acknowledged out of the queue. No timeout wraps the SMS step in the worker (grep for withTimeout found none there). The actual duration is unmeasured, so the user-visible delay is plausible but not demonstrated.

### sync-3 [Give the Firestore unit of work a real single-batch commit for delete, undo and restore use cases.] - Multi-collection delete and undo are documented as atomic but run as separate fire-and-forget batches
- severity: medium (finder said high) | verdict: weakened | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/firestore/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/firestore/FirestoreContainer.kt:21`
- evidence: FirestoreUnitOfWork.run is 'work()' (line 20-22), a pass-through. DeleteTransaction.delete (app/.../usecase/DeleteTransaction.kt:107-128, comment 'بيمسح ذرّيًا') performs up to seven independent commits inside it: tag links, project links, event links, allocations, person links, sourceRecords.saveMany with transactionId = null (line 123), partner unlink, then txns.deleteMany (line 126). DeleteDebt.kt:95-107 and both restore() functions follow the same pattern. Each repository call is its own WriteBatch (FirestoreSpace.kt:101-118) launched without awaiting the result (FirestoreSpace.kt:53-65).
- impact: If the process dies or one batch is rejected between steps, the data is left half-changed: for example the source record is already detached and marked owner-deleted while the transaction still exists and still counts in the balance, or an undo restores the transaction without its links. Nothing detects or repairs this later, and the full backup check rejects rows pointing at missing transactions, so the next backup can fail.
- fix: Make FirestoreUnitOfWork collect the writes of one use case into a single WriteBatch (these deletes are far below 500 operations) and commit once; keep the pass-through only for the import path that has its own staged/committed protocol. Apply the mirror changes after the single commit is issued.
- second reviewer: FirestoreContainer.kt:20-22 is a pass-through and DeleteTransaction.kt:112-128 does issue up to seven separate batches despite the 'atomic' comment, so the non-atomicity is real. But FirestoreSpace.kt:53-65 launches each commit UNDISPATCHED without awaiting the server, so all batches are queued into the SDK's local outbox within milliseconds and are sent in order; the crash window is tiny, and a per-batch server rejection is unlikely because all documents sit under the same users/{uid} rule. The pass-through is also a documented choice (FirestoreContainer.kt:13-19) rather than an oversight, so this is a medium robustness gap, not high.

### perf-8 [Group-by in memory instead of per-person queries in LoadPeopleOverview] - People tab and the Home people card load on the UI thread with one query set per person
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: later | effort: S | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/usecase/LoadPeopleOverview.kt:56`
- evidence: load() (lines 53-75): for every person it calls across.debts(p.id) which (AcrossSpaces.kt:66-68) does obligations.listByPerson + settlements.listByObligations, then per person s.links.listByPerson(p.id) and s.txns.findByIds(...) (a pass over all transactions in mirrorIn, DocQuery.kt:75-85). Each listByPerson is a full scan of its collection (FirestoreLedgerRepositories.kt:20-21, FirestoreEvents.kt:29). Called from PeopleScreen.kt:98 and HomeCardsData.kt:58 with no dispatcher switch; LoadPeopleOverview has no withContext.
- impact: With P people: 3 x P collection scans, plus one 3,000-row pass for each person who has event links. For 40 people and a few hundred obligations/settlements this is tens of thousands of checks on the UI thread (estimate 10-40 ms), and it grows as people x history. Runs on every Home refresh when the people card is shown.
- fix: Read obligations, settlements and links once with listAll and group by person in memory; wrap forSpace in the work context in the people graph.

### errors-2 [Guard AccountSession opening job and add a failed state with retry] - Session-opening coroutine has no error handling: a failed read at startup kills the app
- severity: medium (finder said high) | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/firestore/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/firestore/AccountSession.kt:166`
- evidence: switchTo() launches openingJob (lines 166-196) with no try/catch. Line 176 calls FirestoreSpaceRegistry(account).listAll(), which is select() in DocQuery.kt:97-103: when the mirror is not readable (the 500 ms grace OFFLINE_GRACE_MS expired on a returning device, or the listener of that group failed and was marked stale) it falls back to a direct Firestore get() that can throw. The scope is CoroutineScope(SupervisorJob() + Dispatchers.Default) (AppContainer.kt:67) and a grep for CoroutineExceptionHandler in production returns nothing, so the exception reaches Thread.setDefaultUncaughtExceptionHandler (CrashReports.kt:81-89), which ends the process. The file's own comment (lines 202-204) records that exactly this path once crashed the whole app with PERMISSION_DENIED; only that one cause (account switch) was fixed by cancelling the job. The `completion` launch at line 190 is unguarded too.
- impact: Any exception while opening the account (permission error, decode error of a spaces document, transient Firestore failure) crashes the app during startup, and it will crash again on every launch while the condition lasts. The same code runs when the background worker wakes the process (AppContainer.kt:212), so it can also crash in the background.
- fix: Wrap the body of openingJob in try/catch (rethrow CancellationException), report a ProblemCode, and move to an explicit State.Failed (or fall back to the default space only) that the UI renders with a retry. Also give the app scope a CoroutineExceptionHandler that reports instead of killing the process.
- second reviewer: AccountSession.kt:166-196 has no try/catch, AppContainer.kt:67 scope has no CoroutineExceptionHandler (none anywhere in native-app), and CrashReports.kt:81-89 ends the process; the comment at AccountSession.kt:202-204 records a real crash on this path. The trigger is narrower than 'any transient failure': listAll (DocQuery.kt:97-103) only reaches the throwing get() when the spaces listener failed (LocalMirror.kt:82,93) or the 15 s wait expired, or a spaces document fails to decode; on a never-synced device a failed listener hangs at line 175 instead of crashing. Fix is correct; severity medium for the rare trigger.

### errors-4 [Guard AddOperationSheet save/load and the remaining unguarded launches] - Add-operation sheet (the + button) and ~19 other launches are unguarded: an unexpected exception crashes the app
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/shell/AddOperationSheet.kt:190`
- evidence: The save handler (lines 190-208) does scope.launch { ... shell.addOperation(draft) ... saving = false } with no try/catch, and QuickAddOperation.save (app/.../usecase/QuickAddOperation.kt:90-105) only catches IllegalArgumentException. The LaunchedEffect at line 100 calls shell.addOptions() unguarded as well. A helper for exactly this exists (failureOf in ui/.../screens/operations/OperationsParts.kt:55) and is used on most screens, but a scan found 19 one-line launches without it, e.g. LockScreen.kt:67 (gate.unlock()), BellPopover.kt:119 (markAllRead), PersonProfileScreen.kt:80 (switchSpace), OperationDetailScreen.kt:84-87 and 188, BankSmsSheets.kt:63, BudgetsRows.kt:136. An exception escaping a rememberCoroutineScope launch has no handler and goes to the uncaught-exception handler.
- impact: Any non-IllegalArgument failure during the most common manual action (IllegalStateException from a use case, a repository read falling back to the server and failing) closes the app instead of showing a message; if it did not crash, `saving` would stay true and the button would spin forever.
- fix: Wrap these launches with failureOf/toaster (or a shared safeLaunch helper that always resets the busy flag in finally); make QuickAddOperation.save map every non-cancellation exception to AddOperationResult.Invalid.
- second reviewer: AddOperationSheet.kt:190-208 launches with no try/catch and saving=false is not in a finally; line 100 shell.addOptions() is unguarded; ShellGraph.kt:119 passes straight to QuickAddOperation.save, which catches only IllegalArgumentException (QuickAddOperation.kt:103). Also verified unguarded: LockScreen.kt:67, BellPopover.kt:119, PersonProfileScreen.kt:80, OperationDetailScreen.kt:84-87; the total of 19 was not counted. Realistic triggers are few because writes are fire-and-forget and reads come from the in-memory mirror, so medium at most.

### countries-8 [Guard test and override for Egypt DST rule changes] - Cairo daylight-saving rules are hard-coded, and a second time source (device tzdata) is used for on-demand reads
- severity: low | verdict: weakened | urgency: later | effort: S | status: by_design
- where: `native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/CountryTime.kt:30`
- evidence: cairoLocal (lines 27-34) applies +3 from the last Friday of April to the last Thursday of October for every year >= 2023, with no end year and no remote override. On-demand SMS reading instead uses java.time with the device's tzdata: AppContainer.kt:191 passes ZoneId.of(pack.timeZone) and AndroidBankSms.kt:42-43 computes the day window with atStartOfDay(zone). I checked the current rule by hand and it is correct (start Friday 00:00, end Thursday 24:00 summer time).
- impact: Egypt has changed DST by decree several times (abolished 2011, back 2014, abolished 2015, back 2023). If it changes again, every dateless Egyptian SMS arriving in the affected hour (23:00-24:00 or 00:00-01:00) is recorded on the wrong day until an app update. In the same period the on-demand read window (device tzdata, possibly updated) and the parsed day (hard-coded) would disagree by one hour at day edges.
- fix: Keep the pure implementation, but add a test that fails when java.time disagrees for the current year plus one, and consider a small remote-configurable override (start/end dates per year) in the existing prices/config file.
- second reviewer: The hard-coded rule at CountryTime.kt:27-34 is by design (file header lines 4-7), and the proposed guard already largely exists: CountryTimeTest.kt:15,24,38 compares against java.time every half hour from 2015 to the end of 2027. What remains is that the range is fixed rather than 'current year + 1' and it only catches a decree after the build JDK's tzdata updates; the device-tzdata vs hard-coded mismatch (AppContainer.kt:191) is hypothetical until Egypt changes its rule.

### countries-4 [Handle archived-space leg on space-transfer unlink] - Unlinking a self-transfer while one country is archived leaves an orphan 'internal transfer' leg
- severity: low (finder said medium) | verdict: confirmed | urgency: later | effort: S | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/usecase/TransferBetweenSpaces.kt:166`
- evidence: survivingLegs (lines 166-172) skips any leg whose deps.book(spaceId) is null; the deps comment at line 76 says null means the country is missing or archived. unlink (128-132) and legsIn().detach (146-148, used when an import batch is reverted) then delete the pair and reset only the legs that were found. The skipped leg keeps what asSpaceTransferLeg set (core/SpaceTransfers.kt:58-61): INTERNAL_TRANSFER, confirmed, reviewState CONFIRMED.
- impact: Example: transfer 1,000 SAR to 13,000 EGP, archive Egypt, then unlink the pair from Saudi or revert the Saudi import batch. The Saudi leg returns to 'unclassified' and is asked about again. The 13,000 EGP incoming leg stays a confirmed internal transfer with no pair. After Egypt is restored it is counted as neither income nor a transfer pair, and nothing asks about it. I did not confirm whether the screen offers unlink while a country is archived.
- fix: Either refuse unlink/detach while one side is archived (with a clear message), or open the archived space's repositories for this write so both legs are always reset together.
- second reviewer: TransferBetweenSpaces.kt:166-172 skips a leg when deps.book returns null, and SpaceLegsWiring.kt:25 builds books only from the session's open spaces, so an archived country's leg is left as a confirmed internal transfer while the pair is deleted; the comment at line 165 shows this was a deliberate choice. SpaceTransferScreen.kt:72,122 lists all pairs and calls unlink with no archived check, so the path is reachable. Severity lowered because it needs archive-then-unlink (rare) and the orphan leg stays out of income rather than producing a wrong total; I did not verify what happens on restore.

### perf-11 [Index transactions once in CoveredSmsRepair] - Old-SMS repair checks each candidate with its own full scan of all transactions
- severity: low | verdict: unverified | urgency: later | effort: S | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/usecase/CoveredSmsRepair.kt:212`
- evidence: alreadyRecorded (lines 211-214) calls deps.txns.listByDateRange(row.date, row.date + "T23:59:59.999Z") and is called once per surviving source record inside the candidate loop at line 107. Each call is a scan of the whole transactions mirror (DocQuery.kt:45). rowOf (lines 190-199) also re-parses the message body once per arrival anchor.
- impact: With 1,000 old SMS records reaching that line and 3,000 transactions: about 3 million document checks plus 1,000 small sorts, estimate 1-3 s for the repair screen to list its rows. Owner-triggered and rare, so low.
- fix: Load the covered date range once before the loop and index it by (walletId, day, amount, direction).

### arch-3 [Keep shell state above the Activity and save the navigation stack] - Navigation and shell state are created per Activity and never saved
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/androidApp/src/main/kotlin/app/masroufy/android/MainActivity.kt:37`
- evidence: MainActivity.kt:37 does shell = ShellState() in onCreate with no use of savedInstanceState. ShellState (ui/app/AppShell.kt:63-79) owns Navigator, overlays, toaster and the assistant draft; Navigator (ui/nav/Navigator.kt:50-52) keeps the stack in plain mutableStateListOf with a local id counter and has no saver. AndroidManifest.xml:43 handles orientation|screenSize|screenLayout|keyboardHidden|uiMode|density|fontScale but not smallestScreenSize, locale or layoutDirection, so split-screen, foldables and a system language change recreate the Activity. Saved screen state is keyed by entry id (Navigator.kt:81 's<id>'), which no longer exists after a restart.
- impact: When Android kills the app in the background (common when switching to the bank app to copy a code) or the window is resized, the user comes back to the home tab and loses the screen and the half-filled form they were on.
- fix: Short step: keep ShellState in AppContainer (it already outlives the Activity) and add the missing configChanges values. Later: make Route a serializable description (name + ids) and save/restore the stack and tab in onSaveInstanceState.
- second reviewer: MainActivity.kt:37 creates ShellState() in onCreate and ignores savedInstanceState; the only ShellState( construction in the codebase is there and no onSaveInstanceState exists. AndroidManifest.xml:43 lists orientation|screenSize|screenLayout|keyboardHidden|uiMode|density|fontScale, without smallestScreenSize, locale or layoutDirection, so split-screen and process death do reset to the home tab. Moving ShellState into AppContainer fixes recreation only; process death needs the saved stack, as the finding itself says.

### arch-7 [Lazy area graphs and one-time session side effects] - Whole use-case graph is rebuilt, with side effects, on every session emission
- severity: low | verdict: confirmed | urgency: later | effort: S | status: works_in_code
- where: `native-app/androidApp/src/main/kotlin/app/masroufy/android/AppContainer.kt:153`
- evidence: AppContainer.ready() (lines 147-162) runs inside flatMapLatest (line 100) and, for every Ready value, builds a new SpaceGraph, launches syncSharedMerchantsOf (155) and a usage snapshot (157-160). SpaceGraph.kt:23-38 eagerly constructs all ten area graphs (wiring imports 305 use-case symbols). New Ready values are produced on country switch and space refresh (firestore/AccountSession.kt:111 and 132). spacesOf() (line 179) rewraps every repository set on each call.
- impact: Each country switch pays the full construction cost and repeats a network sync and an analytics write; side effects inside a mapping step are easy to duplicate by accident as more is added there.
- fix: Make area graphs 'by lazy' in SpaceGraph, cache toRepositories() per FirestoreContainer, and move the two launches to a collector keyed by uid so they run once per sign-in.
- second reviewer: AppContainer.kt:147-162 builds a SpaceGraph and launches the shared-merchant sync and usage snapshot on every Ready value inside flatMapLatest (line 94-102), and SpaceGraph.kt:23-38 constructs all ten area graphs eagerly. Rebuilding per country switch is documented as intended at SpaceGraph.kt:13, so only the repeated side effects and eager construction are the real cost; spacesOf (line 178-179) does rewrap repositories per call.

### build-8 [Make App Check on by default for real builds once the owner finishes registration.] - App Check is a build-time switch that defaults to off and nothing ties it to the shipped variant
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: by_design
- where: `native-app/androidApp/build.gradle.kts:20`
- evidence: Line 20: appCheckOn is true only when -Pmasroufy.appCheck=true is passed. Line 80 swaps the source folder (src/appCheckOn vs src/appCheckOff, 30 vs 13 lines) and lines 136-139 add the Play Integrity dependency only in that case. FirebaseSetup.kt line 62 calls AppCheckSetup.install unconditionally, so the off build is a no-op. Recorded as pending owner steps (PLAN-0.6 AUTH-1, tools/security/APP-CHECK-STEPS.md).
- impact: Once the owner enables enforcement in the Firebase console, any build made without remembering the flag is locked out of Firestore for every user; and before enforcement, a forgotten flag silently ships with no App Check on a public-repo project whose API key is extractable from the APK. The setting lives in a command line, not in a file.
- fix: When the owner finishes the Play Integrity steps, flip the default: make owner-facing variants enable App Check unless -Pmasroufy.appCheck=false, expose the state as a BuildConfig field shown in the about/diagnostics screen, and print it in the build summary.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: Owner still has to complete the Play Integrity registration steps before this can be switched on.

### perf-10 [Make DataChanges carry which groups changed] - Any change anywhere reloads every visible screen in full (single global change counter)
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: later | effort: M | status: by_design
- where: `native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/app/MasroufyApp.kt:73`
- evidence: MasroufyApp.kt:73 turns every remote change into shell.changes.changed() after a settle delay. FirestoreSync.kt:84-90 bumps the remote counter when any group first syncs and on any server change in any group. HomeScreen.kt:79, HomeCardsData.kt:45 and OperationsScreen.kt:86 all key their full reload on changes.version, with no notion of which collection changed.
- impact: Changing an unrelated document (a setting, an alert receipt, a price) triggers the full Home reload of perf-1 and perf-3 and the Operations reload of perf-2. During the catch-up after opening from the device copy, groups arrive from the server over several seconds, so Home can reload several times in a row while the owner is already using it. The settle delay limits bursts but not the total.
- fix: Carry the changed group names with the counter and let each screen ignore groups it does not read; at minimum skip the bump for groups no number depends on (alert receipts, interactions).

### tests-11 [Make device SMS tests self-contained] - Device SMS tests depend on a helper script running at the same time and on the wall clock
- severity: low | verdict: unverified | urgency: later | effort: M | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/device/src/androidInstrumentedTest/kotlin/app/masroufy/device/SmsOnDeviceTest.kt:34`
- evidence: Line 34 `LocalDate.now(zone)` and the test then expects a message containing TEST-DEMAND that must be injected from outside (scripts/device/sendTestSms.sh, HANDOVER.md:46); inboxCatchesNewMessages polls up to 120 s then `delay(2_000)`. HANDOVER-ARCHIVE.md:3399 and :3896 both record first runs failing 2 tests for this reason. FirestoreSyncTest.kt:68-70 asserts an offline save returns in under 2,000 ms of wall time on an emulator.
- impact: Intermittent failures on a slow emulator teach sessions to rerun until green or to skip these tests, which weakens the only gate on SMS reading.
- fix: Have the test inject its own SMS through the instrumentation (adb emu via UiAutomation shell or a content-provider insert on the test device) instead of an external script; replace the 2 s wall-clock assertion with a generous timeout.

### arch-9 [Make repository delete methods abstract] - Repository ports ship a throwing default for delete
- severity: low | verdict: weakened | urgency: later | effort: S | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/port/LedgerPorts.kt:146`
- evidence: Nine port methods are declared as 'suspend fun delete(id): Unit = throw UnsupportedOperationException("delete")' (LedgerPorts.kt:146, DuesPorts.kt:21, IncomePorts.kt:15, ReferencePorts.kt:27,38,56,69,114, SavingsGoalPorts.kt:20). An adapter that forgets the override still compiles. A grep found 10 Firestore overrides and only 2 in the memory adapters shown, so tests on memory adapters and production can differ.
- impact: A delete that works in tests can crash on the phone (or the reverse) with no compile-time warning; this matters now that one delete sheet covers many entity kinds.
- fix: Make delete abstract in the ports (or split a small Deletable interface) so every adapter must implement it, and let the compiler list the gaps.
- second reviewer: The nine throwing defaults exist (LedgerPorts.kt:146, DuesPorts.kt:21, IncomePorts.kt:15, ReferencePorts.kt:27,38,56,69,114, SavingsGoalPorts.kt:20). The claim of only 2 memory overrides is wrong: a grep finds about 10 in app/memory (MemoryAssetRepositories.kt:27, MemoryDuesRepositories.kt:28,68, MemoryIncomeSources.kt:17, MemoryPeopleRepositories.kt:28, MemoryReferenceRepositories.kt:38,56, MemoryRepositories.kt:109, MemorySavingsGoals.kt:19, MemoryWalletRepository.kt:23) against 10 Firestore ones, so no current test/production gap is shown. It is a latent compile-safety hazard only.

### build-5 [Make the automatic check build the Android app and run the screen tests.] - CI never builds the Android app and skips the ui, wiring and firestore tests
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: partially_built
- where: `.github/workflows/native-core.yml:30`
- evidence: Line 30 runs only :core:jvmTest :app:jvmTest :data:jvmTest :device:jvmTest; lines 37 and 64 run iOS simulator tests and iOS compiles. No step runs :ui:jvmTest, :wiring:jvmTest, :firestore:jvmTest, any :androidApp:assemble* or any minify task. Contributing cause: ui/build.gradle.kts line 52 puts org.jetbrains.skiko:skiko-awt-runtime-windows-x64:0.144.6 on the jvmTest classpath, so the Compose-scene tests in ui/src/jvmTest (10 files reference ImageComposeScene/skiko; CharacterFromTopTest.kt line 28 gates on Windows) can only render on Windows while both CI jobs are macos-latest.
- impact: The largest module (ui, about 64k lines), the composition root (wiring) and the Android app itself are only ever compiled and tested on the owner's PC. A push that breaks the Android build or the R8 step stays green on GitHub; the next model learns about it only after a 6-12 minute local build.
- fix: Add a job (ubuntu or windows runner) that runs :ui:jvmTest :wiring:jvmTest :firestore:jvmTest and :androidApp:assembleEmulator plus :androidApp:minifyFastEmulatorWithR8 (no secrets needed). Select the skiko runtime artifact by host OS instead of hardcoding windows-x64, or run that job on windows-latest.

### sync-1 [Measure real daily reads for a 3,000-transaction account (opens + background cycles) and cut the background cycle's listeners.] - Whole-collection listeners plus a 4-hour background worker can exhaust the 50,000 reads/day Spark quota with a single heavy user
- severity: high (finder said critical) | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: L | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/firestore/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/firestore/FirestoreSync.kt:74`
- evidence: FirestoreSync.start launches one snapshot listener per group over the entire collection with no filter (line 70-74: space.collection(group).snapshots(includeMetadataChanges = true)). AccountSession.kt:155 and 212 start these for ACCOUNT_GROUPS and SPACE_GROUPS (57 listeners per the comment at DocQuery.kt:89). AppContainer.kt:209-213 starts the session in every background cycle, and BackgroundWork.kt:44,77 schedules that cycle every PERIOD_HOURS = 4 with only a battery constraint. AndroidSignOutCleanup.kt:19 wipes the cache on sign-out, so each sign-in re-downloads everything. ARCHITECTURE.md:293-297 required summary documents and 'every query bounded by a period', which this design does not follow.
- impact: Firestore bills a listener resumed after more than 30 minutes as a new query, i.e. every document. With about 7,000 docs per 3,000-transaction user: 6 background cycles x 7,000 = 42,000 plus 3 opens x 7,000 = 21,000 = about 63,000 reads/day for one user. The quota is project-wide, so once it is hit, every user gets read errors until the next day (and the app falls back to the stale device copy or fails to open on new devices).
- fix: Measure first in the Firebase console (reads per day vs. number of opens). Then reduce: (a) do not attach all 57 listeners in the background cycle, use targeted one-shot queries or cache-only reads there; (b) for the big collections (transactions, sourceRecords, notificationReceipts) listen with an updatedAt cursor (changes since the last synced server timestamp) on top of the persisted cache instead of the whole collection; (c) add a daily read budget meter in the problem log so the owner sees the number.
- second reviewer: Code path is as described: FirestoreSync.kt:70-74 attaches an unfiltered listener per group, AppContainer.kt:209-213 calls session.start() in every background cycle, and BackgroundWork.kt:44,77-78 runs it every 4 hours with only a battery constraint; ARCHITECTURE.md:293-297 rules (period summaries, bounded queries) are not followed and I found no owner decision in OVERRIDES.md accepting the read cost. Additional cause the finding missed: BackgroundWork.kt:66-70 (requestRun) starts the same cycle after every bank SMS, so a dead process is re-attached far more than 6 times a day. Lowered from critical only because the 63,000/day figure is an unmeasured estimate (7,000 docs, process dead before every cycle); the mechanism itself is real.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: OVERRIDES §54 chose the library's device copy as the local store; the decision to listen to every collection in full, including from the background worker, needs an explicit owner choice against the Spark quota.

### build-9 [Move all library versions into one shared file.] - No version catalog: the same versions are hardcoded in many build files
- severity: low | verdict: unverified | urgency: later | effort: S | status: partially_built
- where: `native-app/ui/build.gradle.kts:24`
- evidence: gradle/libs.versions.toml does not exist. Compose 1.11.1 is written in root build.gradle.kts line 10 and again in ui/build.gradle.kts line 24; kotlinx-coroutines 1.10.2 appears in app, data, device (3 times), firestore, ui, wiring and androidApp; icu4j 78.3 in core, app, data, wiring; GitLive 2.7.0 in firestore lines 36/38 and androidApp lines 112-113; compileSdk 36 / minSdk 24 in five modules; plugin versions are duplicated in firebase-trial/build.gradle.kts lines 3-7. androidApp line 130 pins firebase-crashlytics:20.1.0 explicitly although the BoM on line 129 already manages it.
- impact: An upgrade that misses one copy gives mixed versions (for example GitLive in firestore vs androidApp, or the Compose compiler plugin vs runtime), which shows up as confusing resolution or runtime errors. Several AI models editing in turns make a missed copy likely.
- fix: Introduce gradle/libs.versions.toml and reference it from every module; remove the explicit crashlytics version so the BoM decides.

### perf-1 [Move Home reads off the UI thread in ShellGraph and the home graph] - Home tab runs all its reads (including a full-history scan) on the UI thread
- severity: high | verdict: unverified | urgency: now | effort: S | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/screens/home/HomeScreen.kt:79`
- evidence: LaunchedEffect(deps, tick, changes.version) at HomeScreen.kt:79-91 calls shell.me(), loadHome(deps), readingOn(), historyReport() with no dispatcher switch. loadHome (lines 123-138) calls deps.shell.withYouNow(), home.profile.load(), home.homeScreen.load(), home.calendar.summary(), home.alerts.inbox(). wiring/ShellGraph.kt:91 is `override suspend fun withYouNow() = withYou.load(env.today())` with no withContext, and LoadWithYouNow.kt:40-42 reads every transaction from the earliest wallet opening date to today. LoadHomeScreen.load (LoadHomeScreen.kt:119) and LoadCalendar also have no withContext. The read itself is firestore/DocQuery.kt:44-52 (filter over the whole collection, then sortedWith) followed by a second sort in FirestoreTransactionRepository.kt:21. By contrast OperationsGraph.kt:47/83 passes work = c.env.work, so the pattern exists but was not applied to Home.
- impact: At 3,000 transactions each Home load does on the UI thread: 1 full scan + 2 sorts of all 3,000 rows (with-you-now), 1-2 period scans, plus a dozen small list reads. Estimate: 50-150 ms warm (3-9 dropped frames, visible as a stutter of the pull indicator and tab switch); on the first load after start, 3,000 documents are also decoded to entities on that thread (LocalMirror.Entry.entity), estimate 300-600 ms frozen. It re-runs on every save and every remote change.
- fix: Wrap the Home use cases in withContext(c.env.work) inside the wiring graphs (ShellGraph.withYouNow/me, the home graph's homeScreen/calendar/alerts/profile), exactly as OperationsGraph does, so the screen code stays unchanged. Keep the screen showing old numbers until new ones arrive (it already does).

### arch-1 [Move use-case execution off the UI thread at the wiring boundary for all areas] - Use cases run on the UI thread by default; off-thread work is opt-in per screen
- severity: high | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/screens/home/HomeScreen.kt:79`
- evidence: HomeScreen.kt:79-82 calls loadHome(deps) inside LaunchedEffect (UI dispatcher); loadHome (lines 123-137) chains withYouNow, profile.load, homeScreen.load, calendar.summary and alerts.inbox with no withContext. wiring/HomeGraph.kt, PeopleGraph.kt, DuesGraph.kt and ShellGraph.kt contain no dispatcher switch (grep empty). Counts: only 6 of 220 files in app/usecase use withContext; 14 of 155 ui files with LaunchedEffect use withContext. PeopleScreen.kt:96-99 is the same pattern. SmsGraph.kt:51-52 documents that this exact pattern froze the whole app on a phone with thousands of messages, and HANDOVER.md:82 records the full backup running on the main thread.
- impact: As the ledger grows (years of transactions, two countries), home, people and dues loads compute on the UI thread: stutter, frozen taps and possible 'app not responding' dialogs. Every new feature has to remember to opt out by hand, so the problem returns with each slice.
- fix: Make off-thread the default at one boundary instead of per screen: have each <Area>Graph (or AreaContext) wrap suspend use-case calls in withContext(env.work), the way SmsGraph.kt:76 already does with its private work() helper; DeviceEnv.work already exists (Env.kt:54). Then delete the scattered withContext calls in ui.
- second reviewer: HomeScreen.kt:79-92 runs loadHome (123-138) inside LaunchedEffect with no dispatcher switch; ShellGraph.kt:91 (withYouNow) and HomeGraph.kt:26,34 (LoadCalendar, LoadHomeScreen) pass no work context, and firestore/src main code contains no withContext or Dispatchers at all, so the computation resumes on the UI thread. Only a few use cases are opted out (OperationsGraph.kt:47,83, BudgetsGraph.kt:61,67, SmsGraph.kt:76), and PLAN-0.6.md:285 records the owner's real-phone hang on refresh, so the proposed wiring-boundary fix addresses the right cause. The reviewer's counts hold (6 app files, 14 ui files with withContext).

### errors-3 [Opening screen: timeout, offline message, retry and sign-out] - First open can wait forever: no timeout, no error, no retry on the opening screen
- severity: medium (finder said high) | verdict: weakened | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/firestore/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/firestore/AccountSession.kt:171`
- evidence: For a device that never completed a first sync, upTo() runs the block with no timeout (line 171: `if (!seenBefore) return block()`), and the block is accountSync.awaitComplete() = syncedGroups.first { it.containsAll(groups) } (FirestoreSync.kt:105-107). When a listener fails, the catch at FirestoreSync.kt:94-99 calls mirror.markStale(group), which removes the group from `synced` (LocalMirror.kt:71-74), so awaitComplete can never finish. While offline, snapshots arrive from cache and are never marked synced either. The UI for AppSession.Starting/Opening is OpeningScreen (MasroufyApp.kt:103-105, 119-137): a loader, a title and a progress bar only - no error text, no offline hint, no retry, no sign-out.
- impact: A new user (or a reinstall, or a second phone) with weak or no internet, or with one failing listener, stays on the loading screen indefinitely and cannot do anything, not even sign out. For store reviewers and first-time testers this looks like a frozen app.
- fix: Expose listener failures and elapsed time in AppSession.Opening; after N seconds or on a failure show 'no connection / could not load' with Retry and Sign out. Restart failed listeners on retry.
- second reviewer: Confirmed that AccountSession.kt:171 runs awaitComplete with no timeout on a never-synced device, that a failed listener removes the group from synced for good (FirestoreSync.kt:94-99, LocalMirror.kt:71-74), and that OpeningScreen (ui/app/MasroufyApp.kt:119-137) plus AppSession.Opening (AppContainer.kt:97-98) carry only counts: no message, retry or sign-out. Weakened because sign-in itself needs a connection and offline listeners resume on their own when the network returns, so 'forever' only holds for a failed listener; the plain offline case is a long wait with no explanation. The fix must also restart failed listeners, since nothing does today.

### countries-5 [Per-space today in the background cycle] - Background cycle uses the phone's date for every country
- severity: low | verdict: weakened | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/androidApp/src/main/kotlin/app/masroufy/android/AppContainer.kt:187`
- evidence: envFor(uid, space) at line 187 sets today = todayInCountry(...) only when a space is passed, otherwise MasroufyBackground.localNow().date (the device clock). backgroundCycle() at line 219 calls envFor(ready.user.uid) with no space and hands that single env to every country's lane (wiring/BackgroundWiring.kt:67, smsLaneOf(space, r, env, inbox)). The comment at line 186 confirms the background was left as it was.
- impact: With the phone on Riyadh time, at 00:30 on 2026-11-28 the Egypt lane runs with today = 28 while Cairo is still on the 27th (23:30). If the phone is on a third time zone (travel), both countries are off. The SMS day itself is safe, because it comes from the lane time zone (OperationTime.kt:29-33, SmsArrivalDay.kt:77). Which background steps actually read env.today was not traced.
- fix: Build one DeviceEnv per space in backgroundCycle (envFor(uid, space)) or pass a per-space today into smsLaneOf.
- second reviewer: AppContainer.kt:187,219 does give the background a device-date env, but nothing in the background path reads it: a grep for env.today across wiring finds no use in BackgroundWiring.kt (lines 38-90 use env.clock, env.ids, env.work only), and the SMS day comes from pack.timeZone at BackgroundWiring.kt:103-104. So this is latent with no current symptom; it becomes real only when the alert engine (candidates = null at line 78) is wired in.

### sec-firebase-10 [Pin actions in native-core.yml to commit SHAs] - native-core workflow uses actions by moving tag, not commit hash
- severity: low | verdict: confirmed | urgency: later | effort: S | status: partially_built
