- second reviewer: .github/workflows/native-core.yml:24-25 and :80-81 use actions/checkout@v5 and actions/setup-java@v5 by tag, while prices.yml:37 and :42 pin commit hashes. native-core.yml:13-14 is contents: read with no secrets and push/dispatch triggers only, so the impact is as limited as the finding says.

### release-2 [Prepare SMS permissions declaration and a no-SMS fallback flavor] - READ_SMS and RECEIVE_SMS need Play's Permissions Declaration; no fallback build exists if it is rejected
- severity: high | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: partially_built
- where: `native-app/device/src/androidMain/AndroidManifest.xml:4`
- evidence: Lines 4-5 declare READ_SMS and RECEIVE_SMS; lines 9-11 register BankSmsReceiver for SMS_RECEIVED. These sit in the :device library manifest, so every variant of the app merges them. Google's policy page (support.google.com answer 10208820, fetched today) lists the exception 'SMS-based money management' ('apps that track and manage budget') with eligible permissions READ_SMS, RECEIVE_MMS, RECEIVE_SMS, RECEIVE_WAP_PUSH, 'subject to Google Play review and approval', and adds that budgeting apps 'may not exfiltrate or share non-financial or personal SMS history'. The app already has a paste path (BankSmsSettingsParts.kt:97) and statement import.
- impact: The use case is allowed, but approval is a manual Google review that often bounces on the first try. With the permissions hard-wired into the only manifest, a rejection blocks the whole release rather than just the auto-read feature.
- fix: Prepare the declaration: core-feature description, a short demo recording of the permission flow, and proof that only enabled bank senders are read (BankSmsReceiver.kt:37-41 is good evidence). Move the two permissions and the receiver into a build flavor or source set so a 'no SMS' variant (paste plus statement import) can ship if Google says no. A notification-listener alternative is technically possible but carries its own Play sensitivity; treat it as a later option only.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: If Google rejects SMS reading, do we ship a paste-and-statement-only version or keep distributing outside the store?

### sms-4 [Prepare the Play restricted-SMS-permission declaration and disclosure before store submission] - No plan recorded for Google Play's restricted SMS permission declaration
- severity: low (finder said medium) | verdict: weakened | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: not_built
- where: `native-app/device/src/androidMain/AndroidManifest.xml:4`
- evidence: The device module declares READ_SMS and RECEIVE_SMS (lines 4-5) and a manifest SMS_RECEIVED receiver (lines 9-11). Google Play treats these as restricted permissions that need an approved Permissions Declaration (the app is not the default SMS handler, so it must qualify under an exception such as SMS-based money management, with a demo video and matching privacy policy). A grep of OVERRIDES.md, HANDOVER.md, PLAN-0.6.md, KOTLIN_PLAN.md and ARCHITECTURE.md for 'Declaration', the Arabic equivalents and Play SMS policy returned only the permission decisions themselves (OVERRIDES.md:273-282), no store-submission plan.
- impact: The store submission can be rejected or the app removed, and the core feature (automatic reading) is exactly the part under review. Finding this late could delay the release by weeks.
- fix: Add a release task: prepare the Permissions Declaration (use case, video of the in-app disclosure and consent screen, privacy policy text covering what is read, what is uploaded and what is dropped), and verify the in-app prominent disclosure appears before the system dialog. Keep the paste flow as the fallback if the declaration is refused.
- second reviewer: The claim that nothing is recorded is wrong: KOTLIN_PLAN.md:83 notes the Play 'SMS-based money management' exception with declaration form and human review (confirmed 2026-09-19), and OVERRIDES.md:2520 and PLAN-0.6.md:543 note the data-safety page and privacy-policy line. What is missing is only a concrete release task (declaration text, video, disclosure check). I did not open the manifest itself.

### sec-firebase-7 [Re-authenticate before erasing in delete-account] - Account deletion wipes cloud data before confirming the login account can be deleted
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: by_design
- where: `native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/usecase/SignIn.kt:56`
- evidence: SignIn.kt:55-60 calls erase.eraseAll(uid) first; only then line 63 auth.deleteCurrentUser(). If Firebase answers requires-recent-login (lines 64-68) the user is signed out and gets NeedsRecentLogin: data is already gone but the login account (email) still exists until he signs in again and presses delete a second time. No re-authentication step exists in the firestore module (no reauthenticate call found in native-app/*/src).
- impact: A user who signed in days ago (the normal case) will always hit this path: everything is erased, he is thrown out, and the account with his email remains unless he comes back and repeats. If he does not, the store promise 'account deleted' is not met, and he sees a confusing half-finished result of an irreversible action.
- fix: Ask for fresh sign-in first (re-enter password or Google again), then erase data and delete the login account in one go; or at least check the last sign-in time before erasing and send the user to re-login before anything is deleted.
- second reviewer: usecase/SignIn.kt:56 erases server data first, :63 then deletes the login, and :64-68 signs out and returns NeedsRecentLogin with the data already gone; no re-authentication call exists (only AccountDeleteRow.kt:71 shows a 'sign in again' line). The order is documented as intended at SignIn.kt:48-50 and OVERRIDES.md:2721, which also says it was never tried on Firebase or the emulator. The root cause and fix (re-authenticate before erasing) are correct; the second attempt does complete the deletion, so the residue is a leftover empty login if the user does not return.

### tests-7 [Re-enable release lint with a baseline; add minimal detekt or guard tests] - No static analysis at all, and Android lint is switched off for release builds
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: not_built
- where: `native-app/androidApp/build.gradle.kts:98`
- evidence: Line 98: `lint { checkReleaseBuilds = false }` (comment at 97 says release lint gave false errors with the Kotlin version). grep for detekt|ktlint|spotless|kover|jacoco in native-app/build.gradle.kts, all module build files and settings.gradle.kts returns only this lint line. No coverage tool. The 300-line rule is checked by hand: 4 Kotlin main files are over 300 lines by wc.
- impact: Before Google Play, lint is the tool that flags manifest, permission, exported-component and API-level mistakes; with it off and no detekt, such issues are found by the store or by users. No coverage number exists to back the claim of where tests are thin.
- fix: Run `:androidApp:lintFastRelease` (or the shipped variant) at least as a report with a baseline file, and fix or baseline the false positives instead of disabling. Add detekt with a minimal rule set (or a simple test like HardcodedColorBudgetTest for the 300-line rule). Record the reason in ARCHITECTURE.md as rule 8 requires.

### sms-6 [Real-phone SMS test pass (live, scan, reinstall, manual-then-SMS merge and undo) and add real masked messages to golden] - Real-device and real-message behaviour of the SMS path is untested, including merge with a hand-entered transaction
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: partially_built
- where: `HANDOVER.md:91`
- evidence: HANDOVER.md:91 states nothing was tried on a real phone and real bank messages were not exercised; line 230 lists 'transfer to your other account (manual then message, and the reverse)' as not tried; line 265 notes that undoing an SMS batch after it merged into a manual transfer does not restore the manual one. In code, an SMS that matches an existing transaction of the same day, amount and direction (including a hand-entered one) only becomes SIMILAR and waits for the owner (core/.../Dedupe.kt:90-97); the merge itself happens only on the owner's choice (ReviewSmsInbox.kt:186-193). The golden files (golden/sms.json with 428 message bodies, golden/smsFlow.json with 29) pin the old app's parser output, and the many round tests use invented adversarial texts. None of them covers: provider timestamp differences, restored messages on a new phone, a forged sender, two phones, or a batch that fails halfway on real Firestore.
- impact: The first real users are the first real test of the feature that touches money most often. Template drift at a bank, or a provider quirk, would show up as waiting messages at best and duplicates at worst.
- fix: Before a wider release run a scripted on-phone pass with real (owner-supplied, masked) messages per supported bank: live receive, scan after the app was closed, reinstall, manual-entry-then-SMS merge and its undo. Add the masked real messages to the golden set.
- second reviewer: HANDOVER.md:91 says nothing was tried on a real phone or with real bank messages, and HANDOVER.md:230 lists the transfer to own account (manual then SMS and the reverse) as not tried. No test references date_sent or timestampMillis (grep shows only BankSmsReceiver.kt:44 and AndroidSmsInbox.kt:143). I did not verify line 265 or the golden-file counts.

### countries-10 [Remove silent Saudi fallback for unknown country codes in SMS and time paths] - Unknown country code silently falls back to the Saudi pack
- severity: low | verdict: confirmed | urgency: later | effort: S | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/CountryPack.kt:109`
- evidence: countryPack(code) returns DEFAULT_COUNTRY_PACK (Saudi) for any code not in COUNTRY_PACKS (lines 109-110). Callers use it for the time zone (CountryTime.kt:50, AppContainer.kt:191) and for the SMS reader (BackgroundWiring.kt:101-104 smsLaneOf). Creation is guarded (Spaces.kt:98 newSpaceFor rejects unknown codes), but activeSpaceOf (Spaces.kt:114-117) accepts any registry entry.
- impact: Not reachable today with only SA and EG. If a newer app version or a restored backup puts a third country in the registry (say AE) and this version opens it, the space gets the Saudi SMS reader and Riyadh time while its currency is AED. Saudi SAR messages would then be parsed as OK in two lanes. Whether that leads to double recording depends on the wallet mapping, which I did not trace.
- fix: Make smsLaneOf and the time helpers use COUNTRY_PACKS[code] with no fallback (no pack means no lane, and show 'country not supported in this version').
- second reviewer: CountryPack.kt:109-110 falls back to the Saudi pack for any unknown code, and BackgroundWiring.kt:100-104 (smsLaneOf) and CountryTime.kt:50 use it without checking; Spaces.kt:114-117 (activeSpaceOf) accepts any non-archived registry entry while only Spaces.kt:96-98 (newSpaceFor) rejects unknown codes. Not reachable with the current SA and EG packs, so it is forward-compatibility hardening; the double-recording consequence was not demonstrated.

### countries-1 [Replace UTC take(10) 'today' with country day in app use cases] - Use cases take 'today' from the UTC clock instead of the country day
- severity: low (finder said medium) | verdict: weakened | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/usecase/TransferPartyAnswers.kt:101`
- evidence: TransferPartyAnswers.kt:101 creates the debt with today = deps.clock.nowIso().take(10), the first 10 characters of a UTC timestamp. ManageProjects.kt:131-133 does the same and then calls listByDateRange(from, today). A grep shows the same pattern, which I did not open, in PayerKindAnswers.kt:229, RecentCategories.kt:17, PersonPage.kt:140, ManageAssets.kt:224, CalculateInheritance.kt:71, SeedAccount.kt:33, ManageProfile.kt:46 and EditTransaction.kt:132. The correct helper exists (core/CountryTime.kt:50 todayInCountry) and is used by ManageWallets.kt:86 and UpdateBalance.kt:58.
- impact: Between 00:00 and 03:00 Riyadh time (00:00-02:00 Cairo in winter, 00:00-03:00 in summer) these paths see yesterday. Example: on 2026-11-28 at 02:15 Riyadh (UTC 2026-11-27 23:15) the owner marks a transfer as a loan to a person; the debt is dated 2026-11-27, one day early, and with payday 28 that is the previous fiscal month. In ManageProjects the 'old operations' candidate list excludes operations dated on the real current day during the same window.
- fix: Inject a country-aware today (the same env.today / todayInCountry already wired for the screens) into these use cases and remove every clock.nowIso().take(10) used as a calendar day. Add a test at 21:30Z for both zones.
- second reviewer: The headline example is wrong: TransferPartyAnswers.kt:101 passes today with DebtMoney.Operation and due = NONE, and AddDebt.kt:104-105,121-126 only uses input.today for the due date and the Cash branch (line 128), so the debt takes its date from the linked operation and no fiscal-month shift occurs. The UTC day is real (Env.kt:112-115 nowIso is UTC 'Z') and does affect ManageProjects.kt:131-133 (old-candidates range ends one day early between 00:00 and 03:00 Riyadh) and the unopened sites such as ManageAssets.kt:224 and SeedAccount.kt:33, so the cleanup is still worth doing but as a low-severity display/range issue; each remaining site needs its own check.

### perf-4 [Replace withYouNow() with a plain wallet list in the 3 screens] - Screens recompute the balance over the whole history just to get wallet names or opening dates
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/screens/home/CashDetailsSheet.kt:67`
- evidence: CashDetailsSheet.kt:67 `deps.shell.withYouNow().wallets.associate { it.wallet.id to it.wallet.name }` (names only); PeriodPickerScreen.kt:72 `deps.shell.withYouNow().wallets.minOfOrNull { it.wallet.openingAt }` (a date only); SavingsGoalsScreen.kt:75 `space.shell.withYouNow().wallets.map { it.wallet }` (wallet list only). Each call runs LoadWithYouNow.load (LoadWithYouNow.kt:37-50): all transactions since the first wallet opened, sorted twice, then walletBalancesOn. ShellGraph.kt:91 has no dispatcher switch, so this is on the UI thread.
- impact: Opening the month picker, the cash sheet or the savings goals costs a full 3,000-row scan + two sorts on the UI thread (estimate 10-40 ms each, more as history grows) for data that is a handful of wallet documents. Shows as a small hitch when the sheet opens.
- fix: Expose a cheap wallets() on the shell deps (OperationsGraph.wallets() at OperationsGraph.kt:145 already exists and is off-thread) and use it in these three places.

### errors-5 [Report the real exception for each background step] - Background cycle swallows nine steps and drops the SMS exception: failures are invisible
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: now | effort: S | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/usecase/RunBackgroundCycle.kt:149`
- evidence: runCatchingNotCancel (lines 149-155) turns any Exception into null with no Problems.report; it wraps catch-up before/after, SMS state notices, four kinds of repairs, support flush, duplicate scans and subscription catch-up (lines 85-108). The SMS step's catch (line 91) discards the exception object and only sets smsFailed; the worker then reports SMS_AUTO_RECORD_FAILED with a null error (device/.../BackgroundWork.kt:112), so neither the problem log nor crash reporting ever learns the exception type. subscriptionsFailed is returned (line 108) but the worker's `failed` computation (BackgroundWork.kt:116) ignores it.
- impact: When auto-recording or a repair fails on a tester's phone there is nothing to diagnose it with: the log says 'failed' with no cause, and repair/subscription failures leave no trace at all, so the same failure can repeat every cycle unnoticed.
- fix: Carry the Throwable in BackgroundCycleResult (or call Problems.report inside the catch with a per-step ProblemCode); include subscriptionsFailed in the worker's failure decision.
- second reviewer: RunBackgroundCycle.kt:149-155 turns any Exception into null with no report, line 91 drops the SMS exception object, BackgroundWork.kt:112 reports SMS_AUTO_RECORD_FAILED with a null error, and BackgroundWork.kt:116 leaves subscriptionsFailed out of 'failed'. The only step in app/commonMain that reports its own failure is SharedMerchantsLink.kt:37,56; the repairs, duplicate scans and subscription catch-up leave no trace.

### sms-2 [Require matching last-4 digits (when the wallet has them) before auto-recording a bank SMS] - Forged SMS with a bank sender name can create a silent automatic transaction
- severity: low (finder said medium) | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: by_design
- where: `native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/usecase/SmsWaitReasons.kt:55`
- evidence: The only sender check is a case-insensitive string match on the address (device/.../SmsInboxStore.kt:42, BankSmsReceiver.kt:41). Anyone can send a text with an alphanumeric sender name. If the text follows a template the owner already confirmed for that sender, nothing else stops it: waitReasonOf (SmsWaitReasons.kt:55-65) makes a message wait only when its digits belong to ANOTHER wallet of the owner, for refunds, cash and card credits; a purchase whose card digits match no wallet at all is not held. routeSender falls back to the sender's wallet (SmsRouting.kt:104) and the background run records it with no notification (RunBackgroundCycle.kt:20, AutoRecordSms.kt:180). A grep of the code and of OVERRIDES/HANDOVER/ARCHITECTURE for spoofing found no recorded decision. Mitigations that do exist: the receiver requires BROADCAST_SMS (device manifest line 9), the template whitelist plus learn mode, the doubt gate (SmsShapeGate.kt:115-122), and an SMS cannot set a balance directly (SmsRow.toParsedRow passes no stated balance, BankSmsModel.kt:218).
- impact: A fake message (or a scam text imitating the bank) can add a transaction that never happened and shift the wallet balance and budget without the owner being told. No real money moves, but the app's numbers become wrong and trust is lost.
- fix: When the wallet has last-4 digits saved, require the card or account digits in the message to match that wallet (or a card the owner linked to it) before auto-recording; otherwise leave it waiting with a clear reason. Consider a daily 'recorded automatically' digest so silent entries are visible, and hold unusually large amounts for confirmation.
- second reviewer: SmsWaitReasons.kt:55-65 holds unknown digits only for transfer kinds (smsLegVerdict returns Keep for anything outside LEG_KINDS, SmsAccountLegs.kt:33), and routeSender falls back to the sender's wallet (SmsRouting.kt:104), so a purchase with foreign card digits is auto-recorded silently. Sender check is a string match only (SmsInboxStore.kt:42); no spoofing decision found in OVERRIDES/HANDOVER/ARCHITECTURE. Lowered to low: the forger must reproduce an already-learned template exactly and gains nothing but wrong numbers.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: Should a message whose card or account digits do not match the wallet wait for your confirmation instead of being recorded automatically?

### arch-6 [Reset session-scoped UI singletons on sign-out and stop reading global Texts state from use cases] - Global mutable singletons act as hidden dependencies and are never reset between accounts
- severity: low (finder said medium) | verdict: weakened | urgency: later | effort: M | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/Texts.kt:95`
- evidence: Texts (core/Texts.kt:95-104) is an object with plain vars language and arabicVariant. It is written from five places in four layers: firestore/AccountSession.kt:112,135,151,187 (background scope), app/usecase/ManageSpaces.kt:50,67, ui/screens/onboarding/OnboardingState.kt:133 and androidApp/AppContainer.kt:150; it is neither volatile nor Compose-observable. The background cycle is built without a space (AppContainer.kt:219, envFor(uid)) so text for a non-active country uses the active country's dialect. UI singletons PeriodChoice (ui/screens/home/HomeSession.kt:24-39, keyed only by space id), SmsHandoff (ui/screens/imports/SmsHandoff.kt:41) and ImportDrafts (ui/screens/imports/ImportDrafts.kt:24, holds CSV text) have no reset; the only reset on leaving a session is shell.navigator.reset() in ui/app/MasroufyApp.kt:71. AppContainer.kt:179 shows the default space id is one constant for every account.
- impact: Behaviour depends on call order rather than on what a class receives, which is exactly what tests cannot see. A second person signing in on the same phone without closing the app inherits the previous person's chosen month and pending import drafts; wording can flip dialect mid-screen.
- fix: Pass a small session context (dialect, language, privacy) through AreaContext instead of reading objects; give ui singletons a clear() called where MasroufyApp.kt:71 resets the navigator, and key them by uid plus space id.
- second reviewer: Texts.kt:95-104 is a plain mutable object and PeriodChoice (HomeSession.kt:24-38) is keyed by space id only with no reset; MasroufyApp.kt:71 resets only the navigator, and sign-out does not restart the process (only CrashReports.kt:87 kills it). But ImportDrafts (ImportDrafts.kt:24-37) is reachable only through a route id that the navigator reset discards, so drafts are not actually inherited, and the remaining leak is a chosen month and handoff card state for a second account on the same phone without closing the app. I did not verify the five Texts write sites.

### sync-6 [Reset the first-sync mark when the local Firestore copy is wiped.] - The 'device has a full copy' mark survives sign-out, so a wiped or partial cache is trusted as complete after re-login
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/androidApp/src/main/kotlin/app/masroufy/android/DeviceStores.kt:54`
- evidence: AndroidFirstSyncMarks stores 'done|uid' in SharedPreferences 'first-sync' with only completed/markCompleted (lines 51-59); its comment says it is cleared only by uninstall. Sign-out clears the Firestore cache (FirebaseSetup.kt:31-38) but nothing clears the mark (grep for first-sync/FirstSyncMarks in androidApp and device found only DeviceStores.kt and AppContainer.kt:84). On the next sign-in AccountSession.kt:159-164 reads seenBefore = true, calls trustDeviceCopy(), and waits at most OFFLINE_GRACE_MS = 500 ms (line 236); LocalMirror.docsOf then serves any group whose listener delivered a cache snapshot as if it were complete (LocalMirror.kt:83).
- impact: After sign-out and sign-in on a slow or dropping connection, the app can open on a partially downloaded copy and compute balances and totals from it without any message, which is exactly what rule 10 and the comment at AccountSession.kt:43 say must not happen. Background SMS processing in that window can also run duplicate checks against incomplete source records.
- fix: Clear the mark for the uid in the same place the cache is wiped (and whenever clearPersistence runs), so the next sign-in waits for the full first download again.
- second reviewer: DeviceStores.kt:51-59 has only completed/markCompleted and nothing clears the 'first-sync' prefs (grep finds no other reference; AndroidSignOutCleanup.kt:12-20 does not touch it), while FirebaseSetup.kt:31-38 wipes the cache; on re-login AccountSession.kt:159-164 trusts the device copy and LocalMirror.kt:83 serves any group whose listener delivered a cache snapshot. Nuance on frequency: with an empty cache the SDK normally holds the first snapshot until the server answers, so the wrong (empty or partial) copy is served only when the SDK flips to offline after the wipe (connection lost or timing out right after sign-in), which makes it rarer than 'slow connection' but the result is zeros shown as real, a rule-10 violation.

### errors-6 [Retry the SMS worker when the session was not ready] - Worker returns success without retry when the session is not ready or building the cycle throws
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/device/src/androidMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/device/BackgroundWork.kt:103`
- evidence: doWork(): if graph().cycle() returns null or throws, the Elvis at line 103 returns Result.success(). AppContainer.backgroundCycle() (androidApp/.../AppContainer.kt:209-218) returns null when the session is not Ready within 30 s (it reports BACKGROUND_GAVE_UP) - a signed-in user on a slow start. The retry branch (line 118) is only reachable after a cycle actually ran. The periodic safety net runs every 4 hours and only when battery is not low (lines 77-78).
- impact: A bank SMS that arrives while the app is closed and the session is slow to open is not recorded until the next periodic run (up to about 4 hours, longer on low battery) or until the user opens the app. The message itself is not lost (it stays in the device inbox).
- fix: Return Result.retry() (bounded by MAX_ATTEMPTS) when a user is signed in but the session was not ready or cycle() threw; keep Result.success() only for signed-out/no graph.
- second reviewer: BackgroundWork.kt:96-103 returns Result.success() when cycle() is null or throws, and AppContainer.kt:213-217 returns null after 30 s without Ready (reporting BACKGROUND_GAVE_UP); the retry at line 118 is only reachable after a cycle ran. The comment at AppContainer.kt:207 shows this is a deliberate 'the periodic run will catch up' choice, and on a previously synced device opening is bounded by the short grace, so the case should be uncommon; the message is delayed, not lost. A bounded retry for a signed-in user would remove the delay.

### sync-9 [Reuse one server read between restore preview and apply.] - Backup and restore each read the whole account from the server two or three times
- severity: low (finder said medium) | verdict: confirmed | urgency: later | effort: M | status: works_in_code
- where: `native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/usecase/FullBackup.kt:237`
- evidence: createNow calls port.read() (line 188); planNow calls port.read() (line 208); applyNow calls port.read() again (line 237). FirestoreFullBackup.read pages every backup group from Source.SERVER (FirestoreFullBackup.kt:35-49), and addMissing does one transactional get per incoming row (lines 56-58).
- impact: For a 7,000-document account: one backup = 7,000 reads; one restore = 7,000 (preview) + 7,000 (apply) + one get per row to add. A user who makes a backup, previews and restores on the same day spends over 21,000 of the shared 50,000 daily reads. It is correct, but expensive on the free plan.
- fix: For the preview and the apply-time merge, read the existing data from the synced mirror when every group is marked synced from the server, and keep Source.SERVER only for creating the backup file; or cache the preview's read for the apply step when it happens within a few minutes.
- second reviewer: FullBackup.kt:188, 208 and 237 each call port.read(), and FirestoreFullBackup.kt:35-49 pages every group with Source.SERVER, plus one transactional get per row to add at lines 56-58; so one backup is one full read and one restore is two full reads plus the added rows. Lowered to low: the server-only read is a deliberate documented choice (FirestoreFullBackup.kt:18-19, never return an incomplete device copy silently) and the apply-time re-read is intentional (FullBackup.kt:232), and backup/restore is a rare manual action; reusing the preview read for apply is a safe saving, but reading from the mirror would need the same completeness guarantee.

### perf-2 [Run payerQuestions off the UI thread and reuse the zone() read] - Operations tab: salary-payer question scan loads every transaction on the UI thread after each load and pull-to-refresh
- severity: high | verdict: unverified | urgency: now | effort: S | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/screens/operations/OperationsScreen.kt:101`
- evidence: OperationsScreen.kt:101 `val asks = attempt { space.more.incomeSignals.payerQuestions().size }` runs inside the LaunchedEffect (UI dispatcher). IncomeSourceSignals.kt:54 is `everything() = deps.txns.listByDateRange(EARLIEST, LATEST)` and line 60 `payerQuestions() = core.payerQuestions(everything(), deps.sources.listAll(), skipParties())`; a grep of that file for withContext returns nothing (unlike ManageTransfers.zone() at ManageTransfers.kt:82 and LoadTransactionsScreen.load at :83, which do switch). The effect is keyed on reload, chosenKey and changes.version, so it runs after every pull-to-refresh (line 121 reload++), every bulk action and every save.
- impact: At 3,000 transactions: a full scan + two sorts of 3,000 rows plus the payer analysis over all of them, on the UI thread, right after the list appears. Estimate 30-120 ms per run; it lands exactly when the owner is starting to scroll after a refresh, so it reads as the list 'sticking'. This is the remaining UI-thread piece of the pull-to-refresh complaint (the SMS part was already moved off at line 117-118).
- fix: Give IncomeSourceSignals a work context (same pattern as ManageTransfersDeps.work) and wrap payerQuestions in withContext; or compute it from the list ManageTransfers.zone() already loaded so the whole history is read once, not twice.

### sec-firebase-5 [Run secretscan automatically on push and widen its patterns] - Secret scan is manual only and misses unquoted property-file passwords
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: partially_built
- where: `scripts/secretscan.mjs:22`
- evidence: scripts/secretscan.mjs:12 runs only when someone invokes it with two git refs; neither workflow calls it (.github/workflows/prices.yml, native-core.yml) and no hook file is tracked (git ls-files shows none). Line 22 password pattern requires the value in quotes, so storePassword=xxx in a .properties file passes. Lines 34-40 forbidden names do not include service-account/adminsdk JSON or *.properties (a service account is caught only via the private_key text pattern on line 19).
- impact: The repo is public and several AI sessions commit to it in turn. The only protection against a key or signing password being pushed is each session remembering to run the script. One forgotten run publishes the secret permanently in history.
- fix: Add a tiny read-only workflow job (or tracked pre-push hook via core.hooksPath) that runs secretscan on every push; add patterns for unquoted password/secret lines in .properties and names like *adminsdk*.json, *service-account*.json, keystore.properties.
- second reviewer: scripts/secretscan.mjs:12 runs only on manual invocation with two refs; a search of all yml, json, sh, ps1 and bat files found no reference to secretscan or hooksPath, and git ls-files shows only the two workflows and no hook files. Line 22 requires the value in quotes, so an unquoted storePassword=... line passes, and lines 34-40 do not list *.properties or service-account names. Keystore files themselves are blocked by name (line 38).

### errors-7 [Save navigation stack and form fields across process death] - Process death loses navigation and half-filled forms
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: later | effort: L | status: partially_built
- where: `native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/nav/Navigator.kt:46`
- evidence: Navigator keeps the screen stack and open sheets in plain mutableStateListOf (lines 50-51) with no Saver, and it belongs to a shell object created in MainActivity.onCreate (androidApp/.../MainActivity.kt:44, 68), not to saved instance state. AddOperationSheet holds amount, category, wallets and date in `remember`, not rememberSaveable (ui/.../shell/AddOperationSheet.kt:81-90). rememberSaveable appears in about 39 files only. Rotation and theme changes are safe because the manifest handles them itself (androidApp/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:43 configChanges).
- impact: When Android kills the app in the background (typical on low-memory phones when the user switches to the bank app or the SMS app to check a number) the user comes back to the home tab and whatever he was typing is gone. Onboarding and the character builder are protected by their own drafts; ordinary forms are not.
- fix: Give Navigator a Saver for tab + route stack (routes need to be serialisable), and switch the fields of the add-operation, debt and wallet forms to rememberSaveable.
- second reviewer: Navigator.kt:46-52 keeps tab, stack and sheets in plain state with no Saver (no Saver anywhere under ui/nav; the rememberSaveable at Navigator.kt:20 covers per-entry screen state only, which is lost once the stack is). AddOperationSheet.kt:81-90 uses remember for all fields, and AndroidManifest.xml:43 confirms rotation and theme changes are handled. Nothing in the .md files records this as an accepted decision; the onboarding-draft claim was not checked.

### errors-9 [Serialise AccountSession state transitions] - Session and sync bookkeeping fields are mutated from several threads without synchronisation
- severity: low | verdict: weakened | urgency: later | effort: S | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/firestore/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/firestore/AccountSession.kt:95`
- evidence: start() does a non-atomic check-then-set on `cancel` (lines 95-98); `openingJob` and `completion` are plain vars (lines 199, 205) written in switchTo(), which runs from the auth observer on the app scope's Default dispatcher (FirebaseAuthAdapter.kt:41), and in stopAll(). start() is called both at app start and from the worker thread (AppContainer.kt:212). switchTo's same-user guard (line 146) reads current.value before line 158 writes it, so two concurrent calls for the same user could both pass. FirestoreSync.jobs is likewise a plain mutable list touched by start()/stop() (FirestoreSync.kt:65-71, 109-111).
- impact: In a rare interleaving the account could be opened twice (duplicate Firestore listeners: double reads against the free quota and a leaked set of listeners) or a stale opening job could survive a sign-out. Not observed; normal start order makes it unlikely.
- fix: Confine AccountSession state changes to a single-threaded context (a Mutex or limitedParallelism(1) dispatcher) and make start() idempotent atomically.
- second reviewer: The fields are unsynchronised (AccountSession.kt:93-98, 199, 205), but the start() race does not occur: MasroufyApplication.kt:33-34 calls container.start() (AppContainer.kt:107) on the main thread before the background graph is installed, so the worker's s.start() at AppContainer.kt:212 always finds cancel set. switchTo is called from a single collector (FirebaseAuthAdapter.kt:41), so calls are serial; only stop() running alongside that collector remains, which is theoretical.

### perf-3 [Share one Home data bundle between the hero and the cards] - Home does the same heavy reads twice: screen and cards each load the month, the profile and the full-history balance
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/screens/home/cards/HomeCardsData.kt:54`
- evidence: HomeScreen.kt:131 calls home.homeScreen.load(LoadHomeScreenRequest(periodForDate(today, payday), today, payday, includeHistory = false)); HomeCardsData.kt:54 issues the identical request again in its own LaunchedEffect keyed on the same changes.version. home.profile.load() is called at HomeScreen.kt:128 and HomeCardsData.kt:49. The daily card (LoadHomeCardData.kt:76-83 onHand) repeats the wallets + listByDateRange(from opening, today) + walletBalancesOn that LoadWithYouNow.kt:38-42 just did, and calls calendar.items while HomeScreen calls calendar.summary. HomeCardsData.kt:58 also runs the People overview for one total. None of these are on a background dispatcher.
- impact: Roughly doubles perf-1: two full-history scans (3,000 rows each, two sorts each) and two period loads per Home refresh instead of one. Estimate +40-120 ms of UI-thread time at 3,000 transactions, on every save anywhere in the app because both effects key on changes.version.
- fix: Load the month data and the balance once in one place (a small Home loader in the wiring graph that returns one bundle) and pass it to both the hero and the cards; derive the daily card's on-hand figure from the WithYouNow result.

### tests-4 [Shared port contract tests for memory vs Firestore adapters] - No contract suite shared by memory and Firestore adapters; the two already differ
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: L | status: not_built
- where: `native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/memory/MemoryRepositories.kt:49`
- evidence: MemoryTransactionRepository.update (lines 49-62) stores `patch.note` as is. The Firestore test firestore/src/androidInstrumentedTest/.../FirestoreTransactionRepositoryTest.kt:56 expects the same call to return `رقم ****7890` (long digits masked). So the same port call gives different results per adapter. There is no test file under app/src/jvmTest/**/memory/ (no direct tests of the Memory* adapters), no abstract/shared test class used by both sides, and MirrorParityTest only compares the local mirror with Firestore, not memory with Firestore.
- impact: All ~2,100 app/ui/wiring tests run on the memory adapters. Where memory is more permissive than Firestore (masking, missing fields, the 30-id limit, ordering, merge semantics) a use case can pass every test and still behave differently on the phone.
- fix: Write one abstract contract class per port (start with transactions, wallets, source records, import batches, settlement writer) in a shared test source set; run it on JVM with Memory* and in androidInstrumentedTest with Firestore*. Decide whether masking belongs in the use case (so both adapters see it) rather than in the adapter.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: Should masking of long digit runs move into the use case so memory and Firestore behave identically?

### build-11 [Show clearly at the end of each test run whether the real-statement checks ran.] - Acceptance tests on the real statement are silently excluded when the owner's files are absent
- severity: low | verdict: unverified | urgency: later | effort: S | status: by_design
- where: `native-app/core/build.gradle.kts:48`
- evidence: core/build.gradle.kts lines 42-58 exclude RealStatementTest, RealPdfStatementTest and RealQnbPdfTest when files/transactions_full.csv or the pdf-pages JSON files are not found; data/build.gradle.kts lines 46-50 do the same for RealDataFlowTest, RealQnbImportTest and RealTransferPartiesTest. Root build.gradle.kts lines 38-43 print one lifecycle line. CI has none of these files (native-core.yml line 3 says so).
- impact: A green test run on GitHub or on a clean machine does not prove the money criteria in CLAUDE.md (reference totals and balance chain to the halala); it only proves the golden-file tests. A model that reports 'all tests passed' from such a run overstates what was checked. The exclusion itself is correct because the files are private.
- fix: Print an explicit end-of-build summary (count of excluded real-data test classes) and have the handover template require stating whether the real-statement tests ran.

### arch-11 [Single amount-to-input formatter in core] - Several hand-rolled amount formatters in ui hard-code two decimals; one breaks on negatives
- severity: low | verdict: unverified | urgency: later | effort: S | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/screens/operations/OperationCoreEdit.kt:41`
- evidence: minorAsInput at OperationCoreEdit.kt:41 returns "${minor / 100}.${(minor % 100)...}"; for a negative value Kotlin gives a negative remainder, so -150 becomes '-1.-50'. Similar private conversions with a literal 100 exist at investment/future/FutureState.kt:33 and 132, investment/InvestmentFormat.kt:38 and investment/holdings/HoldingIcons.kt:133, next to the core formatter in core/FormatMoney.kt.
- impact: Amount-to-text rules are duplicated outside core, so a fix or a currency with a different minor unit must be changed in five places; a negative amount reaching the edit field would show a malformed number.
- fix: Expose one input-format function in core next to formatAmount and replace the four ui copies; guard the sign.

### tests-10 [Single source for firestore.rules in emulators; run rules tests in CI] - Rules used by the 'real rules' emulator are a hand-kept copy that already differs from firestore.rules
- severity: low | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: bug_suspected
- where: `native-app/firestore/emulator-auth/firestore.rules:23`
- evidence: diff of ROOT/firestore.rules against native-app/firestore/emulator-auth/firestore.rules: root line 23 adds `&& noLongDigits(list[i])` and root lines 33-39 add the RULES-4 function, marked in the comment as a draft not deployed. Most instrumented tests run on native-app/firestore/emulator/test.rules where line 8 is `allow read, write: if true`. The rules unit tests (native-app/firestore/rules-test/rules.test.mjs, 17 cases, line 13 reads ../../../firestore.rules) are not run by any workflow.
- impact: Three rule sets exist (root draft, emulator copy, whatever is deployed). Tests that claim to run under real rules exercise a copy that can drift, and the security rules tests are manual. A rules change can be deployed without the suite having been run against that exact file.
- fix: Point emulator-auth/firebase.json at the root firestore.rules (or generate the copy in a script and fail when they differ) and add the rules tests to CI: the Firestore emulator runs on ubuntu without secrets.

### arch-10 [Split in-memory adapters into a test-support module] - In-memory test adapters and seed JSON live in the production source set of app
- severity: low | verdict: confirmed | urgency: later | effort: M | status: by_design
- where: `native-app/wiring/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/wiring/OperationsGraph.kt:69`
- evidence: app has only commonMain and jvmTest; the memory package (33 files) is in commonMain. Production wiring already uses one of them: MemorySyncCursor() at OperationsGraph.kt:69 and PeopleGraph.kt:98 (comment at 67 says an in-memory cursor is enough there). androidApp/build.gradle.kts:50-52 sets isMinifyEnabled = false for 'release'; only 'fast' and 'fastEmulator' (59-66) run R8, so an unminified build carries all memory adapters.
- impact: Nothing stops a memory adapter being wired into the real app by mistake: it compiles, works until the app closes, then the data is gone. Today the only use is a cursor that is deliberately not persisted.
- fix: Move the memory package to a separate test-support module that only jvmTest source sets depend on, and give the two ManageProjects call sites a tiny named in-process cursor in wiring.
- second reviewer: MemorySyncCursor() is used in production wiring at OperationsGraph.kt:69 and PeopleGraph.kt:98, and androidApp/build.gradle.kts:50-51 sets isMinifyEnabled = false for release with R8 only on fast (58-61). Further memory classes appear in production paths as defaults or previews (AccountSession.kt:47,55 MemoryActiveSpaceStore, overridden by AppContainer.kt:84; SupportForm.kt:42; IosProblemLog.kt:10). No wrong wiring exists today, matching the by_design status.

### build-4 [Stop the real build with a clear message when the Firebase settings file is missing.] - A clean machine can silently build a 'fast' APK with no Firebase inside
- severity: medium | verdict: unverified | urgency: before_release | effort: S | status: bug_root_cause_found
- where: `native-app/androidApp/build.gradle.kts:156`
- evidence: Root build.gradle.kts lines 49-51 look for secrets/google-services.json by walking up parent folders; secrets/ is gitignored. androidApp/build.gradle.kts line 33 writes BuildConfig.HAS_FIREBASE_SETTINGS = (firebaseSettings != null); lines 156-185 apply google-services and crashlytics only when the file exists, otherwise only logger.lifecycle prints a warning (line 184) and every variant, including assembleFast, still builds. At runtime FirebaseSetup.kt line 58 returns null when HAS_FIREBASE_SETTINGS is false.
- impact: On a fresh clone or a worktree outside the owner's folder tree, assembleFast succeeds and yields a real-looking APK with no backend, no sign-in and no crash reports. The only signal is one line among thousands of R8 warnings. If that APK is sent to a tester it looks like a broken release.
- fix: Fail owner-facing variants explicitly: when firebaseSettings == null, make assembleFast/assembleDebug/bundle tasks throw a GradleException with a clear message unless -Pmasroufy.allowNoFirebase=true is passed; keep emulator variants buildable.

### todo-ui-1 [T1] - Onboarding carries exactly one bank end to end (state, card, draft, use case, other-country path)
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: not_built
- where: `native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/screens/onboarding/OnboardingState.kt:56`
- evidence: Six places fix the count at one. (1) OnboardingState.kt:56 `val bank: OnbBank? = null` and :143 `addBank` does `s.answers.copy(bank = bank)` (replaces). (2) OnboardingMoneyCards.kt:103-106 BankCard holds a single bankKey/last4/text and :140 calls addBank once, after which the green reply panel moves to the next card. (3) OnboardingDraft.kt:62 writes one bank in three fixed slots and :69 rejects any draft whose field count is not 16. (4) app/.../usecase/OnboardAccount.kt:36 `OnboardingInput(... val bank: AddWalletInput? = null)`; :121-129 prepares one wallet, :139 saves one. (5) OnboardAccount.kt:121 skips the bank when `wallets.any { it.bankId == b.bankId }`, so a second account at the SAME bank would be silently dropped. (6) OnboardingRoutes.kt:43 `CountryMoney.apply(spaceId, cashMinor, bank: AddWalletInput?)` (Egypt path) takes one bank. Scenario: owner has Al Rajhi 4,837.83 and a second bank 1,200.00; after adding Al Rajhi the only actions are Next/Back, Back and adding again overwrites the first. Tests: OnboardingMoneyStateTest and OnboardingDraftTest exist (grep hits on addBank/finishInput/encodeDraft, contents not opened); none can cover two banks because the type forbids it.
- impact: A user with two bank accounts finishes onboarding with one wallet; SMS from the second bank has no wallet to land on until he finds More > Wallets. HANDOVER.md:146 lists this as not done.
- fix: Build plan (size M). First failing tests: (a) OnboardingMoneyStateTest: addBank twice keeps 2 banks and finishInput returns both; (b) OnboardAccount test: finish with two banks (incl. same bank, different last4) saves 2 wallets, and a retry after interruption saves 0 more; (c) OnboardingDraftTest: round-trip of 2 banks and decoding of an old d1 draft. Changes: OnboardingState.kt bank -> banks: List<OnbBank> (+ removeBank, reply text with count); OnboardingMoneyCards.kt BankCard shows added banks as rows plus 'add another bank' and 'continue' (reuse BankPickerList, PickedBankRow, lastFour, MoneyField already there); OnboardingDraft.kt version d2 with a list segment, keep d1 decode; OnboardAccount.kt OnboardingInput.banks, prepare ALL via ManageWallets.prepare before any write, then save all, dedupe on bankId+last4 instead of bankId; OnboardingRoutes.kt CountryMoney.apply takes a list (and its NewCountryMoney implementation); TextsAreaOnboarding.kt new strings in both dialects. Reuse: ManageWallets.prepare, connectKnownBanks (SmsHandoff.kt:90) already loops over all wallets.
- second reviewer: Confirmed: OnboardingState.kt:56 holds a single `bank` and :143 addBank replaces it; OnboardingDraft.kt:62/:69 has three fixed slots and a 16-field check; OnboardAccount.kt:36 takes one bank, :121 dedupes on bankId only and :139 saves one wallet. Additional places the fix must touch: the rememberSaveable saver `OnbStateSaver` (OnboardingScreen.kt:82) and the reply text at OnboardingState.kt:166. Caveat for the same-bank case: SmsHandoff.kt:87-95 maps a sender to a wallet only when the bank has one wallet, so two accounts at one bank will not be auto-routed, and a bankId+last4 dedupe still drops a second same-bank account when both last4 are empty.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: في البداية: تسمح بحسابين في نفس البنك (بآخر أربع أرقام مختلفة) ولا بنك واحد من كل نوع؟ وفيه حد أقصى لعدد البنوك في البداية؟

### todo-ui-2 [T2] - SMS permission is asked only after onboarding ends; notification permission is asked once, without explanation, outside the onboarding cards
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: partially_built
- where: `native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/screens/onboarding/OnboardingScreen.kt:152`
- evidence: SMS: the SOURCE card only stores a choice (OnboardingState.kt:36 comment 'does not get stored... permission is there'; :147 pickSource). The request happens after finish: OnboardingScreen.kt:150-152 `nav.pop()` then `SmsHandoff.start()`, and the system dialog is launched at imports/SmsHandoff.kt:185 `permissions.requestSms()` (other call site: BankSmsSettingsScreen.kt:193). Android side: androidApp/.../AndroidPermissions.kt:32-37 requests RECEIVE_SMS+READ_SMS together. Notifications: it IS requested on Android 13+ (AndroidPermissions.kt:43-48; manifest device/src/androidMain/AndroidManifest.xml:7), but only from MainActivity.kt:138-146 `askNotificationsOnce`: waits for `AppSession.Ready`, sets the 'asked-notifications' flag BEFORE asking (:143) and fires the bare system dialog (:144) with no explanation card. Grep for requestNotifications over all .kt returns only these three places, so no screen can ask again. What breaks if SMS moves earlier: connect() (SmsHandoff.kt:145-152) calls connectKnownBanks(deps.imports.wallets(), ...), and the bank wallet is written only in OnboardAccount.finish (OnboardAccount.kt:139); granting at the SOURCE card and connecting immediately would find 0 known senders. For Egypt the space does not exist yet either (OnboardingScreen.kt:131-145). No test references requestNotifications/askNotificationsOnce (grep over test sources).
- impact: The promise 'we ask at the start' is not met: the SMS dialog arrives over the home screen after the journey, and the notification dialog appears with no reason given, at a moment not tied to any card. A user who taps 'do not allow' once is never asked again by the app, so auto-recorded transactions produce no notification (AndroidDeviceNotifier.kt:36 documents the silent BLOCKED outcome).
- fix: Size S-M. Keep 'connect' where it is and move only the 'ask'. (1) OnboardingScreen SOURCE step: when SMS is picked show the existing SmsPermissionSheet and call LocalPermissions.requestSms() there; record granted/denied in OnbAnswers. (2) Leave SmsHandoff.start() after finish unchanged: the host already connects when the permission is already present (SmsHandoff.kt:176 `handoffNeedsConnect(st, granted, readable)`, doc at :100 'even if it was there before'), so wallets exist by then. (3) Add a short notifications explanation in the same card (or READY card) and call requestNotifications() from it; delete askNotificationsOnce from MainActivity or make it a fallback for old accounts; set the flag AFTER the result. (4) Add a 'turn on notifications' row in Bank SMS settings for the denied case. Failing tests first: pure state test that picking SMS yields a 'needs permission' sub-step and that denial still lets the journey continue; SmsHandoffOrderTest case 'permission already granted at finish => connect runs without the explain sheet'.
- second reviewer: Confirmed: OnboardingScreen.kt:150-152 pops and only then calls SmsHandoff.start(); the system dialog is at SmsHandoff.kt:185; the only requestNotifications caller is MainActivity.kt:138-146, which sets the flag at :143 before asking at :144 with no explanation. The proposed fix holds for the granted path (SmsHandoffRules.kt:27 handoffNeedsConnect does not require `asked`, so connect runs after finish without the sheet). Gap in the fix: if the user denies at the SOURCE card, SmsHandoff.start() after finish shows the EXPLAIN sheet again (SmsHandoffRules.kt:22, `!st.asked`), so the denial must start the handoff in the asked/card state or the user is asked twice.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: الإذنين يتطلبوا في كارت واحد جوه البداية (مع شرح قصير) ولا كل واحد في كارت لوحده؟ ولو رفض الإشعارات نفكّره تاني إمتى؟

### todo-ui-5 [T3] - Security list (14): 9 confirmed done in code, 2 partial, 1 draft, 2 deferred; no false 'done' found, but caveats on four items
- severity: medium (finder said high) | verdict: weakened | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: partially_built

So every default build ships with App Check off. PRIVACY-4 DONE: device/.../AndroidLocalFiles.kt:27 and :66-68 write to noBackupFilesDir/backups, :39 and :61-64 prune, :79-86 one-time wipe of the old external folder. ANDROID-3 DONE: MainActivity.kt:122-127 FLAG_SECURE + setRecentsScreenshotEnabled, called from onResume :104 and onPause :109; ui/app/MasroufyApp.kt:99-101 clearAndSetSemantics while locked. Caveat: every cleanup call is wrapped in runCatching (SignIn.kt:61,67,70), so a failed wipe is silent. SECRETS-1 NOT DONE (deferred by owner, plan :29, OVERRIDES §118-2). SECRETS-2 DONE: .gitignore:50-60; `git ls-files` shows no google-services.json, keystore or .env tracked (only .env.example). SECRETS-5 DONE: scripts/secretscan.mjs:34-40 forbidden names, :64-71 new binaries. LOGS-2 DONE: CrashReports.kt:46-50 and :59-62. PRIVACY-7 DONE: ui/src/androidMain/.../BackupFiles.android.kt:59 reads on IO, :64 JSON/octet-stream only, :84 size check, :95 bounded read. RULES-4 DRAFT: firestore.rules:49 has the `^cat-[a-z0-9\p{Arabic}-]{1,60}$` rule, while native-app/firestore/emulator-auth/firestore.rules:41 still has the old `size() <= 120`; deployment state cannot be seen from code. PRIVACY-2 PARTIAL as reported: data/StoreRules.kt:17-20 masks spaced/dashed numbers only from 12 digits, :23 only in three groups, :31 only top-level strings. SECRETS-6 DONE: .github/workflows/prices.yml:24 `permissions: {}`, :33-34 job-level write, :37 pinned checkout, :40 persist-credentials false, :70 token only in the push step.
- impact: The open part that matters before the store: App Check is compiled out, sign-up is open and the repo is public, so anyone can create accounts and burn the free daily quota for every user (the plan's own AUTH-1 scenario). The signing key (SECRETS-1) must change before the first store upload or upgrades break later. The rest are low-risk leftovers.
- fix: Before release: owner does the console steps in tools/security/APP-CHECK-STEPS.md, then build with -Pmasroufy.appCheck=true and enforce in the console (AUTH-1); create the real release keystore (SECRETS-1); deploy the RULES-4 rules after copying the same rule into native-app/firestore/emulator-auth/firestore.rules so the emulator tests test what is deployed. Small follow-ups: report (ProblemCode) when a sign-out wipe fails instead of swallowing it (SignIn.kt:61-70); inspect or remove the tracked zip from the public repo; PRIVACY-1 stays a later round.
- second reviewer: The per-item evidence I spot-checked is accurate: App Check off by default (androidApp/build.gradle.kts:20, :80; FirebaseSetup.kt:62), swallowed sign-out cleanup (usecase/SignIn.kt:61-70, 111-113; AndroidLocalFiles.kt:89 also swallows), rules mismatch (firestore.rules:49 vs native-app/firestore/emulator-auth/firestore.rules:41), PRIVACY-2 limits (data/StoreRules.kt:17, :23, :31), prices.yml:24/:40, and `git ls-files` shows only .env.example and masroofi-claude-code.zip. Severity high is overstated: this is a status inventory with no false 'done', the one material open item (AUTH-1) is rated medium in PLAN-0.6.md:9 and is a documented owner-pending console step, and 'the repo is public' cannot be verified from code.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: إمتى تعمل خطوات لوحة فايربيز بتاعة التحقق من البرنامج ومفتاح التوقيع الجديد؟ الاتنين لازم يخلصوا قبل المتجر.

### todo-ui-3 [T4] - Four different date-entry implementations; three sheets make the user type the date as text
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: partially_built
- where: `native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/screens/people/EventForms.kt:168`
- evidence: All grids draw with the shared ui/shell/CalendarGrid.kt:85, but the wrapper differs. (A) `DateField` (people/EventForms.kt:168-194, `internal`, lives in the people package): a SecondaryButton showing dayMonthYear, grid expands INLINE under it. Used by EventForms.kt:110, ProjectSheets.kt:71, dues/DebtSettlements.kt:87, dues/SubscriptionAddSheet.kt:71, imports/SmsPasteParts.kt:86, imports/SmsHistorySection.kt:139, shell/AddOperationSheet.kt:183, operations/OperationCoreEdit.kt:72, more/JobChangeSheet.kt:211. (B) `CalcDateField` (investment/calc/CalcDate.kt:55-125): a field-shaped row with fullDate text that opens a BOTTOM SHEET with a year stepper (:98-104) above the grid. Used by RetirementEgyptPanel.kt:57, RetirementSaudiPanel.kt:67 and :92, SavingsCalculatorScreen.kt:183. (C) a private `DatePicker` copy in budgets/GoalEditSheet.kt:147 used at :91-93 with yet another text format (longDate) and its own open/close logic. (D) no picker at all, a plain TextInput with placeholder = today's ISO date, ltr: more/BalanceUpdateSheet.kt:89, more/WalletAddSheet.kt:161, people/PersonAddOperation.kt:89 (validated by isValidIsoDate at BalanceUpdateSheet.kt:67, WalletAddSheet.kt:99, PersonAddOperation.kt:44). CalendarScreen.kt:126 and PersonOnce.kt:117 use the grid for display, not picking. HANDOVER.md:146 says unifying the date picker is not done.
- impact: The same action looks and behaves four ways: inline grid, bottom sheet, or typing '2026-10-01' by hand. Variant A cannot jump years (month arrows only), so a birthday or an old opening date needs dozens of taps; variant D rejects anything not typed in the exact format.
- fix: Size M. Move one component to ui/components (e.g. DateInput) built from CalcDate's sheet (it already has the year stepper) with DateField's API `(date, today, onPick, modifier)`; make people.DateField and CalcDateField thin aliases, then delete them; replace GoalEditSheet's private DatePicker; replace the three TextInput dates (BalanceUpdateSheet, WalletAddSheet, PersonAddOperation) with it, passing max = today where the code already checks `day <= today`. One text format everywhere. Failing test first: a pure test of the month/year stepping function (year roll-over at December, min/max bounds) extracted from CalcDate.kt:112-116 and EventForms.kt:182-189, which today are two different implementations of the same arithmetic.
- second reviewer: Confirmed all four variants: inline DateField at people/EventForms.kt:168-194 (month arrows and 'today' only, no year jump) with the nine listed call sites; bottom-sheet CalcDateField with year stepper at investment/calc/CalcDate.kt:55-125; private DatePicker at budgets/GoalEditSheet.kt:147; plain typed ISO TextInput at BalanceUpdateSheet.kt:89, WalletAddSheet.kt:161 and PersonAddOperation.kt:89. The two month-stepping implementations (EventForms.kt:182-189 vs CalcDate.kt:112-116) give the same result for a step of one, so this is duplication and inconsistency, not a calculation bug.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: الشكل الموحّد: لوحة بتطلع من تحت فيها السنة والشهر (زي الحاسبات) ولا شبكة بتفتح تحت الخانة (زي إضافة عملية)؟

- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release | effort: M | status: not_built
