Smaller issues: wallet balances add transactions whose currency differs from the wallet, an unreadable statement balance cell is silently skipped from reconciliation, two Double divisions remain in Budget averages, and rateOfMoney multiplies without an overflow check. The CLAUDE.md acceptance totals (302,171.45 / 201,703.18 / 100,468.27 / 300,040.87 and chain 4,837.83 -> 2,707.25 @ 2026-09-04 with zero mismatches) ARE asserted in Kotlin by core/src/jvmTest/.../RealStatementTest.kt, but that test only runs on the owner's machine and is silently excluded elsewhere.

**Strengths:**
- native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/Money.kt: amounts are Long minor units built directly from digit strings (no multiply/divide), more than 2 non-zero decimals is rejected instead of rounded, every add/subtract is range-checked, and splitMoney distributes the remainder so parts always sum to the whole.
- native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/Quantity.kt: price x quantity (gold grams, shares, purity) uses a hand-written 128-bit multiply and a single half-away-from-zero rounding; cost of a sold part is by ratio and the remainder by subtraction, so no halala is lost.
- native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/InheritanceAllocation.kt: fractions to halalas by largest remainder, plus a matrix rounding (augmenting paths) that guarantees every asset row and every heir column sum exactly.
- native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/SmsAmountTokens.kt: SMS amounts are refused rather than guessed - ambiguous number on both sides of the currency, bad grouping, comma decimals, date/time fragments and glued reference numbers all become Malformed/Ambiguous, with a 100-million cap.
- native-app/core/src/jvmTest/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/RealStatementTest.kt + core/Reconcile.kt: the four reference totals and the full running-balance chain (every row, to the halala) are asserted in Kotlin; FormatMoney.kt formats by integer division and remainder only.

**Not checked:**
- Whether the Android decimal keypad on the target devices actually shows a comma key (money-1 code path is certain; the keystroke was not tested on a device).
- How a transaction with a currency different from its wallet can be created (CSV import request.currency, SMS foreign purchases, backup restore) - only AddTransaction.kt:85 and EditTransaction.kt:142 guards were seen.
- SMS 'golden dot' rule: core/SmsAmountTokens.kt:87 reads "1.234 SAR" as 234.00 with goldenDot=true (open owner question OVERRIDES §75.1); the consumer that is supposed to stop it being auto-recorded was not opened.
- Foreign-currency SMS purchases (foreignCurrency / originalAmount) and currencies with 3 decimals (KWD, BHD, OMR): Currency enum has only SAR, EGP, USD, EUR, GBP, AED, all with 100 minor units; storage of the foreign amount was not read.
- Rosca.kt, RoscaWizard.kt, BnplSplitMath.kt, InstallmentCount.kt, ui SplitMath/SplitParse, SavingsCalc.kt, RetirementSaudi/Egypt.kt, InvestFutureMath.kt, AssetSaleGain.kt, HoldingsView.purposePercents - not opened; their headers claim integer-only math.
- Value of ZAKAT_RATE_PER_THOUSAND and whether a Gregorian-year rate (2.577%) is ever needed; hawl logic beyond the wallet series.
- Refund/reversal sign conventions (Reversals.kt, RefundConfirmEffect.kt) and the debit/credit sign of BalanceCheckpoint adjustments were not opened.
- Firestore/data codecs: how a money field stored as Double in an old document is converted to Long (no 'is Double' handling found by grep in data/src; codec files not opened).
- Empty debit/credit cells in legacy CSV: ImportSchemas.kt:104-105 passes "" to tryParseMoney which returns null and rejects the row - correct for the owner's file (test passes with 0 errors) but not verified against other banks' exports.
- No build or test was run (read-only review), so the acceptance test's current pass/fail state on this branch is unverified.

**Other titles:**
- FX rate field accepts comma as decimal (FxSettle.kt:32) while the amount field beside it treats comma as thousands
- formatPercentOrNA/savingsRatePercent use Double for a display percentage (FormatMoney.kt:100-113, documented as a port of the old app)
- ZakatHawl.zakatWealthSeries values non-cash holdings at today's price for the whole year (documented choice, ZakatHawl.kt:8-10)
- LoadWithYouNow total ignores the currency parameter it receives except for labelling (LoadWithYouNow.kt:36-49)

### sms
Lane: bank SMS pipeline (read-only review of r051-int, 0.5.5). The pipeline is carefully built: the receiver is protected, text is sanitised before any storage, recording is guarded by one lock plus a per-message reference, and nothing is auto-recorded unless its template is whitelisted and the owner has confirmed that shape once. I found no critical defect. The main risks are: (1) de-duplication depends on the same SMS always carrying the same timestamp across the live receiver, the inbox scan and a new or restored phone; when it does not, the 0.5.2 "second charge within 60 seconds" rule turns the copy into an automatically recorded second transaction; (2) a forged SMS with a bank sender name and a learned template is recorded silently, because nothing requires the card or account digits to match the wallet; (3) the bank text (names, balance, merchant) is stored in Firestore permanently and the Play restricted-SMS-permission declaration is not planned in the docs; (4) every background cycle re-walks the whole waiting queue with no time limit. None of this has been exercised on real bank messages on a real phone (HANDOVER.md:91).

**Strengths:**
- Receiver is locked to the system: the manifest receiver requires BROADCAST_SMS and the code rejects multipart messages whose parts come from different senders (native-app/device/src/androidMain/AndroidManifest.xml:9, BankSmsReceiver.kt:41-44).
- Privacy by construction on the device: text is sanitised before any write, OTP messages are dropped before storage, the body is wiped once a message is handled, backups are disabled and no code path logs message text (SmsInboxStore.kt:174 and 206-213, SmsVocabulary.kt:97-114, androidApp manifest allowBackup=false at line 22, BackgroundWork.kt:34).
- Idempotent recording: one app-wide lock, a per-message reference that is unique across all wallets of a country, and acknowledge-after-commit so a crash yields 'duplicate' rather than a second record (ReviewSmsInbox.kt:37 and 204-207, ImportPreviewStage.kt:71-83).
- Conservative auto-recording: whole-message template whitelist, a doubt gate for status words, multiple dates, future or stale dates and hidden characters, plus learn mode that requires one owner confirmation per template per sender (SmsShapeGate.kt:115-122, AutoRecordSms.kt:26-28).
- Crash-safe scanning and reinstall recovery: the inbox cursor moves only after the batch transaction commits, and an account-level read point lets a reinstalled device resume from where the account stopped (AndroidSmsInbox.kt:146-168, SmsCatchUp.kt:29-50).

**Not checked:**
- Whether real phones (Samsung Messages, Google Messages, Xiaomi) store date_sent equal to the PDU timestamp, and what restored messages look like on a new phone: this decides how often sms-1 happens. Needs a device test.
- Atomicity of ImportStatement.commit on Firestore (a batch that fails halfway) and the Firestore security rules for transactions and source records: not opened.
- loadLedger cost per wallet route and actual timing with 5,000+ queued messages: not measured, no build or emulator run allowed.
- Two phones signed in to the same account while offline (Firestore cache not yet synced) recording the same message: reasoned only, not traced.
- Effect of renaming a wallet on SMS de-duplication (accountIdentity is the wallet name: SmsWaitReasons.kt:18); the cross-wallet reference check in ImportPreviewStage.kt:71-83 appears to cover it, the amount/day similarity index was not checked.
- UsageEvents.smsParse and Problems.report internals (whether sender names or exception messages could carry message text): not opened.
- Egypt parser files (EgyptBankSms.kt, EgyptSmsShapes.kt, SmsKnownShapesEgypt.kt) and the Saudi field/line files were not read; regex backtracking was only reviewed in SmsSafety, SmsGuards, SmsShapeGate and the top of BankSmsParser.
- ReturnedSmsEffect auto-cancel of an original transaction on a matching reference tail was read (ReturnedSmsEffect.kt:63-96) but core matchReversal/canReverse rules were not opened; a forged 'returned' message cancelling a real transaction was not ruled out.
- Automatic addition of known bank senders: knownBankSenders was read (KnownBankSenders.kt:18-37) but the BankCatalog sender names themselves were not reviewed for generic or numeric names.
- Contents of golden/sms.json and scripts/golden generators were only counted (428 and 29 bodies), not read.
- Notification text for waiting messages (AndroidDeviceNotifier) was not opened to confirm no message text appears on the lock screen.

**Other titles:**
- Period-read adapter (device/.../AndroidBankSms.kt:49-57) uses the provider 'date' column and the unsanitised body, so its SMS reference differs from the inbox path for the same message; its OTP regex (lines 69-72) is also weaker than core SMS_SENSITIVE_PATTERN. I found no UI caller of ReadBankSms.read, so reachability is unconfirmed.
- Usage data logs the sender string per parse run (app/.../UsageSignals.kt:42,46); a sender typed by the user (for example a phone number) would be sent as-is unless UsageEvents filters it.
- Waiting message bodies stay in the on-device SQLite queue in plain text with no retention limit (SmsInboxStore.kt:22, 206-219 wipes the body only on acknowledge); mitigated by allowBackup=false and app-private storage.
- BankSmsReceiver opens its own SQLiteOpenHelper per message while the app holds a shared one (BankSmsReceiver.kt:23, SmsInboxStore.kt:228); serialised by a lock, but two helpers on one file is fragile.
- SmsSafety.financial and REDACT_KEEP use greedy digit runs followed by an optional currency; worst case is quadratic on a long digit string, bounded by the 8000-character cap only on the device path (the paste path has no length cap in ReadBankSms.paste).

### sync
Lane: data integrity, storage and sync (branch r051-int, native-app/firestore + data + the use cases that write through them). The storage layer is careful in several places (server-side transactions for settlements, an add-missing-only restore with checksum and version check, an atomic two-country transfer batch, whole-collection listeners so no total is hidden by a date limit or a silent query limit). The main risks are structural: (1) read cost: the app keeps one listener per collection (57 per the code comment) over ALL documents, and a 4-hour background worker reopens them, so a 3,000-transaction user can exhaust the project-wide Spark quota alone; (2) strict decoding with no document schema version means one unknown value written by a newer build breaks every read of that collection on an older build; (3) the unit of work is a pass-through and every write is fire-and-forget, so multi-collection deletes/undo are not atomic and server-rejected writes are never shown (writeFailures has no collector); (4) sign-out wipes the local cache without waiting for queued offline writes, and the "this device has a full copy" mark survives that wipe.

READ ARITHMETIC (assumption: 3,000 transactions + ~3,000 sourceRecords + ~1,000 other docs = D of about 7,000; up to ~10,000 if notificationReceipts grow per SMS).
- App open, cache resumed within 30 minutes of the last listen: only changed docs + at most ~57 minimum query charges + a few single gets = roughly 60-150 reads.
- App open (or background cycle) more than 30 minutes after the last listen: Firestore bills a resumed listener as a brand-new query, so about D = 7,000 reads.
- Background worker every 4 hours (BackgroundWork.kt:44) with the process dead each time: 6 x 7,000 = 42,000 reads/day without the user opening the app. Plus 3 manual opens: 3 x 7,000 = 21,000. Total about 63,000 > 50,000/day for ONE user, and the quota is shared by all users of the project.
- First sign-in on a device, or any sign-in after sign-out (cache is wiped): D = 7,000.
- Pull-to-refresh: repositories read from the in-memory mirror once a group is synced (DocQuery.kt:98-99), so user data costs 0 billed reads; the refresh handler itself was not traced (see notChecked).
- Full backup: 1 full server read = 7,000. Restore: plan (7,000) + apply (7,000) + one get per restored row = at least 14,000.
The 30-minute billing rule is from Firebase's pricing documentation, not something measured here; confirm in the Firebase console usage graph before acting.

**Strengths:**
- Settlement is a real server transaction with a revision counter, server-only reads, idempotent request ids and bounded retries, so two phones cannot over-settle a debt (native-app/firestore/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/firestore/FirestoreSettlementWriter.kt:28-56)
- Restore only adds missing documents inside transactions keyed by stable document ids, so restoring twice creates no duplicates and never overwrites live data; the file is checked for app, schemaVersion 2/3, per-group counts and checksum before any write (FirestoreFullBackup.kt:51-80, usecase/FullBackup.kt:127-169)
- The two-country self-transfer writes the pair and both legs in one WriteBatch, all or nothing (native-app/firestore/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/firestore/FirestoreSpaces.kt:57-67)
- Reads never mix an incomplete cache into totals by design: a group is served from memory only after a server snapshot or on a device that completed a full download, queries are described once and run identically on mirror and server, and account switch stops listeners and clears memory (LocalMirror.kt:80-98, DocQuery.kt:28-69, AccountSession.kt:143-147, 218-228)
- Account erase and repositories share one list of collection names guarded by a test, and shared-catalog sync uses server timestamps for its cursor instead of the device clock (UserCollections.kt:29-35, FirestoreAccountAdapters.kt:149-179)

**Not checked:**
- What the pull-to-refresh handler actually calls (ui/screens/common/PullRefresh.kt and its callers were not opened); the 0-billed-reads figure is inferred from the mirror read path only
- The 30-minute listener billing rule and the 57-listener count were not measured on a real project; the read arithmetic is an estimate from Firebase's documented pricing and code comments
- Real document counts per transaction (sourceRecords, notificationReceipts, links) were assumed, not counted from fixtures
- Whether Firestore persistence settings (cache size, garbage collection) are customised anywhere; a grep found no cache settings in main sources, so SDK defaults were assumed
- Id generation (the ids port and its Android implementation) and collision risk between two devices
- Delete with three choices UI flow (DeleteWithChoices.kt, Undo.kt, UndoWindow.kt), archive of people/dues/spaces (ArchivePersonSafely.kt, ArchiveDues.kt), and dangling references to archived or deleted categories, wallets and people
- Import path atomicity (ImportStatement.kt, ResumeStagedBatch.kt staged/committed protocol) was not opened
- BackupCheck.kt validation rules (reference integrity, limits) and FullBackupSpaces.kt restore of other countries were not read; only FullBackup.kt lines 95-254
- The repair use cases (Repair*.kt) and the device-side repair backup
- Whether FirestoreSync.errors is collected and shown anywhere in the UI
- firestore.rules content and whether rules could reject normal writes
- iOS wiring (sign-out cleanup, first-sync marks) and the data module codecs other than DocFields.kt
- Behaviour of the Firestore SDK when raising cache snapshots mid-download (relevant to sync-6) was reasoned from SDK knowledge, not tested

**Other titles:**
- saveAll and deleteAll split above 500 items into separate batches, so any non-import bulk write over 500 rows is not atomic (FirestoreSpace.kt:101-118)
- Offline writes launch a coroutine per commit that never completes until the network returns (FirestoreSpace.kt:55), unbounded growth during long offline sessions
- Account erase has no resume marker: an interruption leaves a half-deleted account until the user runs it again (FirestoreAccountEraser.kt:17-27); re-running is safe
- Settlement repository saveMany/deleteMany do not bump the settlements revision counter that FirestoreSettlementWriter relies on (FirestoreLedgerRepositories.kt:37-39)
- Restore writes settlements row by row without re-running prepareSettlement; it relies on checkBackupFinance over the merged data (FullBackup.kt:171-175)

### countries
Two-country lane, branch r051-int (0.5.5), read-only review, 46 tool calls. The core model is sound: each country is a separate space with its own currency, payday and time zone, cross-country transfers store no rate, and no place I opened adds SAR to EGP without an explicit rate. I found no critical wrong-money bug.

The real problems are at the edges:
- **UTC dates:** about a dozen use cases still take "today" from the UTC clock instead of the country day, so anything done between midnight and 02:00/03:00 local gets yesterday's date. With payday 28 that can land in the previous fiscal month.
- **Exchange rate:** the price job fetches only USD_SAR, never USD_EGP. The "both countries" total is therefore always "unavailable", and the owner-decided "today's rate" prefill for paying a debt in the other currency always comes up empty.
- **Bank cap:** the 25-per-country limit is enforced only as 50 in total on one device-wide list, and auto-add stops silently at the cap.
- **Archived country:** unlinking a self-transfer while one country is archived leaves that country's leg as a confirmed internal transfer with no pair.

Smaller items cover the background cycle using the phone's date, the "recorded today" list, Egypt's payday silently following Saudi's, and hard-coded Cairo DST rules.

**Strengths:**
- native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/CountryTime.kt: day boundaries per country in pure integer code with one todayInCountry entry point. I checked the Cairo DST arithmetic by hand (last Friday of April 00:00 to last Thursday of October 24:00) and it is correct, including the missing midnight on the start day.
- native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/SpaceTransfers.kt: a cross-country transfer keeps each leg in its own currency, stores no exchange rate, and computes the display rate by integer long division. checkSpaceTransfer blocks same-country pairs, wrong directions, currency/wallet mismatches and double linking; the write is atomic through SpaceTransferWriter.
- native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/FxSettle.kt: settlement in the other currency uses an integer rate scaled by 10^4, parses the rate without Double, rejects invalid input as null rather than zero, and records the paid amount and rate on the settlement.
- native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/Spaces.kt: SPACE_GROUPS is derived as 'everything not explicitly account-level', so a new collection defaults to being isolated per country. Space ids are deterministic (eg), so two devices creating the same country write the same document. Totals I saw are per space and per currency (wiring/ShellGraph.kt:94).
- native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/SmsArrivalDay.kt with OperationTime.kt: dateless SMS take the arrival day in the country's zone, with a 15-minute skew margin, an 8-hour overnight rule, and a 'wait for confirmation' gate when written and arrival clocks disagree. The hidden time is stored only when the local day matches.

**Not checked:**
- Firestore layout per country (firestore/FirestoreSpace.kt, FirestoreSpaces.kt) was not opened; for firestore.rules I only saw that users/{uid}/{document=**} covers the spaces subcollections, not the rule body
- Leaks between countries on active-country switch (in-memory caches, feedCache, the global Texts.followCountry static used while background lanes of the other country run) were not traced
- Whether one SMS can parse as OK in both the Saudi and Egyptian readers and be recorded in both countries: I read the guards (BankSmsParser.kt:190, EgyptBankSms.kt:111, 115) but not saudiAmount/egyptAmount or routeSender, so no concrete double-record case is confirmed
- Shared merchants and people across countries (SpaceMerchants.kt, AcrossSpaces.kt, PeopleOverview.kt, RepairOpeningDebtCurrency) were not opened; per-currency debt lines are claimed in comments only
- Category delta for Egypt (CategoryDelta.kt) and backup version 3 restore mapping (FullBackupSpaces.kt, BackupSpaces.kt) were not opened
- Date parsing from SMS text (SmsDates.kt saudiTransactionDate, egyptTransactionDate, dd/mm vs mm/dd ambiguity) was not opened; only the dateless path (SmsArrivalDay.kt) was read
- ManageProjects.kt:132 builds the 'from' date by subtracting years from the string; on 29 February this may give an invalid date, depending on OLD_YEARS and listByDateRange, neither of which I checked
- DuplicateChargeAsks.kt:37 uses a variable named utcToday; not opened, so I do not know whether the UTC day is deliberate there
- Which background use cases actually read env.today (device date) was not traced
- Whether callers of connectKnownBanks warn the user when fewer senders were connected than known
- SpaceTransferSheet 'live rate' display and SettleFxPanel UI were not opened
- The iOS side (IosLocalFiles and others) and the tests (CountryTimeTest) were not opened; nothing was built or run

**Other titles:**
- Manual space-transfer legs store rawMerchantName = uiText(KIND_INTERNAL_TRANSFER) in the active country's dialect for both countries (TransferBetweenSpaces.kt:122, with the global Texts.followCountry at AppContainer.kt:150)
- lastReadWhen uses the device zone offset (AndroidBackgroundRead.kt:27), not the country zone; display only
- Catch-up (SmsCatchUp) and SmsHistory take settings from the first space's repositories (BackgroundWiring.kt:76, 94-96); correct only while those settings are truly account-level
- egpPerSar4 rounds to 4 decimals before conversion (InvestFutureMath.kt:195-198): double rounding, negligible at current magnitudes

### arch
Lane: architecture and layer boundaries (branch r051-int, 0.5.5). Read-only, 39 tool calls.

Real module graph (from each build.gradle.kts): core <- app <- {data, device, ui}; data <- firestore; ui <- wiring; androidApp -> wiring + firestore + device. This matches the intended inward direction. core has zero platform/Firebase/Compose/coroutines imports; ui has zero imports of firestore/data/device/memory and only 12 imports from the port package (all value types); no use case imports a memory adapter. UI money math is essentially absent (4 arithmetic hits on minor amounts, all steppers or display percentages). File-size rule holds (4 of ~1300 production files over 300 lines, all text tables or seed JSON). So the layering itself is in good shape and will hold as features grow.

The weak spots are not the layers but what flows around them. The three biggest structural risks:
1) Threading has no default: screens call use cases from LaunchedEffect on the UI thread and only 6 of 220 use-case files (and 14 of 155 screen files) move work off it. Every new feature repeats the choice by hand, and the SMS and backup areas already had freezes from this.
2) Hidden global state: mutable singletons (Texts dialect, MoneyPrivacy, PeriodChoice, SmsHandoff, ImportDrafts, Problems, Usage) act as invisible dependencies. The worst case is the hide-numbers flag living inside core formatMoney as a default argument, so text that use cases build and SAVE (assistant replies) can be stored masked.
3) State and logic sitting in the wrong lifetime: navigation/shell state is created per Activity and never saved; SmsGraph in the composition module holds real orchestration and mutable session state; a UI class commits deferred writes on a detached scope and drops failures.

iOS targets are real at the core/app/data/device level (compiled and tested on a macOS CI runner) but there is no iOS app entry point; jvm target is real (it is where the tests run). firebase-trial is a standalone, unreferenced Gradle build. In-memory adapters live in app/commonMain; production wiring uses only MemorySyncCursor from them.

**Strengths:**
- Dependency direction is genuinely clean: core/src/commonMain has zero android, Firebase, Compose, java or coroutines imports, and ui has zero imports of firestore, data, device or memory packages (native-app/ui/build.gradle.kts:37 depends only on :app).
- Screens receive use cases only, through one small interface per area (native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/app/Deps.kt:33-50); ui imports just 12 symbols from the port package, all value types, and almost no arithmetic on amounts exists in ui.
- Manual composition is split per area with a fixed root (native-app/wiring/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/wiring/SpaceGraph.kt:15-39) and is tested on the JVM with in-memory repositories (native-app/wiring/src/jvmTest: WiringSmokeTest.kt, MemorySpaceRepositories.kt and area tests).
- File-size discipline holds at scale: only 4 of about 1300 production Kotlin files exceed 300 lines, and they are text tables and seed data (for example native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/UiKeys.kt).
- Navigation without a library is small and predictable (native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/nav/Navigator.kt, 179 lines) and screen logic sits in plain Kotlin presenters testable without Compose (native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/shell/ask/AskState.kt:48, AskPresenter).

**Not checked:**
- Whether R8 in the 'fast' build actually strips the unused in-memory adapters and seed JSON from the shipped binary (no build was run).
- Whether ReviewSmsInbox.recordAll (called from SmsGraph.record) takes the same process-wide SMS_RECORD_LOCK as AutoRecordSms (AutoRecordSms.kt:72); if not, a screen 'record all' and the background cycle could overlap.
- Whether the assistant chat re-formats amounts at display time; the stored-masked-text finding (arch-2) was traced through code, not reproduced on a device.
- One-to-one match between the 9 throwing port delete defaults and the 10 Firestore overrides, and which memory adapters lack the override.
- Whether minorAsInput is ever called with a negative amount.
- How CashQuestionRow renders when the hook is null (disabled or active) and what AppSettingsScreen shows for the missing appLanguage hook.
- The exact task list on line 64 of native-core.yml (output was cut): whether ui, wiring and firestore are compiled for iOS in CI.
- The data module (19 files) and the firestore LocalMirror write path were not opened beyond function names; consistency of local mirror versus server writes belongs to the data lane.
- Whether rememberSaveable values across the 99 call sites are all bundle-safe on Android (process-death restore path).
- The remaining 45 nullable hooks were checked only by a name-based grep for 'override val <name>' in wiring; hooks wired under a different construct could be misreported, and hooks overridden with null were not detected.
- firebase-trial contents were only listed (17 tracked files, not in settings.gradle.kts); its files were not opened for secrets.

**Other titles:**
- firebase-trial is a standalone Gradle build tracked in the public repo (17 files), not included in native-app/settings.gradle.kts, yet still compiled by CI (native-core.yml:89): dead weight to remove or archive.
- ManageProjects is constructed twice with separate cursors (OperationsGraph.kt:68-70 and PeopleGraph.kt:98): duplicate use-case instances across area graphs.
- Dozens of build-*.log and *.stamp files sit in native-app and the repo root working tree (not tracked by git): clutter that can be swept.
- AppContainer mixes composition with behaviour (perf sign-in helper and an unsigned emulator token builder at AppContainer.kt:140-145 and 224-232); gated by a build constant but better in an emulator-only source set.
- Goal and budget progress percentages are computed in ui presenters (budgets/GoalsPresenter.kt:76, budgets/CategoryBudgetPresenter.kt:143) rather than returned by the use case.
- ui error handling leans on runCatching(...).getOrNull() (314 uses in ui): failures become 'not available' with no record, which hides real faults from the problem log.

### errors
Lane: concurrency, lifecycle, error handling (branch r051-int, 0.5.5). Overall the coroutine hygiene is good: no GlobalScope or runBlocking in production, one app-wide SupervisorJob scope, CancellationException is rethrown consistently, and the background SMS worker and the open UI share one global mutex for SMS recording. The real weaknesses are at the edges where failures are caught but never reach the user. (1) Firestore writes are fire-and-forget and the failure channels (FirestoreSpace.writeFailures, FirestoreSync.errors) have no collector anywhere, so a server-rejected save is shown as saved and later disappears. (2) The session-opening coroutine has no error handling and the app scope has no CoroutineExceptionHandler, so a failed read there kills the process at startup. (3) The first-open screen can wait forever with no error, retry or offline message. (4) The main add-operation sheet and about 19 other launches are unguarded although a failureOf helper exists. (5) The background cycle swallows nine steps without logging and the worker reports success when the session was not ready. Counts: 615 catch/runCatching occurrences in 222 production files; `catch (_: Exception)` that drops the error: app 15, ui 5, device 2; `!!`: core 82, ui 57, app 43; lateinit only 4 (androidApp). User-typed amounts are parsed defensively (tryParseMoney returning Invalid). Nothing found that produces wrong money by itself; the silent-write-failure path is the one that can make a recorded operation vanish.

**Strengths:**
- No GlobalScope and no runBlocking in production code; one app-wide CoroutineScope(SupervisorJob()) in native-app/androidApp/src/main/kotlin/app/masroufy/android/AppContainer.kt:67 and rememberCoroutineScope in screens
- The background SMS worker and the open UI cannot record the same message twice: one global SMS_RECORD_LOCK (native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/usecase/ReviewSmsInbox.kt:37) is taken per page by auto-record (AutoRecordSms.kt:170), by manual review (ReviewSmsInbox.kt:152) and by foreign-currency answers (ForeignSmsAsks.kt:131), and the SMS is acknowledged only after the save
- CancellationException is rethrown consistently before the generic catch (RunBackgroundCycle.kt:149-155, FirestoreSync.kt:92-93, SharedMerchantsLink.kt:34-35, BackgroundWork.kt:98-99), so cancellation is never swallowed
- WorkManager setup is sound: unique work with APPEND_OR_REPLACE so an SMS arriving mid-cycle is not lost, expedited with non-expedited fallback, bounded retries (MAX_ATTEMPTS = 3), periodic KEEP so reopening does not reset the schedule, and no SMS text ever logged (native-app/device/src/androidMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/device/BackgroundWork.kt:66-86, 118)
- A failed Firestore listener marks its group stale so screens stop reading an outdated in-memory copy instead of showing wrong totals (FirestoreSync.kt:94-99 with LocalMirror.kt:71-84), and crashes are recorded locally then sanitised before reporting (CrashReports.kt:79-91)

**Not checked:**
- Did not reproduce any crash or hang on a device or emulator (no builds allowed); errors-2 and errors-4 are code-path analysis, the exact triggers were not exercised
- Whether the Firestore SDK has durably persisted a fire-and-forget write before the worker returns Result.success and the process is frozen or killed (write() does not await the commit)
- SmsInboxStore internals: BankSmsReceiver opens a new SmsInboxStore(context) per message while a shared singleton also exists (SmsInboxStore.kt:225-229); file-level locking between the two instances was not read
- Races between background repairs/duplicate scans/subscription catch-up and a user editing the same transaction in the open UI (only the SMS path and person links have mutexes); individual repair use cases were not read
- The 82 `!!` in core, 57 in ui, 43 in app and the ~190 first()/single()/getValue() calls were counted, not audited one by one
- Whether OVERRIDES section 85 (state kept when leaving a screen) was meant to cover process death; the section was not opened
- DocCodec decode behaviour on malformed or legacy documents from the old React app beyond the single error() found in InheritanceCodecs.kt
- iOS targets (iosMain) and the firebase-trial module were not reviewed
- Behaviour under battery saver / manufacturer task killers (Xiaomi, Huawei) and after reboot was reasoned from the WorkManager configuration only, not tested
- Dispatchers used by individual use cases and whether any heavy read runs on the main thread from LaunchedEffect (only the background cycle's withContext(deps.work) was confirmed)

**Other titles:**
- AppContainer.ready() launches a shared-merchants sync and a usage snapshot every time the Ready state re-emits (space switch, refreshSpaces), not once per app open (androidApp/.../AppContainer.kt:155-160)
- AppContainer.start() launches wipeLegacyExternal with no try/catch on the app scope (AppContainer.kt:106) - same uncaught-exception path as errors-2 if it ever throws
- MasroufyBackground.uninstall() exists for sign-out but nothing in production calls it; safety relies on auth.currentUser() == null in backgroundCycle()
- requestRun()/schedulePeriodic() catch only IllegalStateException from WorkManager (BackgroundWork.kt:72, 83)
- InheritanceCodecs.kt:80 decode calls error(it) on an unknown enum value: one bad document makes every read of that group throw

### perf
Performance review of ROOT/native-app (r051-int, 0.5.5), read-only, 46 tool calls. The SMS path that caused the reported 4-5 s freeze and the white settings page has been genuinely reworked (paged, off the main thread, cached settings view, scale tests). What remains is a different class of problem: the Home tab, part of the Operations tab, the People tab and the operation detail screen still run their repository reads on the UI thread. Because every read is a full scan + sort of an in-memory mirror (no date index), these costs grow linearly with history and are repeated on every write, every remote change and every pull-to-refresh. Home additionally does the same heavy reads twice (screen + cards). Nothing found that produces wrong money. All millisecond figures below are my estimates from reading the code (no build or device run was allowed), so they need confirming with the existing MPERF trace on the emulator with the owner's data set.

**Strengths:**
- SMS recording is paged by cursor with a lock per page and a 'new only' memo, all on the work dispatcher (app/usecase/AutoRecordSms.kt:137-200); pull-to-refresh now calls only recordNew off the UI thread (OperationsScreen.kt:117-118)
- The SMS settings page emits Loading, then the cached view, then a light fresh view without previews (ui/screens/imports/BankSmsSettingsModel.kt:26-32 with SmsGraph.settings at wiring/SmsGraph.kt:142-150) - this directly addresses the white page
- LocalMirror caches the decoded entity per document version and makes concurrent readers wait for the one listener download instead of issuing parallel server queries (firestore/LocalMirror.kt:33-44 and 91-98)
- mirrorIn answers large id lists in one pass with a value table instead of a scan and sort per 30 ids (firestore/DocQuery.kt:75-85), and scale tests assert bounded work per page (app/src/jvmTest/.../SmsConfirmScaleTest.kt:107-111)
- Bundled seed JSON is parsed lazily and once, not at startup (app/seed/BundledSeeds.kt:26-29); remote changes are debounced before reloading screens (ui/app/MasroufyApp.kt:73)

**Not checked:**
- All millisecond figures are estimates from code reading; nothing was built or run. They should be confirmed with the existing MPERF spans (screen:home, home:withYouNow, screen:operations, ops:zone) on the emulator build with a 3,000-transaction / 5,000-SMS data set.
- Startup: AppContainer construction (openFirebase, crash and usage setup) runs synchronously in Application.onCreate (MasroufyApplication.kt:21-34); I did not open openFirebase, ProblemLogSetup or AccountSession.start, so cold-start cost is not assessed.
- Memory: the mirror holds every synced group for the whole account as raw maps plus decoded entities. I did not open the group list, so I could not confirm whether source records (which hold full SMS bodies) are mirrored, nor estimate heap at 5,000 SMS.
- Number of Firestore listeners (a comment in DocQuery.kt:89 mentions 57) and their cost on first sync were not measured.
- Device SMS store: SmsInboxStore.kt uses a LIMIT query ordered by received DESC (line 195) and a COUNT/MIN query (line 199); I did not check whether the table has an index on (owner, done, received) or how big a page is.
- Bank and merchant logo loading (BankLogo.android.kt uses bundled WebP drawables) was not opened; no network image loading library appeared in the code map.
- Compose-level issues (unstable parameters, missing remember/derivedStateOf inside row composables such as OperationListRow, AmountText formatting per recomposition) were not inspected beyond OperationsScreen and HomeScreen.
- data/src/jvmTest/.../ScaleTest.kt was found by name but not opened, so what it asserts is unknown.
- LoadCalendar, the budgets/dues slots, Investment and Assistant screens were not reviewed; several of them call listByDateRange over long windows (e.g. AdvisorMoreSignals.kt:91 reads 13 months).
- Statement/PDF import preview and the Dedupe index (core/Dedupe.kt) were not opened; quadratic behaviour there is unverified.
- Whether perf-8's per-person queries are also hit by ManagePeopleRow.kt:52-54 and LoadDues.kt:78-81 on the UI thread was seen in grep output only, not traced to their callers.

**Other titles:**
- smsStatementDuplicates is quadratic (core/SmsRepairRules.kt:57-61: every SMS-only move filters all statement moves) - cheap comparisons, about 3M at 2,000 x 1,500, repair screen only
- Operations list puts all rows of a day in one LazyColumn item (OperationsScreen.kt:227-241) and recomputes visibleIds with flatMap on every recomposition (line 125) - fine at about 150 rows per month, would matter for an 'all time' filter
- RunAlertEngine.run re-reads inbox.listAll() inside the candidate loop (RunAlertEngine.kt:96) - N small scans per engine run
- BankSmsR05.flushDetached launches on a new unowned CoroutineScope (BankSmsR05.kt:84) - cannot be cancelled or awaited, minor leak risk
- SmsGraph.overview builds a separate ReviewSmsInbox and inbox refresh per sender (SmsGraph.kt:116-122) - the page is re-read and re-parsed once per bank; bounded by page size and off the UI thread
- FirestoreTransactionRepository.listByDateRange sorts an already sorted result a second time (FirestoreTransactionRepository.kt:21)

### release
Release readiness, version 0.5.5 on branch r051-int: the app is fine for the current owner-plus-brother sideload, but it is not ready for the store and should not go to a wider tester group until four things are closed. (1) There is no privacy policy anywhere, in the app or in the repo. (2) There is no store-signable build: the only minified variant is signed with the debug key. (3) Analytics run with no notice and no switch, and crash reports default on with the account id. (4) "Delete my account" has never been run against Firebase and has no web link.

The READ_SMS / RECEIVE_SMS request is allowed in principle. Google's policy page, fetched today, lists an "SMS-based money management" exception covering READ_SMS and RECEIVE_SMS, subject to the Permissions Declaration Form and Google review. Approval is not guaranteed, and the in-app disclosure text ("on your phone") does not match the code comments that say redacted bank text is stored and restored. targetSdk 36 meets the current rule (API 36 required since 2026-08-31, confirmed today); minSdk 24 is fine.

ORDER BEFORE MORE TESTERS:
1. Create the real release key, back it up in two places, register its fingerprints for Google sign-in, and move the owner and his brother to it now (release-6).
2. Write and host the privacy policy (Arabic and English) and link it on the sign-in screen and in Settings (release-1).
3. Add one honest line and a switch for usage data, or get an explicit recorded owner decision to accept the legal risk (release-4).
4. Test "delete my account" on the Firebase emulator, including the "needs recent login" path (release-8).
5. Correct the SMS permission sheet wording to say what leaves the phone (release-3).
6. Add the inheritance disclaimer (release-11).

ORDER BEFORE THE STORE:
7. Fix the application id, version name and version code scheme (release-7).
8. Build a real release variant: R8, release signing, an app bundle, Play App Signing, and lint back on (release-5).
9. Check the built bundle for 16 KB page-size alignment.
10. Fill the Play Console forms: SMS Permissions Declaration (money-management exception) with a demo, Data safety, Financial features declaration, content rating, and reviewer login credentials (release-2, release-9).
11. Publish a web page for account and data deletion and put its link in the listing (release-8).
12. Prepare a fallback variant without SMS permissions (paste and statement import only) in case the declaration is rejected (release-2).
13. Run the closed test Google requires for new personal developer accounts (from memory: 12 testers for 14 days; not confirmed today).
14. Do a TalkBack and large-font pass on the main flows.

**Strengths:**
- SMS intake is narrowly scoped: the receiver is protected by BROADCAST_SMS, ignores everything unless the inbox is enabled and the sender is on the user's list, and never logs message text (native-app/device/src/androidMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/device/BankSmsReceiver.kt:17-44, manifest line 9). This is exactly the evidence Google asks for in the SMS declaration.
- Advertising surface is fully closed: the ad id permissions are removed and ad id, SSAID, ad personalisation and ad storage are disabled in the manifest and again in code (native-app/androidApp/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:7-9, 29-38; FirebaseUsage.kt:35-44), which keeps the Data safety form simple.
- Crash reporting is privacy-conscious: collection is off in the manifest until the app decides, every error passes through a sanitizer that drops messages, no free-text logs, unsent reports are deleted when switched off, and the uid is cleared on sign-out (native-app/androidApp/src/main/kotlin/app/masroufy/android/CrashReports.kt:42-66).
- Platform levels are current: targetSdk 36 and compileSdk 36 meet the Play rule in force since 2026-08-31 (confirmed today), minSdk 24, allowBackup is false so financial data is not copied by device backup (native-app/androidApp/build.gradle.kts:24-29; AndroidManifest.xml:22).
- A pre-permission explanation sheet with a decline path to manual paste already exists, and the zakat feature carries a clear 'guidance, not a fatwa' line with named sources (ui/screens/imports/BankSmsSettingsParts.kt:85-98; core/TextsZakat.kt:18).

**Not checked:**
- Whether redacted SMS text really reaches Firestore: I read the comments (SmsTransferParties.kt:6, CODEMAP line 761) but did not open the Firestore source-record mapper
- 16 KB page-size support: no .so files are tracked and the direct dependencies look JVM-only, but Compose and Firebase can bring native libraries transitively; needs a check on a built bundle (no build was run)
- Play requirement for new personal developer accounts (closed test, 12 testers, 14 days) and the Financial features declaration: from memory, not re-confirmed today
- Whether Play still rejects debug-signed uploads and requires a demo video for the SMS declaration: the fetched policy page did not mention a video
- Exact consent and notice obligations under Saudi PDPL and Egypt's Law 151 of 2020, and cross-border transfer rules for Firebase hosting region: not researched; needs a lawyer or a focused review
- Notification permission (POST_NOTIFICATIONS) rationale screen and the onboarding permission flow: not opened
- Whether a crash-report switch is actually visible in Settings (the port has setOn; the screen was not opened)
- Accessibility in practice: 199 semantics / contentDescription uses across 87 ui files were counted, but touch target sizes, TalkBack order and large-font layouts were not inspected
- Arabic and right-to-left quality on real screens (mixed-direction amounts, mirrored icons, Google Fonts download fallback when Play services are missing): not inspected
- Which on-device stores (pending SMS inbox, device backups, preferences) are lost on the forced reinstall at key change: not inventoried
- Whether the debug keystore is currently backed up anywhere
- firestore.rules and whether the google-services file or any key is in git history: only current tracked files were listed (none found); history was not searched
- The firebase-trial module's manifest and whether its ids are registered in the production Firebase project

**Other titles:**
- English is locked with a 'soon' label (ui/screens/more/AppSettingsScreen.kt:146-147): store listing must be Arabic-only for now, and Play reviewers will need written Arabic navigation steps plus a test login
- Lint is disabled for release builds (androidApp/build.gradle.kts:98), so missing-translation and RTL lint checks never run
- Fixed 44dp control heights (ui/screens/imports/BankSmsSettingsParts.kt:71-72) combined with sp-scaled text (ui/theme/Theme.kt:49) may clip at large font sizes; not tested
- Crash reports keep the Firebase uid after account deletion; retention must be stated in the policy (CrashReports.kt:64)
- HANDOVER.md:659 gives a package name for adb commands that does not match the real application id

### tests
Lane: tests and quality gates (branch r051-int, 0.5.5). The test suite itself is good: about 3,020 @Test functions in git (core 1048, app 1042, ui 563, data 123, wiring 79, device 96, firestore 73), of which 78 are Android instrumented; that matches the claimed ~2,937 JVM tests. The 20+ files I sampled assert real behaviour (amounts in minor units, exact Arabic strings, rollback to zero, golden parity with the old app), not mirrors of the implementation. The acceptance totals and the balance chain from CLAUDE.md ARE proven in Kotlin (RealStatementTest), but only on the owner's PC. The weak part is the gates around the tests: (1) GitHub CI has been red since 2026-10-10 (last green 2026-10-09; 8 of the last 40 runs failed, the last 5 in a row) because core commonMain uses a JVM-only call, so the iOS step fails, and HANDOVER does not mention it; (2) CI only runs core/app/data/device JVM tests, never ui, wiring, firestore, nor an Android build; (3) the 78 instrumented tests, which are the only proof that the Firestore and device adapters work, are run by hand, by class filter, against an emulator with open rules; (4) there is no contract suite shared by memory and Firestore adapters; (5) no detekt/ktlint, release lint is switched off, secret scan is a manual script on a public repo; (6) the release smoke is described in HANDOVER prose and refers to smoke/speed.sh which is not in the repo. Note: r051-int is only one docs-only commit (OVERRIDES.md) ahead of the pushed branch origin/claude/masroufy-kotlin-setup-c02bd8, so CI does see the real code; it is just failing.

**Strengths:**
- Acceptance criteria from CLAUDE.md are encoded exactly in Kotlin: native-app/core/src/jvmTest/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/RealStatementTest.kt asserts the four totals, 1912 rows, closing 2,707.25 @ 2026-09-04 and zero per-line balance mismatches; RealPdfStatementTest and data RealDataFlowTest repeat the chain through the PDF reader and the import flow.
- Golden parity with the old app is rigorous: native-app/core/src/jvmTest/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/support/Golden.kt fails on empty case lists and reports the first differing path; SmsGoldenTest.kt requires every divergence from the golden file to be listed by name with the owner decision, and fails if the list has one entry too many or too few.
- Source-scanning guard tests turn past incidents into permanent gates: firestore/src/jvmTest/.../UserCollectionsGuardTest.kt (account erase must cover every collection), app/src/jvmTest/.../perf/NoSecretsInLogsTest.kt (no token, password or exception message in any log call), ui/.../theme/HardcodedColorBudgetTest.kt (hard-coded colours may only go down).
- Use-case tests assert outcomes that matter with fixed clocks and sequential ids, not implementation details: app/src/jvmTest/.../usecase/ImportAtomicityTest.kt injects a failing repository and asserts that batch, source records and transactions are all rolled back to zero. No @Ignore or @Disabled anywhere in the Kotlin tests.
- The CI workflow, where it applies, refuses hollow success: .github/workflows/native-core.yml lines 46-59 count executed iOS tests from the XML and fail the run when fewer than 6 device tests or zero tests ran; it also runs with `permissions: contents: read` and no secrets.

**Not checked:**
- Did not run gradle: the 2,937 passing figure is taken from HANDOVER and from counting @Test annotations (about 3,020 including 78 instrumented), not from a test run.
- Only the latest failed CI run (40075627855) was inspected for its cause; the four earlier failures since 2026-10-10 may have had other causes as well. Only the first 10 compiler annotations were read, so there may be more JVM-only calls in commonMain besides AssistGuards.kt:106.
- Whether the rules deployed in the real Firebase project match ROOT/firestore.rules or the emulator-auth copy was not checked (no access to the project).
- scripts/secretscan.mjs was not opened: its patterns and whether it covers owner data (account numbers, statement lines) are unverified.
- Did not verify how rootProject.extra["ownerFiles"] is resolved in native-app/build.gradle.kts, so whether RealStatementTest actually runs from this worktree is unconfirmed.
- Where exactly the long-digit masking of notes happens on the Firestore path (codec in data vs adapter) was not opened; the memory/Firestore difference is inferred from MemoryRepositories.kt:49-62 versus the expectation in FirestoreTransactionRepositoryTest.kt:56.
- spec/06-acceptance.md cases on fixtures: did not map each acceptance case to a Kotlin test.
- Sampled about 22 of roughly 660 test files; app and ui test quality beyond the sample is extrapolated.
- prices.yml workflow and the iOS test sources (device/src/iosTest) were not read.
- Whether the many build-*.log files in the repo root and native-app are tracked in git or contain private data.

**Other titles:**
- Golden harness rewrites expected strings before comparing (plainEgyptian, senderLimit112, old riyal format in core/src/jvmTest/.../support/Golden.kt lines 42-49): owner-approved, but each new rewrite widens what a golden mismatch can hide
- Tests toggle the global Texts.arabicVariant and reset it in @AfterTest (e.g. SmsGoldenTest.kt:21-29, HomeModelTest.kt:22): safe only while test classes run sequentially in one JVM
- No coverage measurement (kover/jacoco) so 'where coverage is thin' cannot be stated as a number
- The 300-line file rule has no automated guard; 4 Kotlin main files exceed it
- CI has no pull_request trigger and uses only macos runners (slow queue) even for the JVM-only step
- LocalMirrorTest.kt:82 and FuturePngTest.kt:165-172 use real delay() polling in JVM tests
- Many build-*.log files sit in the repo root and native-app; worth checking they are ignored and contain no private data

### build
Static review of the native-app build configuration (branch r051-int, versionName kotlin-0.5.5, versionCode 12). The build is reproducible in the narrow sense (all versions pinned, no dynamic versions, no snapshot repositories, secrets kept out of the public repo, and nothing secret is tracked by git). The real weaknesses are around shipping: there is no store-ready variant at all (release is unminified and unsigned; the only variant people actually receive, "fast", is signed with the per-machine Android debug key and nothing in the build verifies that key), the toolchain is at its compatibility edge (Kotlin 2.4.20 on AGP 8.13.0 / Gradle 8.14.3: 5230 R8 "error parsing kotlin metadata" warnings and a recurring KGP "Gradle version deprecated" warning in the existing logs), CI never builds the Android app or runs the ui/wiring tests, and a clean machine can silently produce a "fast" APK with no Firebase inside. Note: gradle/libs.versions.toml named in the task does not exist; versions are hardcoded per module. Existing logs end in BUILD SUCCESSFUL (12m27s debug, 6m30s fast, 10m58s fastEmulator); only build-fast.log contains a failure (keepFastMapping implicit dependency), already fixed in androidApp/build.gradle.kts:152. I did not run gradle.

**Strengths:**
- Secrets stay out of the public repo by construction: google-services.json is looked up outside the tree and copied into the ignored build folder at build time (native-app/build.gradle.kts lines 49-51, androidApp/build.gradle.kts lines 171-181), and git ls-files shows no keystore, google-services or env file tracked
- Fully pinned inputs: no dynamic versions, no snapshot or custom repositories (native-app/settings.gradle.kts lines 3-14), wrapper pinned with validateDistributionUrl
- Emulator variants are cleanly isolated from real data: separate BuildConfig project id, crash reports and analytics off, cleartext allowed only for 10.0.2.2 and only in emulator source sets (androidApp/build.gradle.kts lines 43-49 and 69-85, src/emulator/res/xml/emulator_network.xml)
- Privacy defaults are enforced in the manifest, not only in code: analytics and Crashlytics collection off until the app decides, advertising-id permissions removed, allowBackup false (native-app/androidApp/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml)
- The R8 mapping file is archived per version and exception class names are kept, so crash reports remain readable even when the upload is skipped (androidApp/build.gradle.kts lines 146-154, proguard-rules.pro line 40); CI also guards against 'green but nothing ran' by counting executed iOS tests (.github/workflows/native-core.yml lines 46-59)

**Not checked:**
- I did not run gradle, so dependency resolution, actual resolved versions (for example which androidx.core wins) and task graphs are not confirmed
- Whether the build still configures on a machine with no Android SDK: settings.gradle.kts line 26 always includes :androidApp (com.android.application) while ui/wiring/device/firestore enable their Android target only when ANDROID_HOME or local.properties exists; I suspect any task then fails at configuration, but did not test it
- Whether the 5230 R8 kotlin-metadata warnings cause any real runtime difference in the fast APK (needs a device run of serialization-heavy paths)
- Exact supported-version matrix for Kotlin 2.4.20 / AGP 8.13.0 / Gradle 8.14.3 / Compose Multiplatform 1.11.1 and the AGP 9 Kotlin Multiplatform plugin change: stated from memory, must be verified against current documentation
- Known vulnerabilities for pdfbox-android 2.0.27.0, biometric 1.1.0, GitLive 2.7.0, Firebase BoM 34.18.0, credentials 1.5.0, googleid 1.1.1: not looked up
- Whether emulator and fastEmulator APKs built on the owner's machine also embed the real project's google-services resources and auto-initialize the default Firebase app (the plugin is applied to all variants at androidApp/build.gradle.kts line 157); I did not inspect an APK
- Whether a backup of ~/.android/debug.keystore exists outside the PC; nothing in the repo says so
- Contents of src/appCheckOn and src/appCheckOff, the device module manifest (SMS permissions), baseline-prof.txt (only 6 lines) and the Gradle problems report referenced at the end of each log
- Only the last 40 lines of build-merge.log, build-g.log and build-r051.log were read, plus pattern counts across all logs; the 579 Kotlin compiler warnings were not categorized beyond two recurring deprecations (LocalClipboard, updateFields)
- Unused dependencies: no dependency-analysis tooling exists and I did not trace imports, so none are claimed

**Other titles:**
- Gradle wrapper has no distributionSha256Sum and there is no dependency verification metadata (already recorded in PLAN-0.6.md line 166)
- No build performance settings in native-app/gradle.properties (Xmx2g only; no parallel, build cache or configuration cache) while logged builds take 6 to 12 minutes with most tasks up to date
- native-app/*.stamp build marker files are untracked but not gitignored (only native-app/*.log is), so they can be committed by accident
- The fast build needs network for uploadCrashlyticsMappingFileFast and fails without it unless -Pmasroufy.crashlyticsUpload=false is passed (androidApp/build.gradle.kts lines 159-168)
- proguard-rules.pro keeps all of dev.gitlive.firebase and com.google.firebase.firestore and disables obfuscation: safe, but it limits shrinking (about 26 MB APK per HANDOVER line 17)
- versionCode and versionName are bumped by hand in androidApp/build.gradle.kts lines 30-31 with no check that the code increased, unlike the old app script
- iOS targets are compiled and tested in CI for core/app/data/device/firestore although no iOS app module exists yet; cost is CI minutes only
- Stale comment in data/build.gradle.kts line 4 says the module is still without GitLive
- firebase-trial is a separate Gradle build with its own duplicated plugin versions and is still built in CI

### org
Did not run.



# جولة الأمان التانية (2026-11-14) — 4 مسارات، كل ملاحظة راجعها مراجع تاني

## rules-deep

- not checked: Read-only, 25 tool calls, nothing built or run. Coverage of paths: every non-test collection()/document() call in native-app/firestore resolves to users/{uid}/... (root or spaces/{id}), sharedMerchants/{key} or inbox/{id}; all three are covered by firestore.rules, no collectionGroup query, no Firebase Storage or Realtime Database use, no anonymous sign-in found. No invites, shared spaces or cross-user support threads exist in the firestore module, so there is nothing to review there; the price feed does not go through Firestore. NOT checked: (1) the live rules in the Firebase console versus the repo file; (2) the newline bypass of the digit rule was reasoned from regex semantics, not executed on the emulator; (3) the exact transaction-matching code path that consumes merchantIndex aliases; (4) how feedback ids are generated (ids.next) and whether they are guessable, which would let a stranger pre-create inbox/{id} and get a victim's message refused; (5) whether uid/spaceId values passed to FirestoreSpace can ever contain a slash or come from untrusted input; (6) UserCollections.kt and the guard test content; (7) the old web app under src/ (my one search of src/scripts/tools for non-user collections found only the feedback tool); (8) the service account's IAM role and the private feedback repository settings; (9) firestore.indexes.json; (10) field-level validation under users/{uid}/** (no size/type limits, already reported as sec-firebase-2).

### rules-deep-1 - Shared merchant aliases are attacker-controlled and are used as match keys on every phone: one stranger can capture popular shop names
- severity: low (finder said medium) | verdict: weakened | urgency: before_release
- where: `firestore.rules:22-31 and native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/Categorize.kt:86-90`
- evidence: firestore.rules:22-31 and :48 only check that aliases is a list of up to 10 strings of 1-80 chars; nothing ties an alias to the entry's own name, and the update rule (firestore.rules:15-17) lets any signed-in account rewrite aliases on any unconfirmed entry. Client: core/SharedMerchants.kt:147-152 (planSharedSuggestions) creates a new account Merchant for each unconfirmed entry and copies entry.aliases into it with no check (same at :117-122 for confirmed). core/Categorize.kt:88 puts every alias into merchantIndex as a lookup key. core/SharedMerchants.kt:142-145 then looks up later entries through that same index, so a genuine entry for the captured name finds the attacker's merchant as 'existing' and only fills it instead of adding the real one.
- impact: A self-registered account writes one junk entry whose 10 aliases are well-known shop names. Every account that does not yet have those shops gets a merchant with the attacker's display name and category suggestion answering to those names, written into the user's own merchants collection, and the real shared entry no longer creates its own merchant. This is cross-user data tampering inside private ledgers, not only noise. sec-firebase-1 mentioned aliases only in its fix line; the capture mechanism through merchantIndex is new. Not traced: the exact transaction-matching call that reads merchantIndex.
- fix: Client (no owner decision needed): never copy aliases from an unconfirmed entry; for confirmed ones drop any alias that collides with another entry's name. Rules: on update require request.resource.data.aliases == resource.data.aliases and displayName unchanged, and on create require aliases.size() == 0 (aliases then come only from the console).
- second reviewer: The code is as described: core/SharedMerchants.kt:147-152 copies aliases from unconfirmed entries unchecked, Categorize.kt:88 indexes them, and firestore.rules:15-17,22-31 lets any signed-in account set them. The effect is smaller than claimed: an unconfirmed entry only yields a SUGGESTED category from the app's own tree, applied after the account's rules (Categorize.kt:137-143), and a merchant's own name beats any alias (Categorize.kt:89), so shops the user already has cannot be captured. A later genuine entry still fills the correct category (SharedMerchants.kt:112-115, 142-145), leaving the attacker's display name and a wrong suggestion as the residue. The client-side fix is still worth doing.
- OWNER DECISION NEEDED: Whether aliases should ever come from users, or only from the owner's console confirmations.

### rules-deep-2 - The 'no 4 digits in a row' rule is narrower than the app's own filter and looks bypassable with a line break; the phone does not re-check what it downloads
- severity: low (finder said medium) | verdict: weakened | urgency: before_release
- where: `firestore.rules:35-37`
- evidence: firestore.rules:36 uses matches('.*[0-9 + Arabic-Indic + Eastern-Arabic]{4,}.*'). The app's filter is wider: core/SharedMerchants.kt:25 uses \p{N}{4,} (any Unicode digit). So full-width or other-script digits pass the rule. Also matches() is a whole-string match and '.' does not cross a newline in RE2, so a value containing a line break before the digits should make the pattern fail and noLongDigits return true (from reading, NOT run on the emulator; rules-test has no such case). Digits split by a space or dash pass both. On the way in, FirestoreAccountAdapters.kt:185-195 (entryOf) and core/SharedMerchants.kt:147-152 accept displayName and aliases as they are: no length, digit or control-character check on downloaded entries. displayName may also be empty (firestore.rules:47 has no lower bound).
- impact: The rule exists to keep phone and account numbers out of a collection every user reads (project rule 11). A hand-written client can still publish such numbers, or any 80-character text, into displayName/aliases, and it lands in other users' merchant lists. If the deployed rules are still the older copy without this function (the file comment at :33 says not deployed; see sec-firebase-9), there is no server check at all and the client is the only defence, and it has none on read.
- fix: Rules: replace with a positive whitelist, e.g. d.displayName.matches('^[\\p{L}\\p{M} .&\'-]{1,80}$') plus an explicit digit-run check using (?s) so newlines cannot hide digits; same for aliases and normalizedName. Client: run shareableName-style validation (length, \p{N}{4,}, no control characters) on every downloaded entry before it becomes a Merchant. Add emulator test cases for newline, full-width digits and spaced digits.
- second reviewer: The gap is real: firestore.rules:36 covers only three digit ranges while core/SharedMerchants.kt:25 uses \p{N}, and FirestoreAccountAdapters.kt:185-195 applies no length or digit check on download. The newline bypass is plausible from RE2 semantics but was not run, and rules.test.mjs has no case for it. The honest app already filters before upload (SharedMerchants.kt:28-32, 92), so only a hand-written client can publish digits it chose itself; the harm is junk text in other users' merchant lists, the same class as rules-deep-1. The firestore.rules:33 comment says this block is not deployed, so it is hardening rather than a live hole.

### rules-deep-3 - Update rule accepts a no-change rewrite: one account can 'touch' every unconfirmed shared merchant so all phones re-download the whole list each session
- severity: low (finder said medium) | verdict: weakened | urgency: before_release
- where: `firestore.rules:15-17`
- evidence: firestore.rules:15-17 allows update whenever the stored entry is unconfirmed and the new data is valid; firestore.rules:51 forces updatedAt == request.time, and nothing requires that any field actually changed. FirestoreAccountAdapters.kt:149-152 downloads every entry whose updatedAt is after the last sync, with no limit. So re-saving existing entries unchanged moves them all past every client's cursor.
- impact: Materially deeper than sec-firebase-1: no new junk is needed and adding a page limit alone does not stop it. With N unconfirmed entries, one pass of N writes by a stranger costs N reads on every phone at its next start; repeated daily it eats the shared 50,000 reads/day (and the stranger's writes eat the shared 20,000 writes/day). Legitimate entries are enough fuel once the catalogue grows.
- fix: Rules: require a real change on update, e.g. request.resource.data.categoryId != resource.data.categoryId, and freeze normalizedName/displayName/aliases on update. Client: page the changed-since query and stop after a fixed number of reads per day; sync unconfirmed entries at most once a day instead of every session start.
- second reviewer: Confirmed in code: firestore.rules:15-17 and :51 accept a no-change rewrite that bumps updatedAt, and FirestoreAccountAdapters.kt:149-152 reads everything after the cursor with no limit. At the current scale of an owner plus a few testers, N attacker writes cost N reads per phone, so the attacker exhausts the 20,000 writes/day before the 50,000 reads/day unless several phones sync. The proposed 'require a real change' rule does not stop it, since flipping categoryId back and forth passes; the client-side page limit and once-a-day sync are the effective fix.

### rules-deep-4 - Feedback robot reads the whole unconfirmed catalogue every 3 hours with the admin key and pastes stranger-written text into GitHub issues
- severity: low (finder said medium) | verdict: weakened | urgency: before_release
- where: `tools/feedback-action/inbox-to-issues.mjs:93`
- evidence: inbox-to-issues.mjs:93 runs sharedMerchants where confirmed == false with no limit; feedback.yml:9 schedules it every 3 hours (8 runs a day). Admin reads count against the same project quota. Lines 64-67 put inbox text (up to 2000 chars, firestore.rules:68, written by any signed-in account) straight into the issue title and body; lines 111-114 put displayName into a title and inside ** ** markup. No escaping of markdown, links, images or @ mentions. Inbox is drained oldest-first at 100 per run (lines 21, 58). Line 103-108 keeps a state entry per document id and feedback.yml:44-46 commits that file on every change.
- impact: Roughly 6,000 junk unconfirmed entries make the robot alone use the full 50,000 reads/day, stopping reads for all users, before any phone syncs. A stranger can also open up to about 800 issues a day in the owner's feedback repository, each able to @-mention arbitrary GitHub users from the owner's repository and carry links or remote images; genuine messages wait behind the junk because the queue is first-in-first-out. The state file and its commit history grow with attacker-chosen document ids.
- fix: Add .limit() and an updatedAt cursor to the line-93 query, and run that half once a day. Before posting: wrap user text in a fenced code block, break '@' (for example insert a zero-width space), clip to a few hundred characters, and cap issues per run with one summary issue for the overflow. Cap the state file size. Not checked: what role the service account has (it should be limited to Firestore only).
- second reviewer: Confirmed: inbox-to-issues.mjs:93 has no limit, feedback.yml:9 runs it 8 times a day, and lines 64-67 and 111-114 post user text unescaped. The quota arithmetic holds (about 6,250 entries), but it needs thousands of writes from a signed-in account and is the same flooding family as rules-deep-3. The @-mention impact is overstated: the target is a private repository (mjs:1-3), where mentions only notify people with access. The issue flood of up to 800 a day and the unbounded state file (mjs:103-108) are real nuisances for the owner. This is a template; the deployed copy in the private repo was not inspected.

### rules-deep-5 - A rules refusal on the feedback inbox is recorded as 'sent': if the inbox rule is not live, every support message is silently dropped
- severity: medium | verdict: confirmed | urgency: now
- where: `native-app/app/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/usecase/SendSupportMessage.kt:51-52`
- evidence: FirestoreFeedbackInbox.kt:30 turns PERMISSION_DENIED into FeedbackRejected. SendSupportMessage.kt:51-52 (flush) answers FeedbackRejected with outbox.sent(p.id), on the assumption (comment :21-22) that the first attempt already arrived. The same refusal is what the server returns when the /inbox block is simply not deployed: firestore.rules:54 still says this block was not published, and the tested copy at native-app/firestore/emulator-auth/firestore.rules has no inbox block at all. The first attempt (:60-71) swallows the refusal and reports the message as queued, then the background flush marks it sent.
- impact: The code cannot tell 'already delivered' from 'server refuses this collection'. If the live rules lack the inbox block, or later change shape (a new field, a third country, a longer version string), users see their message as sent and the owner never receives it; nothing is logged as a problem. A copy stays on the device only.
- fix: Confirm in the Firebase console that the live rules contain the /inbox block (ties to sec-firebase-9). In code: treat a refusal on the very first attempt as a real failure shown to the user and reported through the problem log; only treat a refusal as 'already delivered' when an earlier attempt for the same id timed out.
- second reviewer: FirestoreFeedbackInbox.kt:30 maps PERMISSION_DENIED to FeedbackRejected; SendSupportMessage.kt:67-70 swallows it on the first attempt and reports QUEUED, then flush at :51-52 removes the message from pending with no log or user signal. firestore.rules:54 says the /inbox block is not deployed, so today every support message would be dropped silently, with only the local copy kept (SupportOutboxPort.kt:21). This is a reliability and honesty defect rather than an attack; rules.test.mjs:143-185 does test the block's shape against the root file, so the risk is deployment state, not the rule text.

### rules-deep-6 - The device sign-in test suite runs against an old copy of the rules, so it does not prove the real file
- severity: low | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release
- where: `native-app/firestore/emulator-auth/firestore.rules:23`
- evidence: native-app/firestore/emulator-auth/firebase.json:2 loads the local file firestore.rules in that folder. A diff against the root firestore.rules shows the copy has no noLongDigits function (root :33-37, :23, :46-47), a looser category check (size <= 120 instead of the cat- pattern at root :49) and no /inbox block (root :54-71). The separate node suite native-app/firestore/rules-test/rules.test.mjs:13 does read the root file.
- impact: On-device tests for shared merchants and feedback pass or fail against rules that differ from what would be deployed; a breaking mismatch between the Kotlin writer and the real rules (for example a category id the pattern rejects) would only show up in production. It also leaves three rules files in the repo, two of them fully or partly open (emulator/test.rules:7-9 allows all reads and writes under users/ without sign-in), with only a comment preventing a wrong deploy.
- fix: Point emulator-auth/firebase.json at ../../../firestore.rules (as rules-test already does) and delete the copy. Give the open test rules a name that cannot be mistaken (for example OPEN-emulator-only.rules) and keep them out of any folder that has a .firebaserc for the real project.
- second reviewer: native-app/firestore/emulator-auth/firebase.json:2 loads the local copy, and that copy (emulator-auth/firestore.rules:22-44) lacks noLongDigits, uses categoryId size <= 120 instead of the cat- pattern, and has no /inbox block. The wrong-deploy worry is smaller than stated: emulator/test.rules:2-4 is clearly marked, only opens users/ for a demo project, and the only .firebaserc in the tree is at the repo root. Low is fair; this is test drift, not an exposure.

## device-lock

- not checked: Read-only code review, about 33 tool calls; nothing was built or run on a device, so every finding is from reading code (the hardware-keyboard part of device-lock-2 in particular is untested).

Checked and found fine (no finding): no app PIN exists, so nothing to hash or brute-force; lock state starts locked on every activity creation (LockGate.kt:17); notification tap only sets a flag and the lock overlay still covers it (MainActivity.kt:88-93); lock-screen notifications use fixed generic text plus an isLockSafe check and VISIBILITY_PRIVATE with a public version (AndroidDeviceNotifier.kt:123-131); no widgets, share targets, deep links or extra exported activities (only the launcher activity and the SMS receiver guarded by BROADCAST_SMS); no Dialog/Popup/ModalBottomSheet windows that could draw above the lock; TalkBack is cut off from content behind the lock (MasroufyApp.kt:101); below Android 12 the lock background is opaque (LockScreen.kt:71-74); no Log/println/printStackTrace in main code apart from a perf trace sink and the separate firebase-trial module; crash reports go through CrashSanitizer and are off by default in the manifest; the perf_uid intent extra is gated by an empty build constant in owner builds (AppContainer.kt:140-143).

Not checked: SmsInboxStore.kt lines 70-107 and 218+; the body of isLockSafe and CrashSanitizer/ProblemLogFormat (trusted from their doc comments); whether the PerfTrace Logcat sink (MasroufyApplication.kt:29) is limited to emulator builds; BankSmsReceiver and BackgroundWork internals; FileSupportOutbox and AndroidFeedCache contents; character/onboarding draft contents in SharedPreferences (may hold salary or balance figures typed during setup); Firestore offline cache settings (assumed default on-disk, unencrypted - only clearPersistence at sign-out was seen); the contents of analytics events (covered by sec-android-4); the 'hide numbers' feature; the saved-backup export via the system 'save to' window; iOS code (IosDeviceLock, IosLocalFiles); the firebase-trial module; whether the blur at 18dp on Android 12+ leaves large amounts legible (needs a screenshot on a real device).

### device-lock-1 - App-lock 5-minute grace is measured with the wall clock, so setting the phone time back skips the lock
- severity: low (finder said medium) | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release
- where: `native-app/androidApp/src/main/kotlin/app/masroufy/android/AppContainer.kt:68 and :88; native-app/core/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/core/Notifications.kt:101-108; native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/app/LockGate.kt:34-45`
- evidence: AppContainer.kt:68 defines nowMillis as System::currentTimeMillis and :88 passes it to AppLock. LockGate.onHidden (34-36) stores that value in memory at onStop; onVisible (38-45) calls shouldLock, which (Notifications.kt:104-108) locks only if now < hiddenAt or now - hiddenAt >= 5 minutes. Changing the date/time in Android Settings needs no credential. If the app was left at 10:00 and someone picks up the unlocked phone at 11:00, setting the clock to 10:01 makes now - hiddenAt = 1 minute, so the app opens with no fingerprint/PIN prompt (as long as the process is still alive, which is the normal case).
- impact: The exact threat the lock is for (someone holding the already-unlocked phone) can open balances and transactions without the fingerprint or phone PIN. The attacker gets one try per background period and must land in a 5-minute window, but the 'last used' time is easy to learn (they watched the owner, or the recents order / Digital Wellbeing).
- fix: Use a monotonic clock for the lock only: pass SystemClock.elapsedRealtime() (it keeps counting in sleep and cannot be changed by the user) into AppLock instead of the shared nowMillis. Keep the existing 'now < hiddenAt => lock' guard. Add a test in the LockGate/AppLock tests for 'clock moved back to inside the window'.
- second reviewer: Code is exactly as described: AppContainer.kt:68 (System::currentTimeMillis) is passed to AppLock at :88, LockGate.kt:34-45 stores and compares it, and Notifications.kt:104-108 only guards now < hiddenAt; no monotonic clock exists anywhere (no elapsedRealtime in the repo's lock path). Severity lowered from medium: the attacker gets a single attempt per background period (LockGate.kt:44 clears hiddenAt, a miss locks), must hit a 5-minute window he has to know, and needs the process still alive; still worth the one-line fix before release.

### device-lock-2 - Lock screen blocks touches only: Back key (and likely a hardware keyboard) still drives the app behind it
- severity: low | verdict: confirmed | urgency: before_release
- where: `native-app/androidApp/src/main/kotlin/app/masroufy/android/MainActivity.kt:40-49; native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/screens/auth/LockScreen.kt:70; native-app/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/masroufy/ui/app/MasroufyApp.kt:99-109`
- evidence: MasroufyApp.kt:101-109 keeps the whole AppShell composed under the lock and only clears its semantics; LockScreen.kt:70 covers it with a Box whose only guard is pointerInput { detectTapGestures {} }. MainActivity.kt:43-44 handles Back with shell.handleBack() with no check of lock.locked, so Back while locked runs the top overlay's onBack / pops screens (ui/overlay/Overlay.kt:54-57, AppShell.kt:78). Nothing in LockScreen clears or traps keyboard focus, so a text field or button that had focus before locking should still receive keys from a Bluetooth/USB keyboard (Tab/Enter) - this keyboard part is read from the code, not tested on a device.
- impact: A person without the fingerprint cannot read data (content is blurred, or opaque below Android 12, and hidden from TalkBack), but can change state blindly: close an unsaved sheet, navigate back, and possibly type into or activate a focused control behind the lock.
